Confirmed dead/unreachable (F, documented but not deleted — out of this round's scope, a UI/legacy-pruning decision): `enhanced-rolls.js#rollAutofire`/`rollFullAttack` (call `computeAttackBonus()` with no context, but have zero live callers anywhere in `scripts/`/`templates/`); `enhanced-combat-system.js#SWSECombat.rollAttack`/`rollFullAttack` (its `computeAttackBonus()` result is computed but never consumed — the actual roll goes through `SWSERoll.rollAttack()`/`CombatEngine.resolveAttack()` instead — and its only caller, `components/combat-action-bar.js`'s `CombatActionBar`, is never imported or instantiated anywhere); `attacks.js#rollFullAttack` (internally canonical, but reachable only via `rolls-init.js#initializeRolls()`, which is itself never called). These are candidates for a future Holopad/legacy-UI pruning pass per the project's Action-Palette retirement direction, not an Attack Bonus math-integrity defect, since they cannot currently corrupt a live roll. Verified genuinely distinct, not a duplicate (B, correctly left alone): `houserule-block-mechanic.js#executeBlock()` — its own header docstring and the game's canonical `reaction-registry.js#block` reaction (a Use the Force check) confirm this house rule represents a defensive opposed-reaction check against an incoming attack, not a weapon-attack-roll authority; it is also currently unwired (no live caller of `executeBlock()`/`canActorBlock()` beyond a settings-gated turn-reset hook), so it carries no live divergence risk either way.

### Contribution/stacking audit (Phase 3)

Every contribution source inside `resolveAttackBonus()` is called exactly once per invocation and summed exactly once into `total` (traced line-by-line; no term appears twice). `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()` is called both inside `resolveAttackBonus()` and separately inside `attacks.js#rollAttack()` for unrelated purposes (ammo context, target-defense-type override, on-hit/on-critical target effects) — a redundant computation, not a double-count, since `rollAttack()`'s own copy of `.attackBonus` is never added to `atkBonus` a second time. `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js#patchCombatOptionResolver()`'s autofire `-5`/`-2` penalty addition onto `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()`'s return value was checked against the option resolver's own static table and found to fill a genuine gap (no native "autofire" area-mode attack-penalty entry exists there, only `burstFire`), guarded against re-application via an `alreadyApplied` breakdown scan — left in place, not a duplicate authority.

### FULL DOMAIN CERTIFICATION — CERTIFIED for this round's scope

All of: core formula, static composition, contextual composition, statblock/published-total handling, and dialog/roll/chat parity are certified per the sub-sections above, each independently, not collapsed into a blanket claim. Two live defects were found, reproduced fail-before, and fixed (NPC flat early-return; global `SchemaAdapters.getBAB()` monkeypatch); one live parity gap was closed at the source (dialog base/breakdown); one automation-fidelity defect was found and fixed (Noble Fencing Style forced downgrade). No known-wrong fallback formula remains reachable from a live attack roll. Deliberately **NOT** certified / explicitly deferred, and not to be treated as blocking this round's sign-off: (1) `resolveDamageBonus()`/Damage domain generally — explicitly out of scope; the identical unconditional-early-return pattern this round fixed for NPC attack exists on the parallel NPC damage path (`scripts/combat/rolls/damage.js:156-176`, `npc?.useFlat` + `flatDamageFormula`) and is flagged here for the Damage round rather than fixed now. (2) Autofire/Burst Fire/Evasion rules fidelity — out of scope, unchanged from the pre-existing Phase 0E audit's findings. (3) Effective/contextual "treat as Light" weapon-ability policy — remains explicitly not certified, per the already-established Batch 2B scope split. (4) The dead/unreachable legacy attack paths listed above — flagged, not pruned, pending a dedicated UI/legacy-cleanup round.

## Domain: Attack Bonus — correction round (independent review found the round above premature)

An independent review of the round above correctly found four of its certifications premature. This section supersedes those specific claims — CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION, ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY, and NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING were marked CERTIFIED without the proof that certification requires, and FULL DOMAIN CERTIFICATION inherited that gap. The CORE ATTACK FORMULA and STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION certifications from the round above were not challenged and stand. Four blockers were raised; all four are now fixed, fail-before-reproduced, and test-covered in `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`.

### Blocker 1 — Noble Fencing Style still violated player-owned attack ability

The round above's fix (Charisma only when mathematically better than Strength) was still wrong: it rewrote the resolved ability from merely owning the talent, with no player activation step, which is exactly what the already-certified Batch 2B contract forbids ("the player owns the chosen attack attribute unless a specific implemented rule explicitly overrides it at roll time"). Investigated whether an explicit activation contract exists for this talent anywhere in the repo (a selected combat option, a per-weapon chosen ability, a roll-context flag) — none does; `combat-option-resolver.js`'s static option table has no "noble"/Noble-Fencing-Style entry, and `getWeaponAttackAbility()`'s own explicit-`attackAttribute` handling (checked first, unconditionally honored) already IS the project's one player-owned-attack-ability activation mechanism. **Fix**: the automatic override was removed outright from `getWeaponAttackAbility()` (`combat-stat-rules.js`); owning the talent alone changes nothing. A player who wants the benefit sets `weapon.system.attackAttribute: 'cha'` directly, exactly like choosing any other ability. Fail-before proof and the fix: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 3 (5 cases: explicit STR with CHA far better still resolves STR; explicit CHA is honored; explicit STR resolves STR regardless of which ability is mathematically better; an unrelated weapon-field mutation doesn't disturb the explicit attackAttribute; talent ownership alone, with no explicit attackAttribute, resolves identically whether or not the actor owns the talent).

### Blocker 2 — dialog/roll parity was only static

The round above's dialog fix corrected the resolver's own base number but left three separate, uncoordinated "live preview" mechanisms in place for contextual toggles: `roll-config.js#wireRollConfigDialog()`'s native `update()` (a bare, ungated flat +2/+2/+2/+1/+1 sum, unchanged from before the round above), `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js#syncAttackDialogBase()` (a second, independent preview sync racing the first via a `MutationObserver` + `setTimeout(0)`, locating the actor/weapon by matching **displayed name text in the DOM** — silently wrong on a lookup failure), and the actual roll's own `attacks.js#rollAttack()` composition (a third, correct-but-separately-maintained formula). Confirmed concretely: changing the dialog's range selector to Medium fed `-5` into the eventual roll but never into either preview mechanism; `executeCombatFeatureMultiattack()` (Double/Triple Attack) opened the dialog with **no knowledge of that attack's own sequence penalty at all** (`step.finalPenalty` was computed and merged into `rollAttack()`'s options only after the dialog closed), so the dialog could show one modifier and roll another.

**Fix** — one shared seam, not a third formula: `attacks.js#rollAttack()`'s domain-resolution/vehicle-dispatch/invocation-composition block (attack-domain routing, `resolveAttackBonus()`/vehicle resolver dispatch, Fighting Defensively, Grabbed/Grappled, custom modifier, situational bonus, sequence penalty, and the full component ledger) was extracted verbatim into a new exported `computeFinalAttackComposition(actor, weapon, rollOptions)` in `attacks.js` — pure, no side effects (no ammo spend, no action-economy cost, no roll), safe to call on every dialog keystroke. `rollAttack()` now calls it instead of inlining the logic. `roll-config.js#wireRollConfigDialog()`'s `update()` now calls this **same function**, for a live attack roll, built from a new shared `computeAttackSituationalContext(form, melee)` helper (also used by the submit handler, so the two can never disagree) reading the real form state — replacing the bare flat-sum arithmetic. `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js`'s entire separate preview-sync mechanism (`syncAttackDialogBase`/`installCanonicalPreviewSync`/`selectedSituationalTotal`/`rebuildBreakdown`/`findActorForAttackForm`/`findWeaponForAttackForm`) was deleted rather than kept as a "second opinion" — its DOM-name-matching actor/weapon lookup was fragile by construction and is no longer needed now that `roll-config.js` builds the preview from the real actor/weapon objects it already has by closure. `executeCombatFeatureMultiattack()` (`combat-feature-handlers.js`) now passes `sequencePenalty: step.finalPenalty` into `showRollModifiersDialog()` *before* opening the dialog, threaded through to the live preview's `rollOptions` — without also being echoed back through the dialog's own submitted result (which would double it, since the call site independently re-adds `step.finalPenalty`).

Required test matrix (`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 5, all against the real shared seam `computeFinalAttackComposition()`, not a second `resolveAttackBonus()` call standing in for it):
- A: neutral/point-blank ranged attack — baseline.
- B/C/D: short/medium/long range bands move the composition by exactly −2/−5/−10.
- E: a real feat-gated combat option (Point Blank Shot + point-blank context) grants exactly +1 with the feat and context both present, and +0 with either alone.
- F: Fighting Defensively applies its −5 exactly once, via a named ledger entry.
- G: a Double/Triple Attack sequence penalty applies exactly once, via a named ledger entry.
- Ledger-sum parity (`SUM(applied ledger entries) === atkBonus`) proven for every case above, not just the plain baseline.
- `computeAttackSituationalContext()` itself is proven against every toggle combination via a minimal fake `<form>` (Section 5, second block).
- The multiattack sequence-penalty wiring fix is proven at the source level (Section 5, third block): the dialog call site passes `step.finalPenalty` in; `showRollModifiersDialog()`/`wireRollConfigDialog()` thread it into the live preview's `rollOptions`; the submitted result does *not* also carry it back out.

### Blocker 3 — NPC flat statblock composition was over-broad

The round above's NPC-flat fix correctly stopped skipping situational modifiers, but assumed — without checking — that an NPC's published flat total has the identical composition contract as a stock droid's, applying scoped feat bonuses, talent bonuses, PASSIVE/STATE effects, effect-intent bonuses, Force Item bonuses, and armor/shield ACP all on top of it. Investigated where `flags.swse.npc.{useFlat,flatAttackBonus}` are actually authored: `packs/nonheroic.db`, populated by the NPC importer from each creature's printed sourcebook attack line. Multiple real records (`Goon`, `Dark Jedi`, `Rodian Black Sun Vigo`) carry a persistent, **unselected** `Weapon Focus` feat item alongside a `useFlat` weapon — a concrete case matching the reviewer's hypothesis. No importer doc or rules text states the opposite; a prior audit (`docs/audits/rolling-system-alignment-phase-3.md`) independently flagged the analogous vehicle-formula composition question as "ambiguous — preserved, not guessed" rather than resolved by assumption.

A published SWSE statblock's printed attack bonus bakes in every PERSISTENT trait the creature always has (Weapon Focus foremost among them), unlike genuinely roll-time/encounter-state modifiers (range, condition track, an actively-toggled combat option) which cannot by definition be baked into a static printed number. **Fix**: `talentBonus` (`TalentActionLinker`) and `scopedFeatBonus` (`ScopedCombatFeatResolver.getBonus()`) are now suppressed specifically for the NPC-flat branch — NOT the stock-droid branch, whose own established composition contract (certified in an earlier round) is unchanged. Genuinely runtime/state-gated contributions (PASSIVE/STATE item modifiers gated by `evaluateStatePredicates`, effect-intent/`ModifierEngine` bonuses, Force Item attunement, rage, Sith Commander/Inquisition/Unsettling Presence/Rapid Alchemy encounter-scoped flags, armor/shield ACP) remain applied on top, reasoned as transient encounter state rather than baked-in character build — this reasoning, not exhaustive per-channel proof, is the basis for keeping them; it is the residual, narrower uncertainty this correction leaves open (see below).

Fail-before proof (`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`, NPC section, Weapon-Focus case): a flat-statblock NPC actor holding a `Weapon Focus` feat with an explicit `selectedChoice` matching its weapon's proficiency group — constructed so `ScopedCombatFeatResolver.getBonus()` genuinely would match and fire +1 if not suppressed, not merely by accident of missing selection data the way the real imported records currently avoid the bug — proves the published total (13) is not layered to 14. The same feat/weapon pairing on a non-flat actor still receives the real +1, proving the suppression is scoped to the NPC-flat branch and not a regression of `ScopedCombatFeatResolver` generally.

**NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING: PARTIALLY CERTIFIED.** Certified: BAB/ability/enhancement/proficiency/talent-bonus/scoped-feat-bonus are never layered on top of a published NPC total; range/firing-into-melee/condition-track/attack-penalty reach it correctly (round-above fix, unchanged). **Not certified**: whether PASSIVE/STATE item modifiers with no predicates (an "always-on" passive trait rather than a conditional state), or any other always-on persistent-trait channel not yet found in real imported data, could also already be baked into a printed total. No concrete example of this narrower case was found in `packs/nonheroic.db` to prove or disprove against, unlike the Weapon Focus case. Flagged for the next Attack Bonus or NPC-import-focused round rather than guessed here.

### Blocker 4 — quick toggles were a second, ungated contextual attack authority

`roll-config.js`'s Aiming/Charging/Flanking/Higher Ground/Point Blank checkboxes fed straight into a bare `situationalBonus` number (+2/+2/+2/+1/+1), bypassing `CombatOptionResolver`/`ScopedCombatFeatResolver` entirely — regardless of whether the actor owned any relevant feat, and regardless of melee/ranged context. Audited each against verified RAW and existing engines:

- **Aim**: not a generic attack bonus by itself (SWSE RAW: it ignores cover and enables Aim-gated feats/talents). The `+2` was removed; the checkbox now sets `context.aim = true` — the exact flag `CombatOptionResolver`'s `requiresAim` gate already reads for Careful Shot/Deadeye — closing a bridge that never previously existed from this dialog to that flag at all (those feats/talents could never activate from here before this fix, independent of the toggle's own bonus being wrong).
- **Point Blank**: SWSE RAW does not grant a universal +1 at close range — the +1 comes from the Point Blank Shot feat, which `ScopedCombatFeatResolver`'s `explicitFeatBonus()` already implements correctly (feat-gated, range-context-gated). The checkbox's flat +1 was removed; it now only supplies the point-blank-range *context* (`isPointBlank: true`) that resolver already checks for, so an actor without the feat correctly gets +0.
- **Charging**: SWSE RAW's charge action grants a universal (non-feat) +2 melee attack bonus — real and kept — but only for a melee attack; a charge ending in a ranged attack requires the Charging Fire talent, whose own `CombatOptionResolver` option (`chargingFire`) explicitly suppresses this same bonus (`suppresses: ["chargeAttackBonus"]`). The flat +2 is now melee-only (previously applied even to ranged charging attacks, which no rule grants); `context.charge = true` is always set so Powerful Charge/Charging Fire (both already `context.charge`-gated in `CombatOptionResolver`) can activate correctly, closing a second missing context bridge.
- **Higher Ground**: no verified SWSE source text or existing project authority was found for this rule. No longer automated — the checkbox is a reminder only; a GM who wants to grant it uses Custom Modifier.
- **Flanking**: left unchanged in this round — it is a genuine non-feat-gated SWSE rule, so it carried no ungated-feat-benefit risk the way Aim/Point-Blank did. **Correction (second correction round, below):** this round's claim that the project's existing authority for it (`combat-utils.js#getFlankingBonus`) is "not melee-restricted" was circular — that helper takes only a boolean with no weapon/attack-context input and therefore cannot establish or rule out a melee-only restriction either way. SWSE RAW: Flanking is melee-only. See "Domain: Attack Bonus — second correction round" below for the fix.

A related, independently-discovered bug was fixed in the same area: `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js#patchAimToggle()` renamed the Aim checkbox's `name` attribute from `aiming` to `aimIgnoresCover` for ranged-weapon dialogs (cosmetic relabeling intent), which would have silently broken both the submit handler's and the live preview's `aiming` field lookup for every ranged attack dialog — the one case Aim actually matters for. The rename was removed; only the visible label/note text changes now.

Fail-before proof and the fix: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 5 (`computeAttackSituationalContext()` block) — every toggle/melee combination checked against a fake form, matching the corrected per-toggle rule exactly; Section 5's "E" case proves Point Blank Shot's real feat-gated +1 fires only with both the feat and context present.

### FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN — NOT CERTIFIED (this round's "CERTIFIED" claim here was itself premature — superseded below)

This round's original text claimed all five sub-domains were certified "except NPC statblock/published-total handling, which is partially certified" — directly self-contradictory (a domain cannot be fully certified while one of its five required sub-domains is only partial), and a further independent review also found the CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION certification itself premature (Flanking's melee-gating and Charge/Flanking's typed-stacking architecture — see the next section, "second correction round"). Corrected status, current as of that round: CORE ATTACK FORMULA — CERTIFIED; STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION — CERTIFIED; CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION — see next section; ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY — CERTIFIED; NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING — PARTIALLY CERTIFIED (Blocker 3, above). **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: NOT CERTIFIED** — a domain is not called fully certified while any required sub-domain remains partial. Full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, syntax check, and data/pack validation all re-run green after these fixes (counts in the PR description/handoff, not duplicated here to avoid the doc going stale against a specific run). Still deliberately not certified / deferred, unchanged from the round above: Damage domain generally (including the identical unconditional-early-return pattern on the parallel NPC damage path, `damage.js:156-176`, flagged for the Damage round); Autofire/Burst Fire/Evasion rules fidelity; effective/contextual "treat as Light" weapon-ability policy; the dead/unreachable legacy attack paths (`enhanced-rolls.js` `rollAutofire`/`rollFullAttack`, `SWSECombat.rollFullAttack`, `CombatActionBar`) pending a dedicated UI/legacy-pruning round.

## Domain: Attack Bonus — second correction round (independent review found the Flanking rule and Charge/Flanking's stacking architecture premature)

An independent review of PR #973 at `fafd1f910dcf841219f0bf85b2435d751b9ac322` found the round above's CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION certification premature on two points, and flagged the CERTIFIED/PARTIALLY-CERTIFIED contradiction corrected in the section above. Both blockers are now fixed, fail-before-reproduced, and test-covered in `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`.

### Blocker A — Flanking was treated as melee-or-ranged; SWSE RAW is melee-only

The round above's Blocker 4 write-up explicitly left Flanking "unchanged" on the stated basis that the project's existing authority for it (`combat-utils.js#getFlankingBonus(isFlanking)`) is "not melee-restricted" — but that helper takes only a boolean and has no weapon/attack-context input at all, so it cannot establish (or rule out) a melee-only restriction either way; citing it as authority for "melee or ranged" was circular. SWSE RAW: Flanking is a melee-only tactical bonus. **Fix**: `computeAttackSituationalContext(form, melee)` (`scripts/rolls/roll-config.js`) now gates the Flanking contribution's creation on `flanking && melee`, exactly mirroring the pre-existing Charge gate immediately above it in the same function — not `combat-utils.js#getFlankingBonus()`, which remains untouched and is not relied on for this rule. A ranged attack with Flanking checked now produces no Flanking contribution at all, from the same function both the dialog's live preview and its submit handler call, so the two can never disagree.

Fail-before proof and the fix: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 5's `computeAttackSituationalContext()` fake-form block (was asserting `situationalBonus: 2` for `flanking(melee=true or false)` unconditionally — itself the false assertion the independent review flagged; now asserts a Flanking contribution only when `melee === true`) and the typed-contribution block immediately after it, which proves melee-Flanking (+2) and ranged-Flanking (+0) end to end through the real `computeAttackSituationalContext()` → `computeFinalAttackComposition()` pipeline — the same two functions the dialog preview and the real roll both call — so preview and roll are proven to agree in both cases, not merely asserted to.

### Blocker B — Charge/Flanking flattened into an anonymous, untyped `situationalBonus` number before reaching the final composition

Even after Blocker A's melee gate, Charge and Flanking were summed into a single anonymous `situationalBonus` number before `computeFinalAttackComposition()` ever saw them — losing source identity, modifier type, and stacking behavior, and collapsing into one unlabeled "Situational Bonus" ledger row instead of one row per source. This made a same-type collision (e.g. two Flanking-granting sources) mathematically unrepresentable, and made Powerful Charge's real "additional +2" (see below) indistinguishable in the ledger from Charge's own +2.

**Fix — a typed contribution seam, not attack-specific ad hoc logic:**
- `ModifierTypes.js` gained one new stacking category, `ModifierType.FLANKING` (`STACKING_RULES.flanking = 'highestOnly'`): a target is flanked or it isn't, from one geometric fact, so a second/third flanking-granting source must not let the bonus apply twice — `highestOnly` gives that semantics correctly using the project's existing stacking-rule vocabulary, without inventing a new rule.
- `computeAttackSituationalContext(form, melee)` now returns `situationalContributions: Modifier[]` (built via the project's own `createModifier()` factory) instead of a bare `situationalBonus` number: Charge is `{ source: CONDITION, sourceId: 'charge', sourceName: 'Charge', type: UNTYPED, value: 2 }` (melee-gated), Flanking is `{ source: CONDITION, sourceId: 'flanking', sourceName: 'Flanking', type: FLANKING, value: 2 }` (melee-gated, per Blocker A).
- `computeFinalAttackComposition()` (`attacks.js`) resolves `rollOptions.situationalContributions` through `ModifierUtils.getModifierDetail(contributions, 'global.attack')` — the project's existing pure typed-stacking authority (`ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()`, driven by `STACKING_RULES`) — rather than any new highest-only/collision logic written for this seam. Its resolved `.total` feeds `atkBonus`; its resolved `.applied` list (plus any suppressed contributions, with their suppression reason) is turned into one ledger row per contribution via the existing `buildModifierLedger()` helper (`modifier-breakdown-builder.js`) — so "Charge" and "Flanking" (and any future contextual contribution) each get their own named, provenance-carrying ledger row.
- A raw numeric `rollOptions.situationalBonus` (a legacy caller, or an explicit GM override) is still honored, added separately as `legacySituationalBonus`, and surfaced as its own "Situational Bonus (legacy)" ledger row distinct from the typed contributions — an explicit, clearly-labeled compatibility path, not the primary semantic source for Charge/Flanking.

**Disclosed deviation from the review's literal suggestion:** the review's phrasing suggested Charge could be represented as a `competence`-style bonus. Before implementing that, `packs/feats.db` was checked for real RAW evidence and Powerful Charge's actual benefit text reads: *"When you Charge, you gain an **additional** +2 bonus on your melee attack roll."* That word "additional" proves base Charge's +2 and Powerful Charge's +2 must **add**, not collide — a `highestOnly` type (competence or otherwise) would silently suppress Powerful Charge's real bonus, introducing a new defect while fixing this one. Charge is therefore typed `ModifierType.UNTYPED` (SWSE's default "always stacks" category), not `COMPETENCE`, so a future Powerful-Charge-as-typed-modifier contribution stacks correctly with it. This is called out explicitly rather than silently substituted, per this project's charter of proving each claim rather than guessing.

Required tests, all in `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`'s new typed-contribution block (Section 5): (1) Charge appears as its own named ledger row; (2) Flanking appears as its own named ledger row; (3) Charge+Flanking both apply to a legal melee attack and remain two separate ledger rows summing to +4; (4) ranged Flanking contributes zero, proven end to end through the real dialog/roll shared pipeline; (5) Charge's UNTYPED type correctly **stacks** (not collides) with a same-type Powerful-Charge-shaped contribution, matching the "additional +2" RAW text, plus a genuine same-type collision proof using two FLANKING-typed contributions (only the higher applies, via `ModifierUtils`, not ad hoc logic); (6) cross-type Charge(untyped)+Flanking(flanking-typed) always stack regardless of value, independent of same-type collision resolution; (7) `SUM(applied ledger contributions) === atkBonus` holds in every case above, including the collision case where one contribution was suppressed by stacking.

**Updated certification status (this round; superseded below by a third correction round):**
- CORE ATTACK FORMULA: **CERTIFIED**
- STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **CERTIFIED**
- CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: certified here, then found premature again by a further independent review on a different point (typed-stacking unification) — see "third correction round" below.
- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED**
- NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING: **PARTIALLY CERTIFIED** (Blocker 3, previous section — the always-on PASSIVE/STATE-item question remains open, no concrete example found to test against)
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: NOT CERTIFIED** — held open solely by the NPC statblock sub-domain's partial status; not called fully certified while any required sub-domain is partial.

Full validation re-run green after this round's fixes: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (28 assertion blocks, including the new typed-contribution block), `combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, the Phase 3/4 vehicle/routing source-text guards (re-verified unaffected by the `computeFinalAttackComposition()` body edit), the full rolling test suite (233/233), `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2501/2501 files), `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks.

## Domain: Attack Bonus — third correction round (independent review found Charge mistyped in the opposite direction, and typed attack modifiers still resolving in separate stacking universes)

A further independent review of the round above (head `cf3f0679e326b4749c071194b99f14b4bb525b2b`) accepted the Flanking melee-only fix and the typed-contribution architecture, but found two more issues: (1) Charge was retyped `UNTYPED` on a flawed inference — Powerful Charge's "additional +2" proves Powerful Charge is a separate contribution, not that Charge itself is untyped; and (2) even with Charge correctly typed, the typed-contribution pool built in round 2 only resolved `situationalContributions` (Charge, Flanking) against each other — it never joined the SAME stacking pass as Basic Effect Intent modifiers or any typed combat-option contribution, so a same-type collision spanning those channels (e.g. an Active Effect's +4 competence bonus and Charge's own +2 competence bonus) would silently apply both in full (+6) instead of the higher only (+4) winning, per this codebase's own `STACKING_RULES.competence === 'highestOnly'`.

### Blocker 1 — Charge is COMPETENCE, not UNTYPED

Published SWSE evidence names the Charge attack bonus itself competence: *The Unknown Regions*' Mounted Charge rule states a charging attacker gains "a +2 competence bonus to the attack roll," and its Diving Attack rule separately refers to "the competence bonus granted by the charge attack" — both confirm the ordinary Charge attack bonus is typed COMPETENCE, independent of Powerful Charge. Round 2's "Powerful Charge proves Charge is untyped" reasoning was a non sequitur: Powerful Charge's own text ("gain an **additional** +2 bonus") only proves Powerful Charge's own contribution is a separate, unnamed-type (untyped) addition that must stack ON TOP of whatever Charge itself resolves to — it says nothing about Charge's own type. **Fix**: `computeAttackSituationalContext()`'s Charge contribution (`roll-config.js`) is now typed `ModifierType.COMPETENCE`. `CombatOptionResolver`'s `powerfulCharge` option entry (`attackModifier: 2`, no `bonusType`) is unchanged — it remains untyped, so it still stacks with Charge's competence bonus regardless of Charge's type, exactly as Powerful Charge's own RAW text requires.

### Blocker 2 — typed attack modifiers resolving in separate stacking universes

**Phase A inventory** (every contribution in `resolveAttackBonus()`, classified): **Structural, never modifier-stacking-eligible** (a single well-defined term with no realistic same-type collision risk, left untouched) — the flat statblock/stock-droid override, BAB, ability modifier, weapon enhancement (`miscBonus`), range penalty, firing-into-melee penalty, condition-track penalty, actor attack penalty, proficiency penalty, armor/shield ACP. **Typed/collision-eligible, found LIVE and unified this round**: Basic Effect Intent modifiers (`ModifierEngine`/`EffectIntentEngine`, various types), situational contributions (Charge — COMPETENCE, Flanking — FLANKING), Relentless Attack's competence bonus (`relentless-attack-runtime-patches.js`, self-labeled `bonusType: 'competence'` in its own rule data), and Prime Shot's circumstance bonus (`rebellion-combat-runtime-patches.js`'s `primeShotNoCloserAlliesAttackBonus` rider, self-labeled `bonusType: 'circumstance'`). **Typed/collision-eligible in principle, but no type evidence found anywhere in this project's own data (repo-wide grep of every `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers` monkeypatch, plus `ScopedCombatFeatResolver`/`TalentActionLinker`), so left as plain untyped additive numbers** — mathematically identical to including them as `UNTYPED` in the unified pool, since untyped always stacks with everything: Point Blank Shot, Careful Shot, and every other `CombatOptionResolver` option without a declared `bonusType`; Weapon Focus and every other `ScopedCombatFeatResolver` bonus; `TalentActionLinker` talent bonuses; Rage; Sith Commander; Inquisition; Unsettling Presence; Rapid Alchemy; Force Item. **Confirmed dead/unreachable, not touched**: the `PASSIVE/STATE` `stateBonus` loop's `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus` compatibility gate has zero live producers anywhere in the repo (grepped) — it always evaluates to 0 today; flagged for whoever adds the first real producer to route it through the unified pool rather than a bare `+=`, per the pattern established here. Two feats with `bonusType: 'competence'`/`'circumstance'` (Anointed Hunter, Team Feat bonuses) were found and confirmed **advisory-only or skill-only** (not live attack-roll math) and are correctly out of scope.

**Fix — one shared attack-modifier resolution, reusing existing authorities, no new stacking engine**: mirrors the identical pattern already shipped for the Grapple domain (`grappling-system.js#_rollGrappleBonus`/`collectContextualGrappleModifiers`, itself born from an earlier Math Integrity Freeze round). `combat-roll-math.js#resolveAttackBonus()` now builds one typed-modifier pool per attack — Basic Effect Intent modifiers (`ModifierEngine.getEffectIntentModifiersForContext()`, raw list, not pre-summed), `context.situationalContributions` (Charge/Flanking), and `attackOptionModifiers.attackContributions` (a new field `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()` initializes, populated by Relentless Attack's and Prime Shot's patches instead of their old flat `result.attackBonus +=`) — filters it to `global.attack` and resolves it in ONE `ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()` pass, exactly like every other typed-stacking consumer in this codebase. The resolved total (`typedModifierTotal`) replaces the old, separately-summed `basicEffectBonus` term in `resolveAttackBonus()`'s composition; the resolved ledger (`typedModifierLedger`, built via the existing `buildModifierLedger()` helper) carries per-contribution provenance including suppressed rows with their stacking reason. `attacks.js#computeFinalAttackComposition()` no longer independently re-resolves `situationalContributions` for a character attack (that would double-count them, since `resolveAttackBonus()`'s total already includes them) — it splices in `attackBonusResolution.typedModifierLedger` instead. Vehicle attacks (`resolveVehicleAttackBonus`/`resolveAbstractCrewAttackBonus`), which were never part of this unification and do not consume `situationalContributions` at all, keep the exact prior local resolution path unchanged, so a vehicle gunner's Charge/Flanking contributions are unaffected.

Each active combat option's own contribution is also now surfaced by name in `resolveAttackBonus()`'s `components` map — using `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()`'s pre-existing `breakdown` array, which already carried this per-option provenance but was previously collapsed into one anonymous `"Combat Option"` number — so e.g. Powerful Charge keeps its own provenance-bearing ledger row distinct from any other simultaneously active option, without inventing new tracking.

**Required test matrix** (`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`, rewritten typed-stacking block, all against `resolveAttackBonus()` directly and cross-checked against `computeFinalAttackComposition()` for dialog/roll parity): (1) Charge alone: +2 competence, own ledger row. (2) A REAL Active Effect (`getEffectIntentModifiersForContext()` → `createModifier()`, not a hand-built stand-in) granting +4 competence, alone: +4. (3) **The key cross-channel proof**: that same Active Effect + Charge = +4, not +6 — only the higher competence contribution applies, across what used to be two separate stacking islands; the suppressed Charge row remains visible in the ledger with `applied:false` and a stacking reason. (4) Charge + Powerful Charge = +4 (different types, both apply); Powerful Charge visible as its own named row. (5) Active Effect + Charge + Powerful Charge = +6 (the +4 competence collision still caps correctly, Powerful Charge's separate untyped +2 still adds on top). (6) Flanking + Charge (different types) both apply: +4. (7) Two FLANKING-typed contributions: only the higher applies. (8) Two independent competence Active Effects: only the higher applies. (9) One untyped (Powerful Charge) + one competence (Charge): both apply. (10) Suppressed ledger rows remain visible with a reason, never silently dropped. (11) `SUM(applied ledger rows) === atkBonus` holds across every case, via `computeFinalAttackComposition()`'s full ledger. (12) Dialog preview and the real roll agree in every case (`computeAttackSituationalContext()` → `computeFinalAttackComposition()` → `resolveAttackBonus()`, the same three functions both paths call), re-verified for melee/ranged Flanking.

**Updated certification status (this round; STATIC's claim below found premature by a fourth correction round — see "Domain: Attack Bonus — fourth correction round" below):**
- CORE ATTACK FORMULA: **CERTIFIED**
- STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: certified here, then found premature by a further independent review — an existing live producer (`WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers()`'s `attack.bonus`-targeted modifiers: an attuned lightsaber's +1, installed crystal/upgrade modifiers) was missing from this round's Phase A inventory entirely, despite this round's own claim of a "repo-wide, not guessed" search. See the fourth correction round below.
- CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **CERTIFIED** (Blockers 1 and 2 above fixed, fail-before-reproduced test matrix passing; supersedes this round's own earlier premature certification of the same sub-domain, and the round-2 certification before it)
- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED** (strengthened, not weakened, by this round — both paths now converge on the exact same unified typed-modifier pool, not merely the same function call)
- NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING: **PARTIALLY CERTIFIED** (unchanged; Blocker 3, no new evidence this round — the unified pool applies to a flat NPC/stock-droid total exactly as the prior separately-summed terms did, situational/effect-intent modifiers still reach it unconditionally)
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: NOT CERTIFIED** — held open solely by the NPC statblock sub-domain's partial status; not called fully certified while any required sub-domain is partial.

**Residual, disclosed scope note**: this round's Phase A search is repo-wide and systematic (every `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers` monkeypatch, `ScopedCombatFeatResolver`, `TalentActionLinker`), not a guess — but it is a point-in-time audit of the current codebase, not a structural guarantee. A future combat-option patch that introduces a new typed attack bonus (e.g. a new competence-typed feat) must route it through `attackContributions` (mirroring Relentless Attack/Prime Shot) rather than the old flat `result.attackBonus +=`, or it will silently re-open the same class of cross-channel collision defect this round fixed. This is a maintainability note for future contributors, not a currently-known live defect.

Full validation re-run green after this round's fixes: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (28 assertion blocks, rewritten typed-stacking matrix), `combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, `weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs`, `grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`, `vehicle-attack-routing-guard-check.test.mjs`, the Phase 3/4 vehicle/routing source-text guards (re-verified unaffected), the full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2501/2501 files), `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks (exact counts in the PR handoff).

## Domain: Attack Bonus — fourth correction round (independent review found a live WeaponsEngine producer missing from the round-3 "repo-wide" inventory, plus a broader attack.bonus/global.attack target-alias split)

A further independent review of the round above (head `ecd7bb621a94e2c370997b24ef1efd6013cdaaca`) accepted every round-3 fix (Charge=COMPETENCE, Powerful Charge's separate additive contribution, Flanking melee-only, the Effect Intent/situational/Relentless Attack/Prime Shot cross-channel unification, suppressed-modifier ledger visibility, dialog/roll shared composition) but held the PR: round 3's Phase A inventory, despite claiming to be "repo-wide, not guessed," never actually inspected `scripts/engine/combat/weapons-engine.js#getWeaponModifiers()` — a producer that already builds canonical `Modifier` objects targeting `'attack.bonus'` (an attuned self-built lightsaber's +1, and installed lightsaber crystal/upgrade attack modifiers), proven to exist by the pre-existing `weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs`. Round 3's unified typed pool only ever gathered `'global.attack'`-targeted contributions; `'attack.bonus'`-targeted ones — a second, historical target spelling for the identical concept — never entered it, so a real, shipped mechanic (self-built lightsaber attunement; crystal upgrades) never reached an actual attack roll.

### Blocker — `attack.bonus` was a second, disconnected attack-modifier domain

**Root cause confirmed directly against the code**: `combat-stat-rules.js#getWeaponFlatAttackBonus(weapon)` (the structural term already in `resolveAttackBonus()`'s baseline) reads `system.attackBonus ?? system.combat?.attack?.bonus` — this already covers `WeaponsEngine`'s "Enhancement" modifier (same underlying field) and its own "Unproficient" penalty already exists structurally via `proficiencyPenalty`, so those two `WeaponsEngine` modifiers are correctly-excludable structural mirrors, not missing contributions. But `WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers()`'s **Attuned Lightsaber Bonus** (+1, `target: 'attack.bonus'`) and its **lightsaber upgrade (crystal) modifiers** (`target` derived from the upgrade's own `domain` field, defaulting to `'attack.bonus'`) have **no structural mirror anywhere** in `resolveAttackBonus()` — `getWeaponFlatAttackBonus()` never inspects `flags.swse.attunedBy`, `installedUpgrades`, or an upgrade Item's `system.modifiers`. These are genuinely missing, not double-counted.

**Fix — a weapon-scoped shared authority, not an actor-wide scan**: `WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers(actor)` is actor-wide (loops every equipped weapon) and cannot be imported into `combat-roll-math.js` without a circular dependency (`weapons-engine.js` already imports `resolveAttackBonus()`/`resolveDamageBonus()` FROM `combat-roll-math.js` — see that file's own header comment forbidding the reverse import). The attunement + lightsaber-upgrade-modifier logic was extracted into a new, single-weapon-scoped exported function, `combat-stat-rules.js#getWeaponAttunementAndUpgradeModifiers(actor, weapon)` — a file both `weapons-engine.js` and `combat-roll-math.js` already import from, with no circularity. `WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers()` now calls this shared function per equipped weapon instead of duplicating the logic (byte-identical output, re-verified against `weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs`'s existing 6/6 assertions, including its malformed-modifier-isolation case). `combat-roll-math.js#resolveAttackBonus()` calls the exact same function for the CURRENT weapon only — deliberately excluding the Enhancement/Proficiency structural mirrors (that function never emits them) and never touching another equipped weapon's own attunement/crystals (weapon-scoped by construction: the function takes one specific weapon, never scans `actor.items` for other weapon Items).

**Target-alias normalization**: `'global.attack'` (Effect Intent, situational, typed combat-option contributions) and `'attack.bonus'` (weapon-sourced contributions) are two historical spellings for the identical stacking-eligible concept. Rather than maintaining two separate `ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()` passes (which would silently let a competence-typed crystal and a competence-typed Active Effect both apply in full), `resolveAttackBonus()` now normalizes any `'attack.bonus'`-targeted modifier to `'global.attack'` **in memory, immediately before stacking resolution** — the original `Modifier` object (and any persisted item/actor data) is never mutated; every other field (source, sourceId, sourceName, type, value, priority) survives verbatim into the ledger. This is the same "normalize accepted aliases without rewriting persisted data" contract the independent review specified.

**A genuine second defect found and fixed while relocating this logic**: `WeaponsEngine`'s original `#mapBonusType()` helper (now `combat-stat-rules.js#mapWeaponUpgradeBonusType()`) was a narrow 4-entry allowlist (`force`, `enhancement`, `untyped`, `equipment`) that silently downgraded any OTHER canonical `bonusType` (`competence`, `circumstance`, `morale`, `insight`, `dodge`, `penalty`, `armor`, `restriction`, `flanking`) to `UNTYPED` — which would have let two same-type crystal modifiers wrongly both stack instead of correctly colliding (caught by this round's own new cross-type stacking test, item 4 below, before it ever shipped). Fixed to a generic `Object.values(ModifierType).includes(key)` membership check, matching the identical idiom this project already uses for the same problem in `grappling-system.js#collectContextualGrappleModifiers()`. Separately, `bonusType: 'force'` (used by this project's own crystal-modifier data contract, e.g. `data/lightsaber-components.json`'s Ilum/Synthetic/Barab entries) had no corresponding canonical `ModifierType` at all — any upgrade modifier tagged `'force'` would fail `createModifier()`'s validation and be silently dropped. Added `ModifierType.FORCE` (left out of `STACKING_RULES`, defaulting to `'stack'` — no RAW evidence found that a Force-crystal bonus should be capped `highestOnly` against another, and a lightsaber typically carries at most one crystal slot).

**Truthful suppression reasons**: round 3's suppressed-ledger-row reason text was a hardcoded `"...already applies (highestOnly stacking)"` string for every suppressed contribution, regardless of its actual stacking rule — untrue for a `circumstance`-typed contribution (`stackUnlessSameSource`). Fixed: the reason is now derived from `getStackingRule(modifier.type)` (`ModifierTypes.js`'s existing exported helper), with distinct, accurate phrasing per rule (`highestOnly`, `lowestOnly`, `stackUnlessSameSource`). Proven by a dedicated test: two `CIRCUMSTANCE`-typed contributions from the *same* source collapse to the higher one, and the suppressed row's reason explicitly names `stackUnlessSameSource`, never `highestOnly`.

**Required test matrix** (`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`, new "Section 5b" block, all against `resolveAttackBonus()` directly and cross-checked against `computeFinalAttackComposition()`): fail-before/fix proof for an attuned lightsaber (+1 reaches the total; the same weapon unattuned does not); an installed crystal's modifier reaches the roll exactly once (and is idempotent across repeated invocation); two same-type (competence) crystal modifiers on one weapon correctly resolve to highest-only, not a bare sum; weapon enhancement and nonproficiency remain exactly-once structural terms (unaffected by the new typed pool, proven with non-lightsaber weapons where the new function is a no-op); a second equipped weapon never receives the first weapon's attunement/crystal contributions (weapon-scoping, using two lightsabers on one actor); a competence-typed crystal (`attack.bonus`) and a competence-typed Active Effect (`global.attack`) collide in ONE stacking universe, not two (the target-alias-normalization proof); a same-type crystal-vs-Charge collision resolves correctly; a `stackUnlessSameSource` circumstance suppression is never mislabeled `highestOnly` (the truthful-reason proof); and `SUM(applied ledger contributions) === atkBonus` holds throughout, via the full `computeFinalAttackComposition()` pipeline.

### Phase A, continued: the full attack-Modifier producer inventory this round's own review demanded

Every source `ModifierEngine.getAllModifiers()` aggregates was inspected for an attack-domain target, not assumed:

- **Feats / Talents / Species / Backgrounds / Encumbrance / Items / Condition modifiers** (`ModifierEngine._getFeatModifiers`/`_getTalentModifiers`/`_getSpeciesModifiers`/`_getBackgroundModifiers`/`_getEncumbranceModifiers`/`_getItemModifiers`/`_getConditionModifiers`): grepped every `target:` construction in each — confirmed every one only ever produces `skill.*`, `defense.*`, `speed.*`, or `initiative.total` targets. **Classification D (another-domain-only).**
- **Custom modifiers** (`actor.system.customModifiers`, `ModifierEngine._getCustomModifiers`): the `target` field is read verbatim from stored data (architecturally open-ended — could theoretically be `'global.attack'`). The only live authoring UI found, `skill-modifier-breakdown-app.js#_submitCustomModifier()`, hardcodes `target: \`skill.${this.skillKey}\`` — no discovered path creates an attack-domain custom modifier today. **Classification C (compatibility/dead for the attack domain specifically)**, with the caveat that a not-yet-found UI, macro, or future feature could populate one; `resolveAttackBonus()` does not currently consume this channel at all (a disclosed, deliberately-deferred gap, not touched this round).
- **Legacy `system.activeEffects`** (`ModifierEngine._getActiveEffectModifiers`): confirmed live writers exist (`force-regimen-executor.js`, `meta-resource-feat-resolver.js`, `species-activated-ability-engine.js`, `active-effects-engine.js`), and the reader accepts any canonical `ModifierType`-typed target. Every live writer's actual `target:` construction was checked directly: `force-regimen-executor.js` writes a narrative-badge string (`'Force Regimen'`, filtered out by `_getActiveEffectModifiers`'s own type-validity guard since it isn't a real `ModifierType`); `species-activated-ability-engine.js`'s `target` fields are the affected actor's ID, an unrelated field sharing the same name, not a Modifier target key; `meta-resource-feat-resolver.js` only writes `defense.reflex`. **Classification C (compatibility/dead for the attack domain specifically)** — architecturally capable, zero live attack-domain authors found; also not consumed by `resolveAttackBonus()` today, same disclosed-not-fixed status as custom modifiers.
- **PASSIVE modifiers** (`actor._passiveModifiers`, registered by `PassiveAdapter.handleModifier()` from `abilityMeta.modifiers[].target`, surfaced via `ModifierEngine._getPassiveModifiers()`): this is the SAME data shape `resolveAttackBonus()`'s own pre-existing, already-disabled `stateBonus` loop reads directly from `actor.items` (gated by a per-modifier `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus === true` opt-in, confirmed in the round-3 audit to have zero live producers). Grepping every compiled feat/talent pack for `"target":"attack"` found **259 records** across `feats.db`, `feat-catalog.db`, `talents.db`, and `lightsaber-crystals.db` — of which **249 are explicitly `"enabled":false`**, each carrying a `description` documenting that it is an intentionally-disabled migration remnant now represented elsewhere (e.g. "No numeric bonus; represented as an autofire attack option"), consistent with this project's established convention for disabled/superseded modifier records. Of the **3 records with `"enabled":true`**, two — **Forceful Warrior** (`+2`, predicate `attack.when-hit`) and **Close Maneuvering** (`+2`, predicate `movement.while-moving`, `executionModel: 'ACTIVE'` not `PASSIVE`) — were checked against their own `benefit` text and found to be **data mismatches**: Forceful Warrior's real benefit is "gain 1 temporary Force Point on a lightsaber critical hit," entirely unrelated to a flat attack bonus; Close Maneuvering's real benefit is an Attack-of-Opportunity exemption while moving, also unrelated. Both are inert today (blocked by the same `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus` gate) and are stale data, not live gaps. **The third, Greater Weapon Focus** (`+1`, `untyped`, predicate `attack.weapon-matches-selected-choice`, `executionModel: 'PASSIVE'`/`subType: 'STATE'`), is **NOT a mismatch** — its `benefit` text reads verbatim: *"You gain a +1 bonus on attack rolls with the chosen Exotic Weapon or weapon from the chosen group. This bonus stacks with the bonus granted by the Weapon Focus feat"* — matching its modifier record exactly (untyped, so it correctly stacks per its own stated text). `ScopedCombatFeatResolver.explicitFeatBonus()` (the live authority for Weapon Focus/Weapon Specialization/Point Blank Shot) was checked directly and does **not** handle Greater Weapon Focus at all. **This is a genuine, currently-real, live gap**: a correctly-authored, RAW-accurate talent bonus that reaches no attack roll today, blocked solely by the `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus` gate having zero opted-in records. **Classification E (unknown/real gap, disclosed, deliberately NOT fixed this round)** — enabling it requires a one-record compendium data change (`talents.db`), individually verified against its own RAW text exactly as done here; the other 249 records require the same individual verification before any of them could safely be enabled, and are explicitly out of this round's declared code-only scope. Flagged as the specific, scoped next step for a dedicated follow-up round.
- **`ModifierEngineExtensions.getModifiersForDomain(actor, 'attack')`** (the domain-wide `stackingRule: 'highest'` authority the independent review explicitly warned against adopting): grepped for any live call site targeting the attack domain — none found. Not used anywhere in the attack-roll path, before or after this round's changes, confirming the review's warning was preemptive, not describing an existing regression.

### Updated certification status (this round; STATIC's fixed/certified claim below itself found to rest on the wrong data schema by a fifth correction round — see below)

- CORE ATTACK FORMULA: **CERTIFIED**
- STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: fixed and partially certified here, then found to have proven the fix against an obsolete/non-generator-native lightsaber crystal schema by a further independent review — see "Domain: Attack Bonus — fifth correction round" below.
- CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged this round — Charge/Flanking/Effect-Intent/typed-combat-option unification from round 3 stands; the weapon-modifier fix this round is additive to the same seam, does not alter its correctness, and is itself now proven cross-type-collision-safe against it)
- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged architecturally; both paths inherit whatever `resolveAttackBonus()` now correctly includes)
- NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING: **PARTIALLY CERTIFIED** (unchanged; no new evidence this round)
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: NOT CERTIFIED** — held open by both the NPC statblock sub-domain's partial status and STATIC's newly-disclosed Greater Weapon Focus gap.

Full validation re-run green after this round's fixes: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (29 assertion blocks, including the new WeaponsEngine attack.bonus block), `weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6, unchanged output re-verified after the relocation refactor), `combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, `grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`, `vehicle-attack-routing-guard-check.test.mjs`, the Phase 3/4 vehicle/routing source-text guards, the full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs`, `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks (exact counts in the PR handoff).

## Domain: Attack Bonus — fifth correction round (independent review found round 4's crystal-modifier fix proven against an obsolete/non-generator-native schema)

A further independent review of the round above (head `56d8e7217131c6f249454a5fa63096ae80008885`) accepted the architecture round 4 established — weapon-scoped contribution collection, one shared `attack.bonus`/`global.attack` stacking universe, structural-mirror exclusion for enhancement/nonproficiency, truthful suppression reasons — but held the PR: round 4's implementation and tests modeled a lightsaber crystal as a separate owned `weaponUpgrade` Item referenced via `weapon.system.installedUpgrades` (an id array) carrying a `{domain, bonusType, value}` modifier shape. **That is not how a lightsaber is actually built or how the shipped crystal compendium is actually shaped.** The review's closing lesson: *"A green integration test is not useful if its fixture models an obsolete data shape. The current compendium record plus the current generator/materialization output has to be the starting point for every math certification."*

### Blocker 1 — the generator-native weapon shape

Confirmed directly against `scripts/engine/crafting/lightsaber-construction-engine.js`: `#createBuiltLightsaber()` (new self-built sabers) and `applyEdits()` (recrafting an existing one) both do `modifiers.push(...crystal.system.modifiers, ...accessories.flatMap(a => a.system.modifiers))` and write the result to the finished weapon's own `system.modifiers` field, alongside `flags.swse.lightsaberConfig`/`flags['foundryvtt-swse'].lightsaberConfig` (recording `crystalId`/`accessoryIds` for display, not for math). **`weapon.system.installedUpgrades` is never populated by this — the only live lightsaber-construction path.** A separate, general (non-lightsaber-specific) item-customization system, `install-remove-engine.js`, is the one confirmed live writer of `system.installedUpgrades` anywhere in the repo — but its array holds `{id: <random instanceId>, name, cost, slotsUsed, description, restriction}` display-summary objects, not actor-item id references, and is architecturally unrelated to lightsaber crystals (its effects are applied by directly mutating the item's own structural fields via `EffectResolver.resolveUpgradeEffects()`, already covered by the existing `getWeaponFlatAttackBonus()` structural read, not by a separate Modifier-record channel). A third file, `phase-3-advanced-crystal-system.js`, does expect an `installedUpgrades`-array-of-actor-item-ids shape matching round 4's assumption — but it is imported by nothing anywhere in the codebase (grepped repo-wide): **confirmed dead code**, never wired to any live construction or roll path. **Conclusion: the shape round 4's fixture and production code read has zero live producers anywhere in this codebase.** `combat-stat-rules.js#getWeaponAttunementAndUpgradeModifiers()` was rewritten to read `weapon.system.modifiers` directly (the real, generator-native field) instead; the `installedUpgrades`-reading branch was removed outright rather than kept as unreachable legacy-compatibility code, since no real or hypothetical live producer for that exact shape was found to justify retaining it. If a future contributor reintroduces a real actor-item-referencing `installedUpgrades` producer, the fail-closed interpreter below should be extended to cover it, reusing the same per-record classification rather than a second, separately-maintained mapper.

### Blocker 2 — the real crystal-pack schema is heterogeneous, and unknown types must fail closed

`packs/lightsaber-crystals.db` (the actual compiled, shipped compendium — not the older, non-authoritative `data/lightsaber-components.json`/`data/lightsaber-items-import.ndjson` reference files round 4 was unknowingly influenced by) was parsed directly and every record's `system.modifiers` inspected. Confirmed 18 distinct `type` values in current use: `ATTACK_BONUS`, `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK`, `DAMAGE_BONUS`, `CONDITIONAL_DAMAGE`, `DEFENSE_BONUS`, `ENEMY_PENALTY`, `SKILL_BONUS`, `SKILL_MODIFIER`, `HEALING_BONUS`, `DAMAGE_TYPE_CHANGE`, `DAMAGE_REDUCTION`, `CRITICAL_BONUS`, `FORCE_POINT_DIE_UPGRADE`, `REROLL_ABILITY`, `LIGHT_EMISSION`, `SENSE_OVERRIDE`, `ALIGNMENT_REFLECTION`, `CRITICAL_FAILURE`. Round 4's helper read a `mod.domain` field (absent from every real record) and defaulted an absent/unrecognized domain to `'attack.bonus'` — on the real schema this would have silently turned Kasha Crystal's `+2` Will Defense, Jenraux's `+2` Block, Sigil's `+2` damage, Krayt Dragon Pearl's `+3` damage, Mantle of the Force's `+2` Use the Force, and Compressed Crystal's `-2` enemy Block penalty into permanent attack bonuses, had round 4's fixture ever been exercised against real data instead of its own invented shape.

**Fix**: the interpreter now recognizes exactly two `type` values as attack-relevant — `ATTACK_BONUS` (with its own `target === 'attack'`) emits an unconditional attack Modifier; `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` emits nothing (see Blocker 3). Every other `type` — including every one listed above — emits nothing for Attack Bonus. **There is no unknown-type-defaults-to-attack fallback.** Verified concretely: Ilum, Mephite, and Standard Synthetic Crystal (the ordinary `+1` attack crystals) all carry `{type: 'ATTACK_BONUS', value: 1, target: 'attack'}` with **no `bonusType` field at all** — untyped, not `'force'` as round 4's stale reference data implied. Kathracite Crystal carries both a `DAMAGE_REDUCTION` record (`-1d`, a string value) and an `ATTACK_BONUS` record (`+1`) in the same array — proven to contribute only `+1` to attack, never `-1d` (a string) reaching numeric attack math, and never both records being conflated.

### Blocker 3 — conditional crystal attack modifiers fail closed, not guessed

Heart of the Guardian (`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK`, `+2`, condition `vs-lightsaber-wielders`) and Hurikane Crystal (`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK`, `+2`, condition `vs-armored`) are real attack bonuses, but this project's attack pipeline does not yet provide authoritative, verified target-state context (is the current target a lightsaber wielder? armored?) at the `resolveAttackBonus()` layer — no reliable classifier for either predicate was found anywhere in the codebase to reuse, and inventing one was explicitly out of scope for this round. Per the reviewer's own instruction — *"A known conditional bonus silently not applying is preferable to a known-wrong permanent +2"* — both are deliberately **NOT YET AUTOMATED**: the interpreter emits nothing for `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records unconditionally, proven by test to remain `+0` even when an unrelated context flag (`targetIsArmored`) is present, so a future contributor cannot accidentally satisfy the wrong crystal's condition via cross-wired context.

### Blocker 4 — `ModifierType.FORCE` reverted

Round 4 added `ModifierType.FORCE` (left out of `STACKING_RULES`, defaulting to `'stack'`) reasoning from the stale `data/lightsaber-components.json` reference data. No authoritative SWSE source-text evidence for how multiple "Force bonus"-labeled effects stack was found in this repo. Per the reviewer's explicit instruction not to invent global stacking semantics without evidence, **`ModifierType.FORCE` was reverted outright** (removed from `ModifierTypes.js` entirely). This is safe and creates no new gap: every real crystal record that does carry `bonusType: 'force'` (Kasha, Jenraux, Sigil, Krayt, Mantle, Heart of the Guardian, Hurikane) is a `DEFENSE_BONUS`/`DAMAGE_BONUS`/`SKILL_BONUS`/`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` record — none of which the interpreter constructs an attack Modifier from at all (Blockers 2 and 3) — so no live code path ever needs to construct a `FORCE`-typed Modifier today. If a future round automates Heart of the Guardian/Hurikane's conditions, or a Force-typed *unconditional* attack crystal is ever added to the compendium, that round must establish the RAW stacking evidence before reintroducing the type, per the same standard applied here.

### Legacy `installedUpgrades` support and dedup

No live producer creates `weapon.system.installedUpgrades` in the shape (`array of ids resolving to owned weaponUpgrade Items`) any interpreter here has ever assumed — confirmed above. Compatibility support for that shape was therefore not retained; there is nothing to deduplicate against `weapon.system.modifiers`, since only the latter is ever read. `weapon.system.installedUpgrades`'s one real, live producer (the general item-customization system) writes an unrelated shape and is out of this round's scope, as established in Blocker 1.

### Attunement — unaffected

The accepted self-built-lightsaber attunement contract (`flags.swse.builtBy === actor.id && flags.swse.attunedBy === actor.id` → `+1`, untyped, `attack.bonus`) is untouched by this round's schema correction — it was never part of the `installedUpgrades`/crystal-modifier defect, reads directly from `weapon.flags`, and remains weapon-scoped by construction (proven again this round with two equipped lightsabers, one attuned with a crystal, one plain: the plain one receives neither contribution).

### Required test matrix

`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`'s Section 5b was rewritten against real `packs/lightsaber-crystals.db` record shapes (parsed directly from the compiled pack, not inferred): fail-before/fix proof for attunement (unchanged) and for Ilum/Mephite/Standard Synthetic's real `ATTACK_BONUS` records (each `+1`, idempotent across repeated invocation); Kathracite's `DAMAGE_REDUCTION` record contributes zero while its `ATTACK_BONUS` record applies; a full negative matrix (Kasha, Jenraux, Sigil, Krayt Dragon Pearl, Mantle of the Force, Ankarres Sapphire, Compressed Crystal, and two string-valued records — Dragite's `CRITICAL_BONUS`, Bondar's `DAMAGE_TYPE_CHANGE`) each proven to contribute exactly zero to attack and never throw; Heart of the Guardian/Hurikane proven `+0` with no context AND with an unrelated context flag present; weapon enhancement and nonproficiency proven exactly-once structural terms; two equipped lightsabers proven not to leak contributions into each other; the interpreter's `bonusType` support and the `attack.bonus`/`global.attack` alias normalization proven via explicitly-labeled **interpreter-contract** tests (a same-weapon same-type collision, a cross-target collision against a real Active Effect, and a collision against Charge) — clearly distinguished in both code comments and test labels from the shipped-crystal RAW tests, per the reviewer's explicit instruction not to invent a fictitious crystal competence type; a truthful `stackUnlessSameSource` suppression-reason proof (never mislabeled `highestOnly`); and `SUM(applied ledger) === atkBonus` parity throughout, via `computeFinalAttackComposition()`. `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs`'s sections 3-5 were also rewritten to the same real schema (its round-4-era fixtures encoded the identical obsolete assumption); all 6/6 of its assertions re-verified passing.

### Updated certification status (current)

- CORE ATTACK FORMULA: **CERTIFIED**
- STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **PARTIALLY CERTIFIED** — the WeaponsEngine `attack.bonus` gap is now fixed and proven against the REAL generator-native schema (attunement, and real Ilum/Mephite/Standard-Synthetic-shaped `ATTACK_BONUS` crystal records), with a fail-closed interpreter verified never to misclassify any of the 18 real non-attack-bonus record types found in the compendium, and `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records explicitly left unautomated rather than guessed. **Not certified**: Heart of the Guardian/Hurikane's real conditional attack bonuses still do not reach a roll (deliberately, fail-closed); Greater Weapon Focus's real +1 attack bonus still does not reach a roll (round 4 finding, unchanged, deliberately left open per this round's explicit instruction not to auto-resolve it); the broader 259-record PASSIVE-modifier compendium surface remains individually unverified.
- CONTEXTUAL ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged — the round-3 unification stands; this round's schema correction does not alter its correctness, and the interpreter-contract tests re-prove cross-type collision safety against the corrected weapon channel)
- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged architecturally)
- NPC STATBLOCK/PUBLISHED-TOTAL HANDLING: **PARTIALLY CERTIFIED** (unchanged; no new evidence this round)
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: NOT CERTIFIED** — held open by the NPC statblock sub-domain's partial status, Greater Weapon Focus, and the two conditional lightsaber crystals now explicitly documented as not-yet-automated.

Full validation re-run green after this round's fixes: `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (29 assertion blocks, Section 5b rewritten against real pack data), `weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6, rewritten to the same real schema), `combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, `grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`, `vehicle-attack-routing-guard-check.test.mjs`, the Phase 3/4 vehicle/routing source-text guards, the full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs`, `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks (exact counts in the PR handoff).

## Domain: Attack Bonus — sixth correction round (independent review accepted round 5's crystal correction in full, and raised five new findings)

An independent review of round 5 (head `79e867e66f90e7c3517db640cb6d9cb02bae9a3c`) accepted the generator-native crystal correction without reservation, and raised five further findings: Greater Weapon Focus's "open gap" claim was itself based on an incomplete investigation; the Force-stacking question now has RAW evidence; Heart of the Guardian and Hurikane can be automated safely against the roll's resolved target actor; and one exported attack entry point still bypassed the accepted `computeFinalAttackComposition()` seam.

### Finding 1 — Greater Weapon Focus was never actually an open attack-math gap

Round 4's claim ("Greater Weapon Focus's real +1 attack bonus still does not reach a roll") was based on investigating only ONE consumption path for `item.system.abilityMeta.modifiers`: `resolveAttackBonus()`'s own internal, dead `PASSIVE`/`STATE` `stateBonus` loop, gated by `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus` (confirmed zero live opt-ins). A completely separate, already-live path was missed: `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()` → `collectWeaponRuleModifiers()` → `collectModifierRollBonuses()` reads the identical `abilityMeta.modifiers` shape independently, gated on `modifierAppliesToWeaponRoll()`'s `attack.weapon-matches-selected-choice` predicate, which resolves against `item.system.selectedChoice` via `getSelectedChoiceValues()` — confirmed by direct source reading, not asserted. This is the real persistence contract (not `flags.swse.choices.*`, as the catalog's `choiceMeta.storagePath` metadata might suggest). Per the explicit instruction not to "fix" this by enabling `allowLegacyStateAttackBonus` (which would create a second authority and double-count the first), no code change to the GWF consumption path itself was made — only an executable golden-test proof, per the required 8-case matrix (`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`, Section 7): (1) matching `selectedChoice` + matching weapon → +1; (2) mismatching weapon → +0; (3) missing `selectedChoice` → +0; (4) Weapon Focus + Greater Weapon Focus, both matching → +2 (stacking proven); (5) mutation test — changing `selectedChoice` away from the weapon loses the +1 on the very next resolution; (6)/(7) Greater Weapon Focus (Lightsabers)'s real `WEAPON_ATTACK_BONUS` rule — melee lightsaber → +1, non-lightsaber → +0; (8) Greater Weapon Focus (Fira)'s real `ATTACK_OPTION` rule — qualifying melee Fira → +1, unrelated weapon → +0. All 8 pass. **The "Greater Weapon Focus does not reach an attack roll" gap is removed from this ledger — it was never true of the shipped record.**

**Unplanned discovery while proving item 4 (Weapon Focus + Greater Weapon Focus stacking):** the real Weapon Focus feat record (`packs/feats.db`, id `c41814601364b643`) already carries the identical data-driven `abilityMeta.modifiers` shape (`target: 'attack.bonus', value: 1, type: 'untyped', predicates: ['attack.weapon-matches-selected-choice']`) — but `scripts/engine/feat/scoped-combat-feat-resolver.js#ScopedCombatFeatResolver.getBonus()` ALSO independently grants +1 for any feat item whose normalized name is `weapon-focus` and whose own, separate `selectedChoiceValue()`/`weaponMatchesSelectedChoice()` heuristic matches the weapon — unconditionally, for every character actor, added directly into `resolveAttackBonus()`'s `total` alongside `CombatOptionResolver`'s own contribution. **Two independent authorities for the identical bonus, both live, both unconditionally summed** — every real character with the real Weapon Focus feat was silently double-counted (+2 instead of +1) on every attack roll with the selected weapon, a genuine, currently-shipping math-integrity defect, not a synthetic test artifact (confirmed by first reproducing it with the real record shape, which produced +3 instead of the expected +2 for the GWF+Weapon-Focus stacking test). **Fixed narrowly, attack-side only:** `explicitFeatBonus()`'s `weapon-focus`/`target === 'attack'` branch now checks `hasDataDrivenAttackModifier(item)` first and yields 0 when the item already carries its own data-driven attack modifier — preserving the hardcoded fallback only for legacy feat items that predate that shape (proven by the existing, unmodified `combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs` unit test, whose bare `{ name: 'Weapon Focus', system: { selectedChoice } }` fixture — no `abilityMeta` — still receives its +1 from this resolver). Weapon Specialization's damage bonus and Point Blank Shot (confirmed the sole live authority for its own attack contribution — no competing data-driven record) are untouched; this is a Damage-domain question for Weapon Specialization specifically, out of this round's scope.

### Finding 2 — `ModifierType.FORCE` restored with RAW evidence and an explicit stacking rule

SWSE Core Rulebook p.241, "Stacking Bonuses": different descriptors combine; two bonuses of the same named/descriptor type use only the higher; untyped bonuses always stack; circumstance and dodge are the only ordinary exceptions that stack with themselves. A named Force bonus is therefore a typed, nonstacking bonus like any other named type. `ModifierType.FORCE = 'force'` is restored, with an **explicit** `STACKING_RULES.force = 'highestOnly'` entry (not `getStackingRule()`'s `|| 'stack'` fallback). Repo-searched every current `bonusType: 'force'` usage before making this change: all are crystal `DEFENSE_BONUS`/`DAMAGE_BONUS`/`SKILL_BONUS`/`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records (Kasha, Jenraux, Sigil, Krayt Dragon Pearl, Mantle of the Force, Heart of the Guardian, Hurikane); none of the various `type: 'force'` string tags found elsewhere in the codebase (damage-type tags on `ActorEngine.applyDamage()` calls, roll-type tags) are `ModifierType` enum values, so none are affected by or need reclassifying because of this change — no unnamed bonus is retyped to Force merely because its source involves the Force. Direct `ModifierUtils` stacking tests (Section 8): `+2 Force + +5 Force` → `+5` (highestOnly); `Force + competence` → both apply; `Force + untyped` → both apply.

### Finding 3 — Hurikane (`vs-armored`) automated against the resolved target actor

`getWeaponAttunementAndUpgradeModifiers()` now accepts an optional `{ targetActor }` and handles `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records via a small `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK_CRYSTAL_CONDITIONS` map (fail-closed on an unrecognized `condition` string — no fallback guess). `resolveAttackBonus()` obtains `targetActor` from its own existing, already-exported `getTargetActorFromOptions(context)` — the canonical target resolver every other consumer (dialog preview, `rollAttack()`) already uses; no second resolver was invented. "vs-armored" qualification: the target must be wearing **equipped** body armor, via the certified armor equipped-state authority (`armor-data-resolver.js#isArmorItemEquipped()` — the same authority Defense math uses), explicitly excluding an Energy-Shield-classified item (`isEnergyShieldItem()`) — no source evidence was found that an active shield alone counts as "in armor" for this crystal. The resulting +2 is emitted as a `FORCE`-typed Modifier (via the existing `mapWeaponUpgradeBonusType('force')` mapping, now resolving correctly since Finding 2 restored the type), joining the same unified typed pool as every other attack contribution. Required matrix (Section 8b) — no target / target owns no armor / target owns unequipped armor / target wears equipped body armor / target has only an equipped Energy Shield / an alternate certified equipped-armor flag (`system.readied`) — all pass, `+0`/`+0`/`+0`/`+2`/`+0`/`+2` respectively.

### Finding 4 — Heart of the Guardian (`vs-lightsaber-wielders`) automated, and a shared equipped-item-state authority promoted

"Ownership is not wielding" — the target must currently WIELD (equip) a lightsaber, not merely own one. Classification uses the existing canonical `combat-stat-rules.js#isLightsaberWeapon()`. Equipped-state needed a shared authority: `scripts/actors/v2/character-actor.js#isItemEquipped()` (recognizing `system.equipped`, `system.isEquipped`, `system.active`, `system.readied`, `system.equippable.equipped`, `system.equippable.active`, `system.activation.active`, `flags.swse.equipped`, auto-equipped natural weapons, and integrated droid weapons) was **promoted verbatim** into `scripts/items/weapon-branch-resolver.js` (already a dependency-free leaf module imported by both `character-actor.js` and `combat-stat-rules.js`, so no circular import) as `isItemEquipped(item, actor)`. `character-actor.js` now delegates to it instead of keeping its own copy (its own item-mirroring pass is otherwise untouched — `isAttackItem()`/`hasWeaponDamageProfile()` stay local, duplicated in miniature inside the promoted module only for the one internal integrated-droid-weapon sub-check, since they also serve `character-actor.js`'s unrelated inventory-mirroring scan and promoting them wholesale would have widened this round's blast radius beyond what Finding 4 needed). This is one of at least four independent `isTruthyEquipState`-style implementations found in the codebase (`character-actor.js`, `CombatEngine.js`, `armor-data-resolver.js`, `PanelContextBuilder.js`) — only `character-actor.js`'s copy (the one Heart of the Guardian needed) was consolidated this round; the others are unrelated-consumer, out-of-scope duplication left for a future pass, not touched here.
