Required matrix (Section 8b) — no target / target owns an unequipped lightsaber / target wields an equipped lightsaber / target equips a non-lightsaber weapon / an alternate certified equip field (`system.readied`) / mutation (unequipping the target's lightsaber loses the +2 on the very next resolution) / two different targets (no state leakage) — all pass.

### Force stacking + conditional-crystal cross-type matrix (Section 8c, items A–H)

A. Heart alone, qualified → +2. B. Hurikane alone, qualified → +2. C. condition unsatisfied → +0 for both. D. a qualifying conditional crystal + an unrelated +4 Force contribution → only +4 applies (`highestOnly`, same descriptor). E. qualifying +2 Force crystal + +2 competence → both apply (+4, different descriptors). F. qualifying +2 Force crystal + +1 untyped → both apply (+3). G. the suppressed Force contribution remains visible in the typed-modifier ledger with `applied: false` and a truthful `highestOnly` reason (not mislabeled). H. `computeFinalAttackComposition()` (the dialog-preview/roll shared seam) sees the identical target-conditioned result as `resolveAttackBonus()` alone when a target is available, and fails closed to +0 — never guesses a target — when none is resolvable. All pass.

### Finding 5 — the narration attack-path divergence closed (attack side only)

`scripts/combat/rolls/attacks.js#rollAttackAndDamageWithNarration()` called `resolveAttackBonus(actor, weapon, null, rollOptions).total` directly, bypassing `computeFinalAttackComposition()` — the shared seam both the attack dialog's live preview and `rollAttack()` already go through. That meant Fighting Defensively, the grapple-state attack penalty, a custom modifier, a sequence penalty, and the legacy `situationalBonus` override — all invocation-only additions `computeFinalAttackComposition()` layers on top of the resolver's own total — were silently dropped for this exported entry point specifically. No current production caller reaches this function (confirmed: a dormant exported divergence, not a reproduced player bug), but per the explicit instruction, a known alternate attack-roll formula is not left in place for a future caller to find. Fixed: only the attack-bonus line now delegates to `computeFinalAttackComposition()` (with its own `!composition.ok` failure handling, mirroring `rollAttack()`'s own); Damage composition in the same function is untouched. Required live parity tests (Section 9, `RollEngine.safeRoll`/`SWSEChat.postRoll` stubbed, everything else live): Fighting Defensively, the grapple-state attack penalty, a custom modifier, a sequence penalty, and a typed contextual modifier — each proven to produce the exact rolled attack bonus `computeFinalAttackComposition()` reports, with an explicit fail-before contrast (the old `resolveAttackBonus()`-only total genuinely differs) for the three cases that are true invocation-only additions (a typed contextual modifier is resolved *inside* `resolveAttackBonus()` itself, so it was never actually part of this bug, and is proven equal without a fail-before contrast).

### Required validation

`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`: 34 assertion blocks (was 29; Sections 7, 8, 8b, 8c, 9 added), all pass. `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6), `tests/combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `tests/armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `tests/stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, `tests/vehicle-attack-routing-guard-check.test.mjs`, `tests/attack-outcome-wiring.test.mjs`, `tests/attack-transaction-rollback.test.mjs`, `tests/phase3-reroll-supersession.test.mjs`, `tests/character-actor-inventory-index-and-vehicle-skip.test.mjs`, `tests/legacy-equip-state-single-pass-authority.test.mjs` all re-run green. Full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2501/2501 files), `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks all green (exact rolling-suite pass count in the PR handoff).

No newly-discovered static source remains open.

- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED** — the narration-path divergence (Finding 5) is closed; dialog preview, `rollAttack()`, and `rollAttackAndDamageWithNarration()` now all agree via the one shared `computeFinalAttackComposition()` seam.

## Domain: Attack Bonus — seventh correction round (independent review found round 6's STATIC/CONTEXTUAL certification premature: the standard-crystal attunement double-count, Heart's missing "active" requirement, and Hurikane's wrong armor condition)

An independent review of round 6 (head `c2acc05962a70cec99756ff582bb59eae6e3462c`, CI green, independently re-verified) accepted six of round 6's fixes outright (Greater Weapon Focus's live path, the Weapon Focus double-count fix, `ModifierType.FORCE`, the narration-path fix) but held the PR on the STATIC and CONTEXTUAL Attack Bonus certifications: the lightsaber-crystal math was still not source-correct.

### Two factual corrections to the review's own disclosure claims

Before the substantive fixes: the review asked for two "omitted disclosures" — that round 6 added `ModifierType.FLANKING` (because `roll-config.js` already emitted it before the enum member existed) and that round 6 removed the automatic Noble Fencing Style → CHA override from `getWeaponAttackAbility()`. Both were checked directly against `git show c2acc05` before acting on them, and neither is accurate: `FLANKING` was already present in `ModifierTypes.js` since the second correction round (`ModifierType.FLANKING = 'flanking'`, `STACKING_RULES.flanking = 'highestOnly'`) — round 6's diff to that file added only `FORCE` and its stacking rule, confirmed by the actual patch hunk. `combat-stat-rules.js` has zero lines touched in round 6's commit at all — Noble Fencing Style's CHA-override removal happened in the very first correction round (documented there as "Blocker 1 — Noble Fencing Style still violated player-owned attack ability") and was never revisited in round 6. No disclosure correction was made to the ledger for either claim, since there was nothing round 6 actually did that needed disclosing.

### Blocker 1 — the standard/self-built lightsaber crystal was worth +2, not +1, and applied to non-attuned wielders

The largest finding, confirmed directly against `lightsaber-construction-engine.js` and the actual compiled `packs/lightsaber-crystals.db`. Every round through the sixth added the generic "Attuned lightsaber" +1 UNCONDITIONALLY for any self-built/attuned weapon, and separately interpreted the weapon's `ATTACK_BONUS`/`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records with NO attunement gate at all. Two independent defects followed: (a) an attuned Ilum-crystal saber (Ilum's real record: `{type:'ATTACK_BONUS', value:1, target:'attack'}`) scored +2 — the generic +1 plus the crystal's own +1 — when SWSE lightsaber construction rules make these the SAME benefit (the standard crystal's normal benefit IS the self-built/attuned +1; an alternate crystal's benefit REPLACES that +1, never adds to it); (b) a non-attuned wielder of any crystal-bearing saber still received the crystal's own `ATTACK_BONUS`/`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` records, since only the generic +1 was ever attunement-gated.

**Fix**: `getWeaponAttunementAndUpgradeModifiers()` now gates its ENTIRE body — the generic +1 and every `ATTACK_BONUS`/`CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` record — on a new `isSelfBuiltLightsaberAttunedToActor(actor, weapon)` helper (`builtBy === actor.id && attunedBy === actor.id`, resolving both the current `flags.swse` and legacy `flags['foundryvtt-swse']` scopes). Which of the two benefits applies (the generic +1, or the crystal's own recorded benefit) is decided by a new `isStandardBaselineLightsaberCrystal(crystalId, weaponModifierRecords)`: true when the weapon's recorded crystal (`lightsaberConfig.crystalId`) is the generator's virtual, no-mechanical-payload baseline crystal (`lightsaber-crystal-standard-kyber`, confirmed by direct read of `lightsaber-construction-engine.js#getDefaultKyberCrystalOption()` — its `system.modifiers` is `[]`), or when no crystal identity was ever recorded at all AND the weapon's modifiers array is empty (a pre-`lightsaberConfig` legacy/compatibility case). Deciding this from crystal IDENTITY rather than "the modifiers array happens to be empty" matters specifically because an alternate crystal with no `ATTACK_BONUS` record of its own (Sigil, Kasha, ...) must not regain the generic +1 its own chosen benefit replaced. Accessories remain a separate, independent component slot — their own records (if any) still run through the normal per-record loop regardless of which crystal was chosen, so a baseline-crystal saber combined with a hypothetical attack-relevant accessory would correctly receive both.

### Blocker 2 — Heart of the Guardian ignored blade activation

The real Heart of the Guardian condition is a target CURRENTLY WIELDING AN ACTIVE lightsaber — two independent facts. Round 6's fixtures and check only asked whether the target's lightsaber was equipped, never whether its blade was on. **Fix**: `targetActorWieldsActiveLightsaber()` now requires `isLightsaberWeapon(item) && canonicalIsItemEquipped(item, targetActor) && isItemActivated(item)`. `isItemActivated()` is a new, narrow, purely-mechanical predicate (`system.activated === true || system.active === true`) deliberately NOT sourced from `WeaponVisualProfileResolver.resolveActiveLightsaber()` — confirmed by direct read that authority additionally requires `emitLight` (a token-light VISUAL setting a GM could toggle off for any reason), which a combat rule must never depend on. It instead mirrors exactly the "current" activation-state read `InventoryEngine.toggleActivated()` itself uses before flipping the flag, so this predicate can never disagree with what the toggle button itself considers "currently active."

### Blocker 3 — Hurikane checked "wears armor," not "has an Armor Bonus to Reflex Defense"

The real Hurikane condition is a target with an Armor Bonus to Reflex Defense, not merely "wears an armor-typed item." Round 6's fixture (`{type:'armor', system:{equipped:true, armorType:'medium'}}`) carried no Reflex bonus at all and still qualified. **Fix**: `targetActorHasPositiveReflexArmorBonus()` now requires `item.type === 'armor' && !isEnergyShieldItem(item) && isArmorItemEquipped(item) && Number(resolveArmorData(item).reflexBonus) > 0` — using the canonical resolved armor data (`armor-data-resolver.js#resolveArmorData()`), never reconstructed inline. The real Cortosis Gauntlet (`packs/armor.db`, id `armor-cortosis-gauntlet`: `armorType: 'medium', defenseBonus: 0`) is the mandatory negative regression — equipped armor with a genuinely zero Reflex bonus, worn specifically for its lightsaber-blocking property, correctly does not qualify.

### Blocker 4 — crystal/accessory provenance safety (regression guard, no code change needed)

Construction flattens the selected crystal's AND every selected accessory's own `system.modifiers` into one array with no per-record origin tag. `isStandardBaselineLightsaberCrystal()`'s decision is based on the weapon's recorded CRYSTAL identity only, so it stays correct regardless of what an accessory contributes — but it is only SAFE as long as no accessory can be mistaken for a crystal's own attack-relevant benefit. Parsed the actual compiled `packs/lightsaber-accessories.db` directly (10 records, not inferred from a reference file): none carry an `ATTACK_BONUS` or `CONDITIONAL_ATTACK` type today. A new regression test (`tests/lightsaber-accessory-attack-provenance-safety.test.mjs`) locks this down as a trip-wire — if a future accessory record gains one of these types, this test fails immediately rather than the change silently altering attack math. No `installedUpgrades` path was reintroduced.

### Independently confirmed (no code change): `system.selectedChoice` persistence contract

The review's claim that `talent-step.js` is the real progression writer of the choice-persistence contract this round's (and round 6's) Greater Weapon Focus proof relies on was checked directly: `talent-step.js`'s talent-acquisition commit path does write `nextSelection.system.selectedChoice = selectedChoice` after choice-legality validation. This corroborates, independently, the `system.selectedChoice` contract already exercised end-to-end by `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 7 — no code or test change was needed, since the golden tests already proved this contract directly rather than merely asserting it.

### Required test matrix

`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` Section 5b was substantially rewritten: a 10-item Standard Kyber/attunement matrix (builtBy+attunedBy present/absent/wrong-wielder; Ilum/Mephite/Standard Synthetic/Kathracite each proven exactly +1, explicitly asserted NOT +2; a mutation test — attunedBy flips from null to the rolling actor and the very next resolution reaches +1; Sigil, attuned, proven to NOT regain the generic +1; every negative-crystal record re-proven zero for an ATTUNED creator specifically, not merely because attunement was absent; a fully-unattuned crystal-bearing saber proven zero); every interpreter-contract fixture (bonusType/alias-normalization tests) updated to be attuned, since the gate is now universal. Section 8b's Hurikane/Heart matrices were rewritten to the 10-item lists above (Hurikane: positive-vs-zero Reflex bonus, the real Cortosis Gauntlet by name, Energy Shield exclusion, alternate equip flags, a mutation test, non-attunement; Heart: all four equipped×activated combinations, two independent mutation tests — deactivating and unequipping — an alternate wield field, no cross-target leakage, non-attunement). Section 8c's Force-stacking matrix (A–H) was updated to use attuned crystal fixtures with a deliberately-unattuned baseline reference point, so its cross-type-stacking assertions stay isolated from the separate attunement question. `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` sections 3–4 were rewritten: section 3 now proves a real crystal's `ATTACK_BONUS` record produces exactly one modifier for its attuned creator (not a second generic Attuned bonus alongside it — the fail-before proof for Blocker 1's double-count); section 4 (new) proves the IDENTICAL record produces nothing for a non-attuned wielder.

### Required validation

`tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`: still 34 assertion blocks (Section 5b/8b/8c rewritten in place, no new top-level sections), all pass. `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6, sections 3–4 rewritten), `tests/lightsaber-accessory-attack-provenance-safety.test.mjs` (new), `tests/combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `tests/armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `tests/stock-droid-attack-math.test.mjs`, `tests/vehicle-attack-routing-guard-check.test.mjs`, `tests/attack-outcome-wiring.test.mjs`, `tests/attack-transaction-rollback.test.mjs`, `tests/phase3-reroll-supersession.test.mjs`, `tests/character-actor-inventory-index-and-vehicle-skip.test.mjs`, `tests/legacy-equip-state-single-pass-authority.test.mjs` all re-run green. Full rolling test suite, `check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2502/2502 files), `validate-data.js`, `verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` parse checks all green (exact rolling-suite pass count in the PR handoff).

- STATIC ATTACK MODIFIER COMPOSITION: **CERTIFIED** — the standard/self-built lightsaber crystal is now attunement-gated end to end and no longer double-counted against a real crystal's own `ATTACK_BONUS` record; the generator's virtual baseline crystal is distinguished from a real named crystal by identity, not an incidental empty array; accessory-provenance safety is locked down by a regression test. No newly-discovered static source remains open.

- ATTACK DIALOG/ROLL/CHAT PARITY: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged this round)

**Sequencing update (established alongside this round, not yet started):** the reviewer separately identified a consumer-layer architectural defect — the shared roll dialog's hardcoded "Quick Toggles" (Aim/Charge/Flanking/Higher Ground/Point Blank) render for nearly every roll type, not just attacks (contaminating Skill/Force/Use the Force dialogs with attack-only arithmetic in their live-preview fallback), Higher Ground remains an automated UI control despite this project's own source comments calling it an unverified rule, and dozens of already-implemented `ATTACK_OPTION`-metadata feats/talents are not consistently surfaced through `CombatOptionResolver.getAvailableAttackOptions()`/`summarizeAttackOptions()` while a parallel set of hand-coded checkboxes exists for others. The reviewer explicitly directed this be treated as **Round 8** ("Attack Dialog Context + Dynamic Option Architecture"), sequenced on this same PR AFTER this round (7) passes independent review and BEFORE Round 9 (NPC statblock flat-composition, the domain's remaining certification gate) — explicitly not mixed into this round to keep it surgical. ## Domain: Attack Bonus — round 8 (Attack Dialog Context + Dynamic Combat Option Presentation Authority)

Round 7 (`c4c3fcf`) was pushed and the reviewer's next messages elaborated the Round 8 rationale in detail (repo-verified counts of 136 total `ATTACK_OPTION` rules across feats/talents) but, at the point this round began, had not yet returned a formal accept/hold verdict on `c4c3fcf` itself in the established "I reviewed SHA X directly, CI is green" format. Rather than assume a verdict, this was raised explicitly and the reviewer confirmed: begin Round 8 now, ahead of a separate Round 7 verdict. This round proceeds on that explicit authorization; Round 7's own certification status above is unchanged and still awaits its own review.

This round is a **UI-authority and domain-isolation audit**, not a feat/talent implementation project — per the reviewer's own explicit instruction, no attempt was made to make every `ATTACK_OPTION` record "work" end-to-end; the goal was (a) stop attack-only controls from rendering outside attack rolls, (b) replace ad hoc hardcoded checkboxes with the existing `CombatOptionResolver` metadata pipeline wherever a real record already exists, and (c) make the presentation state model honest about what an owned-but-gated option's UI state actually is.

### Attack-context authority: `CombatOptionResolver.getAvailableAttackContexts()`

New static method (`scripts/engine/combat/combat-option-resolver.js`) deciding which of the four generic, non-feat-specific attack-context toggles (Aim, Charge, Flanking, Point Blank Range) are relevant to even show for a given actor/weapon. The base rule is the ordinary melee/ranged split (Aim and Point Blank are ranged-only; Flanking is melee-only), **except** Charge: the ordinary melee Charge +2 stays melee-only, but "is Charge context meaningful right now" is a different question from "does the flat melee bonus apply" — a ranged attacker who owns a `requiresCharge`-gated ranged option (e.g. Charging Fire) must still see the Charge toggle, because checking it changes what that option's gate evaluates to. This is detected generically: `getAvailableAttackContexts()` probes `getAttackOptionsWithState()` (charge gate assumed satisfiable) and checks whether *any* reachable option declares `requiresCharge === true` for a non-melee attack type — never by name-matching "Charging Fire" specifically, so any future ranged-charge option is picked up the same way without a code change.

### Dynamic option-state model: `CombatOptionResolver.getAttackOptionsWithState()`

New static method solving the "owned-but-gated option is indistinguishable from not-owned" problem named in the round's Part 5. `getAvailableAttackOptions()` (pre-existing, unchanged, still the sole authority `collectAttackModifiers()` — the real roll math — consumes) excludes an option entirely the instant *any* gate fails, toggleable or not. `getAttackOptionsWithState()` calls it twice: once with the real context, once with a probe context that force-satisfies only the three player-toggleable gates (`aim`, `charge`, `autofire`) — never the weapon/attack-type/target/maneuver gates, so an option genuinely wrong for this weapon or attack type still never appears, satisfying "not owned / can never apply here → do not show it at all." Diffing the two runs yields, per option: `state: 'available'` (real gates all pass), `state: 'disabled'` with a human `reason` naming exactly which toggleable gate is still unmet (e.g. `"Requires Aim"`), or omission entirely (a non-toggleable gate failed — weapon group, attack type, etc.). This is a presentation-only addition; it does not touch `collectAttackModifiers()`'s resolution path at all.

### `ATTACK_OPTION` record coverage (Part 11)

Produced programmatically by a new reusable tool, `tools/report-attack-option-coverage.mjs`, which parses `packs/feats.db`/`packs/talents.db` directly and classifies every `type: "ATTACK_OPTION"` rule by its actual `control` field and gate shape (mirroring `CombatOptionResolver`'s own id-derivation and gate logic exactly, not an invented scheme). Output: `docs/audits/generated/attack-option-coverage-report.{md,json}`.

**136 total records** (88 feats, 48 talents):

| Classification | Count | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| SELECTABLE | 56 | Toggle/flag control, no unmet non-toggleable gate — renders as a live checkbox in the current attack dialog today |
| PASSIVE | 33 | `control: 'passive'` — a real, always-on modifier `CombatOptionResolver` surfaces; never a player checkbox |
| CONTEXT-GATED SELECTABLE | 17 | Toggle/flag gated on Aim/Charge/Autofire — the dialog *can* satisfy this gate; renders disabled-with-reason until it does (Careful Shot, Charging Fire, Powerful Charge, Burst Fire, Deadeye, Strafe, Autofire Sweep/Assault, Spray Shot, Improved/Maniacal Charge, Flèche, Aiming Accuracy, Steadying Position, Hunter's Mark, Precision Fire, Takedown) |
| SLIDER/VALUE | 3 | Numeric slider control (Power Attack, Power Blast, Melee Defense) |
| METADATA PRESENT BUT RUNTIME INCOMPLETE | 27 | A real `ATTACK_OPTION` record whose gate (`requiresManeuver`, `requiresTargetFeat/Talent/Item/Text/Type/FlatFooted/DeniedDexBonus`, `requiresOpportunityAttack`, `requiresAreaAttack`, or `requiresOption`) depends on context the live attack dialog never supplies (target state, maneuver selection, opportunity-attack framing, area-attack framing, or another option's live value) — `CombatOptionResolver`'s gate logic for these is not wrong, but no invocation of *this* dialog can ever resolve one of these to "available" today. Full list in the generated report. |
| NOT IMPLEMENTED | 0 | No usable id, or an unrecognized control type |

This report is an inventory, not a certification that all 106 SELECTABLE+PASSIVE+CONTEXT-GATED+SLIDER records are wired to every downstream consumer (damage packets, chat cards, AI); that is explicitly out of this round's scope per the reviewer's own instruction.

### Hardcoded checkboxes removed (confirmed duplicates, verified against `packs/feats.db`)

- `attackOptions.burstFire` — duplicates the real `option: 'burstFire'` record (Burst Fire feat).
- `attackOptions.rapidShot` — duplicates the real `option: 'rapidShot'` record (Rapid Shot feat).
- `attackOptions.powerAttack` — duplicates the real `option: 'powerAttack'` slider record (Power Attack feat).
- `attackOptions.flurry` — duplicated real records under TWO different option ids (Flurry: `option: 'flurry'`, `requiresWeaponGroups: light/lightsaber`; Rapid Strike: `option: 'rapidStrike'`, no weapon-group restriction) and was itself bugged: it always submitted as `attackOptions.flurry` regardless of which the actor actually owned, and bypassed `optionAllowedForWeapon()`'s weapon-group gate entirely.
- Higher Ground quick toggle — removed per Part 2. This project's own source comments already called it an unverified/non-automated rule; no SWSE source text or existing project authority was ever found to justify the automated +1 it granted. Its removal is documented in source (`ROLL_MODIFIERS.situational`'s comment block) rather than silently deleted, per the instruction not to erase historical documentation.

**Retained, not duplicates:** Double Strike (`attackOptions.doubleStrike`) and Triple Attack/Strike (`attackOptions.tripleStrike`) — confirmed there is no `ATTACK_OPTION`-metadata equivalent; both belong to a separate multiattack-sequencing subsystem (`combat-feature-handlers.js`) untouched by this round.

### Newly-discovered conflict, fixed: a stale DOM hotfix was undoing the Charging Fire exception

Repo-search for other places attack-only context leaks outside its domain (Part 1's explicit instruction) found `scripts/patches/attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js`, a live-registered post-render DOM patch (`registerAttackDialogCombatCorrectionsHotfix()`, wired in `runtime-bugfix-hotfixes-init.js`). Its `patchRangedOnlyRules()` unconditionally removed the `charging`/`flanking` DOM inputs from any form whose text "looks ranged" (`formLooksRanged()`), with no awareness of an actor's owned options. Before this round that was harmless (Charge/Flanking rendered on every attack form regardless of melee/ranged, so blind removal was the ONLY ranged-side gate). After this round's template change — Charge now correctly renders for a ranged attacker who owns a `requiresCharge` ranged option, per `attackContexts.charge` — this hotfix would have torn that exact checkbox back out of the live DOM the instant it ran, silently defeating the Charging Fire exception this round exists to add. Fixed by deleting the two blind `removeNamedInput(form, 'charging'|'flanking')` calls from `patchRangedOnlyRules()`; the function's Aim-relabeling and Brace-Autofire-toggle responsibilities (unrelated to Charge/Flanking) are unchanged. This is browser-DOM code (`document`/`MutationObserver`) and is not exercised by the Node test harness; it was verified by direct source reading and `node --check`, not an automated test — noted honestly rather than claimed as test-covered.

Also reviewed and found **not** a conflict: `scripts/patches/combat-ui-behavior-hotfix.js`'s chat-button "quick action" launchers (Power Attack, Flurry, Burst Fire, Charging Fire, Powerful Charge, etc.) — these call `showRollModifiersDialog({ rollType: 'attack', actor, weapon, combatOptions, attackOptions })`, i.e. they route into the SAME shared dialog with a preset seed rather than rendering any competing UI, so there is no duplicate control surface. One pre-existing, unrelated gap was noticed in passing: `buildRollConfigModel()`'s `combatOptions`/`attackContexts` construction only passes `{ attackType }` as context, not the caller's seed `combatOptions`/`attackOptions` — so a quick-action launch does not visually pre-check its dynamic option card on first render (the option still activates correctly once the form is actually submitted, since submission reads live form/seed state, not the initial render). This predates Round 8 (verified against the pre-round-8 `summarizeAttackOptions()` call, which had the identical narrow context) and is out of this round's scope; flagged here rather than silently left undocumented.

### Test matrix (Part 10) — `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority.test.mjs` (new, 10 sections, all passing)

1. **Domain isolation**: skill/force/force-power/initiative/ability roll models all carry `isAttackRoll: false`, an empty `combatOptions` array, and all-`false` `attackContexts` — proven directly via `buildRollConfigModel()`, not inferred from the template.
2. **Non-attack preview contamination removed**: source-text guard confirms the stale `for (const name of ['aiming','charging','flanking','higherGround','pointBlank'])` loop is gone; the non-attack preview total is exactly `base + custom`.
3. **Higher Ground removal**: absent from the template checkbox, the submit handler's `data.get()` reads, and `attackContexts`; removal documented in source (regex-verified).
4. **Melee/ranged visibility**: melee weapon → Flanking/Charge shown, Aim/Point Blank hidden; ranged weapon with no ranged-charge capability owned → Aim/Point Blank shown, Flanking/Charge hidden.
5. **Charging Fire exception**: a ranged attacker who owns Charging Fire sees Charge context (detected via `requiresCharge`, not by feat name) without gaining Flanking or the melee-only flat Charge bonus.
6. **Careful Shot (Aim-gated)**: shown `disabled`/`"Requires Aim"` before Aim is checked, `available` after; absent entirely (not merely disabled) for a non-owner or on a melee attack.
7. **Powerful Charge (Charge-gated)**: `disabled`/`"Requires Charge"` before Charge is checked, `available` after.
8. **Burst Fire (autofire-gated)**: `disabled`/`"Requires an autofire-capable weapon or autofire mode"` before autofire context is set, `available` after; absent on a melee attack and for a non-owner even with autofire context set.
9. **Duplicate-checkbox removal**: source-text guards confirm all four hardcoded duplicates are gone and Double Strike/Triple Strike remain; the dynamic `combatOptions` list is confirmed as the live replacement.
10. **Preview/roll/chat parity**: `computeFinalAttackComposition()` — the exact seam the dialog preview, the real roll, and the chat ledger all share — adds exactly `+1` when Careful Shot is selected via `combatOptions.carefulShot` with `aim: true`, and adds nothing when Aim alone is set without selecting the option.

### Required validation

`node --check` on all changed files. `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority.test.mjs`: 10/10 sections pass. `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (34 assertion blocks), `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6), `tests/lightsaber-accessory-attack-provenance-safety.test.mjs` re-run green (full validation-suite results recorded below once the full rolling suite is re-run for this round).

### Updated certification status (this round — reported separately, per the reviewer's explicit instruction not to merge these into one vague "UI certified" line)

- **ATTACK dialog option completeness: NOT CERTIFIED (unchanged claim, now evidence-backed)** — this round never claimed full completeness. 106 of 136 `ATTACK_OPTION` records (SELECTABLE/PASSIVE/CONTEXT-GATED/SLIDER) are reachable through the current dialog; 27 (METADATA PRESENT BUT RUNTIME INCOMPLETE) depend on context (target state, maneuver, opportunity-attack, area-attack, another option) this dialog does not supply and remain out of reach from here specifically. See the coverage report for the full per-record list.
- **SHARED roll-config domain isolation: CERTIFIED** — attack-context controls (Aim/Charge/Flanking/Point Blank, the "Your Attack Options" panel) are now structurally absent, not merely inert, from every non-attack roll type; the non-attack preview fallback can no longer read attack-context fields at all; the one live DOM hotfix capable of undoing part of this (the ranged charging/flanking strip) was found and fixed.
- **SKILL/FORCE dialog preview parity: CERTIFIED** — proven directly (Section 1/2 of the new suite): a Skill/Force/Force-Power/Ability roll's model, preview total, and breakdown are provably unaffected by any attack-context state, since that state is never computed or read for those roll types at all.
- **ATTACK dialog dynamic option presentation: CERTIFIED for the toggleable-gate model** — an owned option blocked only by Aim/Charge/Autofire now renders disabled-with-reason instead of vanishing; an unowned or attack-type/weapon-mismatched option still never renders at all, in either state.
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: still NOT CERTIFIED** — this round is presentation-layer only and does not touch the NPC statblock sub-domain, the domain's one remaining certification gate (per round 7).

## Domain: Attack Bonus — round 8 correction #1 (independent review held round 8 on four production blockers plus one audit inconsistency)

An independent review of round 8 (head `dca4a91674c79a73b7aa50780accb379587267cc`) accepted the round's general direction in full — domain isolation, the four confirmed-duplicate hardcoded checkboxes' removal, Aim/Charge dynamic gating, the Charging Fire exception, Higher Ground's removal, and the ranged-hotfix fix — but held certification on dynamic option presentation specifically, citing three production blockers and one audit-tool inconsistency, all confirmed by direct source/data inspection before any fix was made.

### Explicit correction to round 8's own certification claims

Round 8 stated "ATTACK dialog dynamic option presentation: CERTIFIED for the toggleable-gate model" and reported a 106/136 reachable count. Both are corrected here, not silently rewritten: the presentation model was built on `CombatOptionResolver.getFeatRules()`, which (Blocker 1 below) admitted non-`ATTACK_OPTION` records into the same pipeline the certification was proven against — the certification's own foundation was unverified at the time it was written. The 106/136 count was also arithmetically wrong (its own displayed buckets summed to 109, not 106) — flagged directly by the reviewer as reason alone to distrust the report pending correction. Neither the domain-isolation nor the duplicate-removal certifications are affected; both were proven independently of `getFeatRules()`'s defect.

### Blocker 1 — `getFeatRules()` admitted non-`ATTACK_OPTION` rules into the attack-option pipeline

Confirmed directly: `pushRule()` accepted `rule.type === 'ATTACK_OPTION' || rule.option || rule.id` — any `abilityMeta.rules` entry carrying an `option` or `id` field, regardless of its own declared type. Scanning the actual compiled packs found 180+ non-`ATTACK_OPTION` rules that also carry an `id` for their own unrelated purposes: 90 `RUNTIME_CONTEXT_REFERENCE`, 44 `TALENT_RULE`, plus `HIT_RIDER`, `CRITICAL_RIDER`, `DEFENSE_BONUS`, `COVER_CONTEXT`, and more. Concretely, Oath of Duty (`RUNTIME_CONTEXT_REFERENCE`), Force Warning and Healing Boost (`TALENT_RULE`) were all one card render away from appearing as "Your Attack Options" checkboxes.

**Fix**: `getFeatRules()` is replaced by an exported `extractAttackOptionRules()` (`scripts/engine/combat/combat-option-resolver.js`) that requires `type === 'ATTACK_OPTION'` strictly for every `abilityMeta.rules` entry. The two dedicated, single-purpose compatibility slots (`abilityMeta.primitives[]`, `abilityMeta.attackOption`) remain supported — no shipped record uses either today, confirmed against every pack — but a primitive still requires its own explicit type, and the singular `attackOption` slot's presence is itself the declaration (never a mixed-type collection), normalized to carry the type explicitly and rejected if it declares a conflicting one.

### Blocker 2 — the coverage-report tool scanned a different population than production, with an arithmetic error on top

The tool's own `type === 'ATTACK_OPTION'`-only scan was, by coincidence, the SAFER of the two populations — but it could never prove it matched what `CombatOptionResolver.getAvailableAttackOptions()` actually consumed, since the two were independently implemented. Its four displayed buckets (56 selectable + 33 passive + 17 context-gated + 3 slider = 109) also did not sum to its claimed "106 reachable."

**Fix**: `tools/report-attack-option-coverage.mjs` now imports `extractAttackOptionRules()` directly from `combat-option-resolver.js` rather than re-parsing packs itself — the report and the production resolver are now provably the same population by construction, not by coincidence. Every total in the report is derived live from the records array (`bucketSum === all.length` is asserted at generation time and throws if it ever disagrees), so the round 8 arithmetic error cannot recur silently.

### Blocker 3 — target-gated options were never actually unreachable; the dialog just never threaded its own target selection into them

Round 8's report classified every `requiresTargetType`/`requiresTargetFeat`/`requiresTargetTalent`/`requiresTargetItem`/`requiresTargetText`/`requiresTargetFlatFooted`/`requiresTargetDeniedDexBonus` option as structurally unreachable ("the dialog never supplies target context"). Confirmed false: the dialog's own "Target Context" panel (`buildTargetPanel()`) already offers Selected Token / Combatant selection, the submit handler already builds a `targetContext` from it, and `CombatOptionResolver.optionAllowedForWeapon()` already implements every one of these gates correctly — `buildRollConfigModel()`'s option-presentation calls simply never passed a resolved target into the context at all (`{ attackType }` only), and the live-preview `update()` function never read the Target Context panel's fields into its own `rollOptions` either (a genuine preview/roll parity gap independent of option presentation, also fixed here).

**Fix**: `combat-option-resolver.js` gains an internal-only `__probeDiscoveryGates` flag (set solely by `getAttackOptionsWithState()`'s existing probe pass, never by a real caller) that lets the seven target gates and `requiresOption` be probe-satisfied for discovery, exactly as Aim/Charge/Autofire already were — so a target-gated option blocked only by an absent/non-qualifying target is now found and given a truthful reason ("Requires a target" vs. "Target does not meet this option's requirement") instead of silently vanishing. `roll-config.js` now resolves the actual target actor via `getTargetActorFromOptions()` — the SAME shared authority the real roll and the submit-time `targetContext` already used (`combat-roll-math.js`), never a second target resolver — and threads it into both `buildRollConfigModel()`'s initial model build (so a token already targeted before the dialog opens is honored on first paint, via that function's own existing `game.user.targets` fallback) and the live `update()`/`rebuildAttackOptionsPanel()` recompute (so changing Selected Token/Combatant re-evaluates option state immediately, via the same form-level `input`/`change` listener that already drives every other live recompute — no new event wiring was needed). `update()`'s `rollOptions` also now carries `targetTokenId`/`targetActorId`, closing the preview/roll target-resolution parity gap for the numeric composition itself (Heart of the Guardian/Hurikane's live preview), not just the option cards.

Concrete effect: Droid Hunter, Jedi Hunter, Cunning Attack, and Sucker Punch move from the old report's "unreachable" bucket to the corrected report's `TARGET_GATED` bucket (8 records total) — reachable, conditionally, on whichever target is selected, exactly as the reviewer described.

### Blocker 4 — Point Blank had two independently-settable authorities for the same fact

Confirmed: the ranged dialog rendered BOTH a Range Band selector (`name="rangeBand"`, with its own `"pointBlank"` option value) AND a separate `name="pointBlank"` checkbox, both feeding the submitted result independently (`rangeBand: data.get('rangeBand')` and `isPointBlank` from the checkbox). A player could produce `rangeBand: 'medium'` with the checkbox still checked — an impossible real-world state — and `ScopedCombatFeatResolver`'s Point Blank Shot gate (`isPointBlankContext()`, which already also independently checks the range band string) would apply the feat's +1 based on the checkbox alone in that case, disagreeing with the selected range.

**Fix**: the checkbox is deleted from the template entirely. `isPointBlank` is now derived in exactly one place, `computeAttackSituationalContext(form, melee)` — the single function both the submit handler and the live preview already shared — via `form.querySelector('[name="rangeBand"]')?.value === 'pointBlank'` (the Range Band select's fixed, closed value space; correctly always false for melee, where the field does not render at all). `getAvailableAttackContexts()` no longer returns a `pointBlank` key at all — Point Blank was never a generic player-toggleable context distinct from the range itself, and is not re-added as one; the reference-only `ROLL_MODIFIERS.situational.pointBlank` label is updated to document the derivation. The backward-compatible `result.situational.pointBlank` field is kept (for any external caller still reading that legacy shape) but now derives from `rangeBand` too, rather than reading a field that no longer exists in the form.

### Option-state model: target and `requiresOption` reasons added (supports Blocker 3)

`unmetToggleableReasons()` now also explains a blocked target gate ("Requires a target" with none selected; "Target does not meet this option's requirement" with one selected that doesn't qualify) and a blocked `requiresOption` gate ("Requires `<id>` to be selected first") — both resolvable from this same dialog's own controls, so both belong in the disabled-with-reason model alongside Aim/Charge/Autofire, not the permanently-unreachable category. `requiresManeuver` and `requiresOpportunityAttack` remain deliberately un-probed and unreasoned — this dialog has no maneuver selector or opportunity-attack/reaction framing at all, so an option gated on either is genuinely, not just presentationally, unreachable from here; exposing it with a checkbox would misrepresent a reaction-only mechanic as an ordinary attack option, which the reviewer explicitly warned against.

### Corrected coverage report

Regenerated via `tools/report-attack-option-coverage.mjs` (now importing `extractAttackOptionRules()` directly): **136 total records** (88 feats, 48 talents; unchanged, confirming Blocker 1's fix did not change what counts as a genuine `ATTACK_OPTION` record, only what leaks in alongside it) —

| Classification | Count |
|---|---:|
| SELECTABLE | 58 |
| PASSIVE | 37 |
| CONTEXT_GATED_SELECTABLE (Aim/Charge/Autofire/Range Band/`requiresOption`) | 20 |
| SLIDER | 3 |
| TARGET_GATED (reachable once a target is selected — Blocker 3) | 8 |
| EXTERNAL_WORKFLOW_GATED (`requiresManeuver`/`requiresOpportunityAttack` — genuinely unreachable from this dialog) | 10 |
| RUNTIME_INCOMPLETE | 0 |
| INVALID_NON_ATTACK_RULE | 0 |

**Reachable from the current attack dialog today (SELECTABLE + PASSIVE + CONTEXT_GATED_SELECTABLE + SLIDER): 118/136** — up from round 8's (arithmetically wrong) claim of 106, both because `requiresAreaAttack`/`requiresOption`/`requiresRangeBand`-gated records were previously miscounted as unreachable (they are weapon-derived or dialog-resolvable, not context gaps) and because the bucket-sum invariant is now enforced at generation time. The 8 `TARGET_GATED` and 10 `EXTERNAL_WORKFLOW_GATED` records are reported separately rather than folded into either "reachable" or a single vague "incomplete" bucket, per the reviewer's explicit classification requirement.

Two gaps discovered in passing but explicitly left unfixed as out of this correction's scope (neither was part of the reviewer's four blockers, and fixing them changes real roll-math gating behavior rather than presentation): three records (`Overwhelming Attack`, `Critical Strike`, `Mighty Swing`) declare `requiresSwiftActions` and one (`Autofire Assault`) declares `excludesOptions`, neither of which `optionAllowedForWeapon()` currently enforces at all — both are therefore effectively unconditional today in the real resolver, which this report's classification does not misrepresent (it reports what the resolver actually gates on) but which is worth a future round's attention.

### Test matrix

New `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority-correction.test.mjs` (7 sections, all passing): rule-type isolation using real Oath of Duty/Force Warning/Healing Boost/Careful Shot record shapes (Blocker 1); a source-guard plus cross-check proving the coverage tool and production resolver share one extraction function (Blocker 2); Droid Hunter and Jedi Hunter's full disabled/no-target → disabled/non-qualifying-target → available progression, plus a `buildRollConfigModel()` wiring proof using `game.user.targets` (Blocker 3, 3 sub-sections); a source-guard proving no `pointBlank` field exists anywhere in `roll-config.js` plus a live `resolveAttackBonus()` proof that Point Blank Shot's +1 applies only at Point Blank range (Blocker 4, 2 sub-sections). `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority.test.mjs`'s three assertions that named the removed `pointBlank` context key are updated in place (still 10/10 passing). `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs`'s `computeAttackSituationalContext()` toggle-matrix fixture (`fakeForm`) is extended to simulate a select's `.value` (not just a checkbox's `.checked`) for the Range Band field, and its one fixture missing an explicit `type: 'ATTACK_OPTION'` (relying on the very fallback Blocker 1 removed) is corrected — both are pre-existing test-fixture debt this correction's stricter extraction exposed, not new regressions.

### Required validation

All of the above plus every previously-certified test file re-run green: `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority.test.mjs` (10/10), `tests/attack-dialog-context-authority-correction.test.mjs` (7/7, new), `tests/attack-bonus-math-integrity.test.mjs` (34/34), `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` (6/6), `tests/lightsaber-accessory-attack-provenance-safety.test.mjs`, `tests/combat-feat-attack-modifier-regression.test.mjs`, `tests/stock-droid-damage-math.test.mjs`. Full validation-suite results recorded in the PR handoff for this correction.

### Updated certification status (this round)

- SHARED roll-config domain isolation: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged)
- SKILL/FORCE dialog preview parity: **CERTIFIED** (unchanged)
- ATTACK OPTION DISCOVERY (which records CAN enter the pipeline at all): **CERTIFIED** — `extractAttackOptionRules()` is strictly `type === 'ATTACK_OPTION'`-gated; proven against real leaking records (Oath of Duty, Force Warning, Healing Boost) that a prior pass would have missed.
- ATTACK OPTION COVERAGE REPORT: **CERTIFIED** — the tool and production resolver are now provably the same extraction population by shared function, not independent implementations; every total is derived from records, with a self-check against silent arithmetic drift.
- ATTACK dialog dynamic option presentation (target-aware model): **CERTIFIED** — an owned option blocked only by Aim/Charge/Autofire/Range Band/another option/target state now renders disabled-with-reason; a maneuver- or opportunity-attack-gated option correctly remains entirely absent, not misrepresented as a normal checkbox.
- POINT-BLANK CONTEXT: **CERTIFIED** — one authority (Range Band); the impossible dual-authority state can no longer be produced by this dialog.
- **ATTACK dialog option completeness: still NOT CERTIFIED (unchanged claim)** — 118/136 records reachable from this dialog; 10 `EXTERNAL_WORKFLOW_GATED` records remain genuinely out of reach (no maneuver/reaction framing exists here) and are not claimed otherwise.
- **FULL ATTACK BONUS DOMAIN: still NOT CERTIFIED** — presentation-layer only; the NPC statblock sub-domain (round 7's remaining gate) is untouched.

Per the reviewer's explicit instruction: this correction does not begin NPC-flat math and does not begin the Damage domain.

### Addendum — Action Authority groundwork (explicitly groundwork-only, not a migration)

Alongside the four blockers above, the reviewer separately requested laying groundwork for a future scalable "Action Authority" capable of eventually ingesting hundreds of SWSE actions/feats/talents/reactions/Force abilities — explicitly to establish clean SSOT boundaries (`PrerequisiteChecker` = may acquire; a new `ActorActionResolver` = does the actor own it; a new `ActionAvailabilityEngine` = can it be used right now, and why/why not; `ActionEngine` = action-economy cost, unchanged) before `CombatOptionResolver` keeps absorbing every new responsibility. Explicitly NOT a migration of the 136 `ATTACK_OPTION` records, NOT a `CombatOptionResolver` rewrite, and NOT a rewiring of the live attack dialog.

Added: `scripts/engine/actions/action-definition.js` (versioned `ActionDefinition` schema, domain/state/predicate vocabularies), `action-definition-normalizer.js` (pure `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` adapter from the strict `type: 'ATTACK_OPTION'` shape, reusing `extractAttackOptionRules()` rather than a second parser), `action-registry.js` (deterministic `ActionRegistry`, duplicate-id-safe), `actor-action-resolver.js` (`ActorActionResolver.getOwnedActions()` — entitlement only, no availability evaluation), `action-availability-engine.js` (`ActionAvailabilityEngine.evaluate()` — a composable `all`/`any`/`not` requirement tree over a small reusable predicate vocabulary, distinguishing `hidden` (structural weapon/attack-type mismatch) from `disabled` (a player-actionable context/target gate) from `external-workflow` (a maneuver/opportunity-attack gate this dialog has no control for at all, never silently dropped) from `available`/`passive`, failing closed on any unrecognized predicate type). Full design record, explicit SSOT boundaries, and the complete non-goals list: `docs/architecture/action-authority-v1.md`.

Proven, not just asserted: `tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` (16 required proofs across 11 assertion blocks) against nine representative real record classes (Power Attack, Rapid Shot, Careful Shot, Powerful Charge, Droid Hunter, a rejected non-`ATTACK_OPTION` record), including a direct parity cross-check showing this groundwork engine's available/not-available verdict agrees with the live, certified `CombatOptionResolver.getAttackOptionsWithState()` for identical actor/weapon/context inputs — demonstrating the new layer *could* eventually replace the dialog's consumption without any per-feat UI knowledge, without actually rewiring anything in this round. `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()` remains the sole, unchanged, certified authority for real attack-modifier roll math; `scripts/rolls/roll-config.js` is not touched by this addendum.

This groundwork does not change any certification status above — it introduces no new production code path any live roll consumes.

### Addendum correction #2 — the groundwork's own contracts were hardened against four blockers (still groundwork-only)

An independent review of the Action Authority groundwork addendum (head `fdf3122`) accepted round 8 correction #1's four production fixes in full — CI confirmed green, mergeable — but held certification on the groundwork's own internal contracts, citing four blockers, all confirmed by direct inspection against the real 136-record dataset before any fix was made. As with every prior correction, this round is a hardening of a layer no production code path consumes yet — `CombatOptionResolver` was not rewired, the attack dialog was not touched, and neither `PrerequisiteChecker` nor `ActionEngine` were altered.

**Blocker 1 (most severe) — the v1 normalizer was lossy: an omitted requirement is fail-open.** `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` translated only a subset of the 24 real `requires*`/`excludes*` gate fields that real `ATTACK_OPTION` records use, silently dropping the rest — a definition missing a requirement its source record actually declares would evaluate as unconditionally available, the single worst failure mode this whole freeze exists to prevent. **Fix**: every one of the 24 fields is now classified NORMALIZED / EXTERNAL-WORKFLOW / UNSUPPORTED in a closed inventory (`ATTACK_OPTION_GATE_FIELD_DISPOSITION`, `action-definition.js`), enforced by a schema guard — `validateAttackOptionNormalization()` (`action-definition-normalizer.js`) — that throws on any gate field present on a rule but not recognized, or recognized but not actually translated into a requirement leaf (`collectSourceFields()` tree-walks the built requirements and checks true value-level coverage, not merely that a field name appears on an allowlist). `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` now calls this guard automatically — it cannot be bypassed by a future call site. Proven table-driven across the FULL real 136-record dataset (`tests/action-authority-groundwork-normalization-audit.test.mjs`, generating `docs/audits/generated/action-authority-normalization-audit-report.{md,json}`), not a sample, plus a mutation test proving a hypothetical future unknown field (`requiresMountedCombat`) is caught and fails loudly rather than silently passing through.

**Blocker 2 — `ActionRegistry` was keyed by bare `id`, contradicting the schema's own "unique within a domain" documentation.** Two different domains could legitimately want the same short id (e.g. a `force` and an `attack` action both named `push`), which the bare-id registry would have collided as a duplicate-registration error. **Fix**: `ActionRegistry` is now keyed by the composite `${domain}:${id}`, with a `get(domain, id)` API replacing the old bare-id lookup.

**Blocker 3 — `ActorActionResolver` embedded a specific granting item's provenance directly into each `ActionDefinition`, so two different sources granting the same logical action would collide as a duplicate-definition registry error.** Confirmed: nothing in the real dataset does this today, but the design itself was wrong — "what the action is" and "why this actor has it" are different questions with different cardinalities (one definition, many possible grants). **Fix**: `ActionDefinition` (what the action IS — one canonical object per `domain:id`) is now separated from a new `ActionEntitlement` (why THIS actor has it — one per granting source item, carrying `actionKey`, `actorId`, and the granting item's own `source` provenance). `ActorActionResolver.getOwnedActions()` now returns `{ definitions, entitlements }`: each unique `domain:id` definition is registered at most once, while an actor with the same action granted by two different items produces two entitlements pointing at the one definition. No persistent entitlement database — computed fresh from the actor's current items on every call, exactly as the original groundwork's own no-actor-mutation constraint required.

**Blocker 4 — `ActionAvailabilityEngine` was reimplementing fuzzy text-matching logic (`weaponText()`, `targetOwnsItem()`, and others) instead of delegating to `CombatOptionResolver`'s already-certified equivalents — a drift risk, not yet a divergence.** **Fix**: the certified helpers (`normalizeKey`, `getAttackType`, `normalizeRangeBand`, `getRangeBand`, `contextManeuver`, `weaponText`, `weaponDamageText`, `isVehicleWeapon`, `weaponMatchesGroup`, `textMatchesAny`, `isUnarmedWeapon`, `isAreaAttackContext`, `targetText`, `targetHasOwnedItem`, `weaponSupportsAutofire`, `actorItems`, `getSelectedChoiceValues`, `weaponMatchesSelectedChoice`, `actorHasFeatSelectedChoiceMatchingWeapon`) are extracted verbatim into a new neutral shared module, `scripts/engine/combat/weapon-target-gate-classifiers.js`; both `combat-option-resolver.js` and `action-availability-engine.js` now import from it — a pure refactor with zero behavior change on the production side, verified by the full pre-existing test suite re-passing unchanged. While hardening this path, two further defects were fixed alongside it: the `attackType` predicate previously auto-passed when the type couldn't be resolved ("unknown") — now fails closed, consistent with the rest of this freeze's fail-closed standard; and `evaluateNode()`'s boolean-tree evaluation (`all`/`any`/`not`) previously could short-circuit, losing the provenance of branches it never reached — it now fully evaluates every branch via `.map()` before applying `.every`/`.some`, and a failing `not`-node synthesizes a truthful, non-null reason (`EXCLUDE_REASON_BY_TYPE`) rather than a blank one.

### Test matrix (correction #2)

`tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` extended: domain-qualified registry identity assertions (Blocker 2), a new definition/entitlement-separation section proving two distinct source items granting the same `domain:id` action produce one definition and two entitlements (Blocker 3), a fail-closed `attackType` section proving an unresolvable type now blocks rather than passes (Blocker 4), and a boolean-tree provenance section covering `all`-with-multiple-failures, `any`-with-one-success, `any`-with-all-failures, `not`-success, `not`-failure, and nested `all`/`any`/`not` compositions (Blocker 4) — 18 total numbered sections, all passing. New `tests/action-authority-groundwork-normalization-audit.test.mjs` (5 sections, all passing) scans every real `ATTACK_OPTION` record in `packs/feats.db` and `packs/talents.db` directly (136 total: 88 feats, 48 talents — matching round 8 correction #1's own count, confirming no discovery drift), runs each through `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` + `validateAttackOptionNormalization()`, and asserts zero failures.

**136-record normalization audit result**: 0 records failed normalization; 208 total gate-field occurrences classified — **195 NORMALIZED**, **10 EXTERNAL-WORKFLOW** (`requiresOpportunityAttack`: 7, `requiresManeuver`: 3 — genuinely unreachable from a dialog with no maneuver/reaction framing, exactly as round 8 correction #1 already established, never silently dropped), **3 UNSUPPORTED** (`requiresSwiftActions`: 3, on `Overwhelming Attack`/`Critical Strike`/`Mighty Swing` — flagged in round 8 correction #1 as unenforced by the live resolver too; this groundwork layer marks them explicitly unsupported rather than silently treating them as unconditional). Zero fields observed in the real dataset lack a disposition entry. Full per-field breakdown (all 24 real `requires*`/`excludes*` fields observed): `requiresAttackType` 68, `requiresContextFlags` 29, `requiresWeaponGroups` 15, `requiresUnarmed` 14, `requiresWeaponText` 12, `requiresCharge` 8, `requiresAutofire` 7, `requiresOpportunityAttack` 7 (external-workflow), `requiresAim` 7, `requiresOption` 5, `requiresRangeBand` 4, `excludesAreaAttack` 4, `requiresAreaAttack` 4, `requiresTargetType` 3, `requiresManeuver` 3 (external-workflow), `requiresSwiftActions` 3 (unsupported), `requiresFeatSelectedChoiceMatch` 3, `requiresTargetFlatFooted` 2, `requiresTargetDeniedDexBonus` 2, `requiresDamageType` 2, `requiresVehicleWeapon` 2, `excludesDamageType` 1, `excludesWeaponGroups` 1, `excludesOptions` 1, `requiresTargetFeat` 1. Generated report: `docs/audits/generated/action-authority-normalization-audit-report.{md,json}`.

Full rolling syntax check: **2513/2513 files** pass `node --check`. `tools/check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` JSON-parse checks all green.

### Updated certification status (correction #2)

No certification status listed above changes — this correction hardens a non-production-wired groundwork layer's own internal contracts, and reports the following as newly proven rather than newly certified (the layer remains unwired, not a claimant to any Attack Bonus certification):

- Action Authority normalization: **lossless, proven against the full current 136-record dataset** (0 silent drops; every gate field either normalized, marked external-workflow, or marked explicitly unsupported).
- Action Authority registry identity: **domain-qualified**, matching its own documented uniqueness contract.
- Action Authority definition vs. entitlement: **separated**, no duplicate-definition collision possible from multi-source grants, no persistent entitlement state.
- Action Authority availability evaluation: **delegates to the same certified classifier logic `CombatOptionResolver` uses** (shared module, not a second implementation), fails closed on unresolvable attack type, and records truthful provenance for every branch of a boolean requirement tree, including `not`-node failures.

Deliberately deferred, unchanged from correction #1's addendum: the production attack dialog is not wired to this layer; `PrerequisiteChecker`/`ActionEngine` are untouched; the NPC-flat and Damage domains are untouched; no caching or singleton registry lifecycle has been built. Per the reviewer's explicit instruction, this correction does not begin NPC-flat math and does not begin the Damage domain.

### Addendum correction #3 — the groundwork's contracts had four more blockers plus a proof gap, all hardened (still groundwork-only)

A third independent review of correction #2 (head `0c2f7ea`) accepted round 8's production fixes and correction #2's classifier extraction/registry composite-key/136-record field-coverage work in full, but held the groundwork's own internal contracts on four blockers plus one proof-quality gap, all confirmed by direct inspection against real code and real data before any fix was made.

**Blocker 1 — the definition/entitlement separation correction #2 introduced was incomplete: the "canonical" definition was still whichever source item was scanned first.** Confirmed: `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` still embedded the specific granting item's `source` (and the raw `_legacyRule`) directly inside `ActionDefinition`, and `ActorActionResolver.getOwnedActions()` silently kept whichever owned item's definition it normalized *first* for a given `domain:id`, discarding the rest — an order-dependent identity, exactly what the definition/entitlement split was supposed to eliminate. **Fix**: `ActionDefinition` no longer carries `source` or `_legacyRule` at all — it is now pure, source-independent content (id/name/domain/ownership/presentation/requirements/economy/execution/effects/tags only). The grant-specific raw rule moved to `ActionEntitlement.configuration.rule`. Two sources granting the same `domain:id` with identical resulting content register as one canonical definition plus two entitlements, exactly as before; two sources whose resulting content genuinely *differs* now throw a loud, explicit `ActorActionResolver.getOwnedActions(): conflicting ActionDefinition content...` error instead of silently picking a source — this groundwork does not yet support representing genuine per-grant configuration divergence for the same logical action id (a future round would need an explicit grant-normalized/`ActionInstance` layer for that; documented, not built). `ActionRegistry.register()` was changed from object-identity to content-equality re-registration (`definitionsContentEqual()`, exported for reuse) so a future shared/global registry receiving independently-normalized-but-identical definitions from different callers works cleanly rather than colliding. **Honest, documented scope limit** (not silently assumed fixed): the v1 schema still does not model slider bounds (`rule.max`) at all, so two sources disagreeing *only* on `max` would still normalize to identical content today and would not be caught by this conflict check.

**Blocker 2 — availability state precedence was wrong for mixed failures: a structurally-impossible option could surface as merely `disabled` (or even `external-workflow`) instead of `hidden`.** Confirmed against two real record shapes: Mighty Swing (`requiresAttackType: 'melee'`, `requiresSwiftActions: 2`) and Improved Disarm (`requiresAttackType: 'melee'`, `requiresManeuver: 'disarm'`), both in `combat-option-resolver.js`'s own `DEFAULT_ATTACK_OPTIONS`. `ActionAvailabilityEngine.evaluate()` only returned `hidden` when *every* unmet requirement happened to be structural; on a ranged weapon, Mighty Swing's structural `attackType` failure coexisted with its unsupported `requiresSwiftActions` failure, so the mix fell through to `disabled` — contradicting `CombatOptionResolver.optionAllowedForWeapon()`'s own unconditional, early-return attackType gate, which the live resolver never lets a second gate override. **Fix**: reordered `evaluate()` so *any* unmet structural leaf dominates to `hidden` before the external-workflow/unsupported/disabled checks run at all, matching the live resolver's unconditional gate exactly regardless of what else is also unmet.

**Blocker 3 — an unknown predicate wrapped in `not` failed OPEN, the opposite of fail-closed.** Confirmed by direct trace: `not: { type: 'futureUnknownPredicate' }` evaluated the unrecognized inner predicate as ordinary `met: false`, then negated it (`met = !false = true`) — permission by default under negation, the exact failure mode this entire freeze exists to prevent, and precisely the case an independent review's own regression test targeted. **Fix**: an unrecognized predicate type inside a `not` node now short-circuits directly to `met: false` for the whole `not` node, bypassing the negation entirely — unmet whether it appears positively or negated.

**Blocker 4 — classifier centralization was incomplete (two target predicates still duplicated, and had silently dropped two of the live resolver's own aliases), and `not(any(...))` still short-circuited and discarded its inner provenance.** Confirmed by diffing against `CombatOptionResolver.optionAllowedForWeapon()`'s exact source: `ActionAvailabilityEngine`'s `targetFlatFooted`/`targetDeniedDex` evaluators were still reimplemented inline rather than delegating to the correction #2 shared module, and had dropped the `context.flatFootedTarget` alias (flat-footed) and `context.deniedDexBonus` alias (denied-Dex) the live resolver honors. Separately, the `not` handler's composite-sub-node case (`not: { any: [...] }}` — the exact shape `excludesOptions` normalizes into) used `Array#some()` directly on the sub-nodes, which short-circuits after the first success, and discarded its own inner `evaluateNode()` calls' collected leaves entirely instead of merging them into the result's provenance array. **Fix**: extracted `isTargetFlatFooted()`/`isTargetDeniedDexBonus()` verbatim (byte-for-byte the live resolver's own two conditions — deliberately NOT composed from one another, since the live resolver's own checks are not symmetric and composing them would have silently changed certified production behavior rather than merely extracting it) into `weapon-target-gate-classifiers.js`; both `CombatOptionResolver` and `ActionAvailabilityEngine` now delegate to them. The `not` handler's composite case now delegates the whole sub-tree to one `evaluateNode()` call (which already never short-circuits at any level, `all`/`any` use `.map()` before reducing) and merges its collected leaves back into the outer result.

**Issue 5 — the "lossless" guard proved field-NAME coverage, not value-level reconciliation.** Confirmed: `validateAttackOptionNormalization()` only checked that a `sourceField` name appeared *somewhere* in the requirements tree — a leaf tagged `sourceField: 'requiresWeaponGroups'` whose `value` had been silently truncated (e.g. `['rifles', 'heavy']` corrupted to `['rifles']`) by a translation bug, or by anything mutating the definition after normalization, would still pass, since only the field's NAME was checked against an allowlist. **Fix**: the guard now independently recomputes each data-carrying field's expected value from the raw rule (array-normalized and order-insensitively compared for array fields; exact-match for scalar fields; specially unwrapped for the `not`/`not:{any:[...]}` shapes `excludesDamageType`/`excludesWeaponGroups`/`excludesOptions` produce) and compares it against the leaf's actual value, throwing on any mismatch. Re-running the full 136-record audit against this stricter guard found **zero new failures** — every real shipped record's translations were already value-correct; this closed a genuine gap in the *proof*, not a live defect in the translations themselves.

**Documentation inconsistency — "24 fields" was wrong on two counts.** `ATTACK_OPTION_GATE_FIELD_DISPOSITION` has always had 28 entries, not 24; 25 of those are actually observed in the current 136-record dataset (confirmed by direct count against the generated audit report), and the remaining 3 (`requiresTargetTalent`, `requiresTargetItem`, `requiresTargetText`) are proactively-supported compatibility fields the live `CombatOptionResolver` already understands but no current shipped record uses. Every prior "24 fields" claim (`action-definition.js`, `action-definition-normalizer.js`, `docs/architecture/action-authority-v1.md`) is corrected to the accurate 25-observed-plus-3-compatibility-equals-28-recognized breakdown; this correction's own prose above and the correction #2 section above it are left as the historical record of what was claimed at the time, per this ledger's established convention of correcting forward rather than rewriting prior rounds' text.

**Latent, documented (not fixed, no current-data blocker) — `requiresAttackType: 'any'` bypass semantics.** `CombatOptionResolver.optionAllowedForWeapon()` treats `option.requiresAttackType === 'any'` as a bypass (never enforces attack type for that specific option); the groundwork normalizer/evaluator do not currently replicate this bypass. Confirmed no shipped record among the current 136 uses `requiresAttackType: 'any'`, so this is not a present blocker — documented in the architecture doc so a future record using it is not silently mishandled.

**Test hygiene — the normalization audit test rewrote tracked, timestamped report files on every run.** Confirmed: `tests/action-authority-groundwork-normalization-audit.test.mjs` called `fs.writeFileSync()` on the generated `.md`/`.json` report as a direct side effect of running the test suite, so simply running `npm test` dirtied the working tree with a new timestamp every time. **Fix**: extracted the scan/tally logic into a shared module (`tools/action-authority-normalization-scan.mjs`); the test now only computes and asserts (no file writes); a new dedicated tool, `tools/report-action-authority-normalization-audit.mjs`, is the sole writer, run deliberately rather than as a test side effect. Verified: running the test suite twice in a row now leaves the report files' hash unchanged; running the report tool deliberately still regenerates them correctly (195/10/3 counts unchanged, only the timestamp updates).

### Test matrix (correction #3)

`tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` extended to 23 numbered sections (from correction #2's 18): section 1/2 and 15 rewritten to prove `ActionDefinition` carries no `source`/`_legacyRule` and that provenance lives exclusively on `ActionEntitlement`; section 3 extended for content-equal idempotent re-registration plus a genuine-content-conflict throw; new section 3c (Blocker 1, divergent requirement content between two sources throws, with an explicit documented note on the `rule.max` scope limit); new sections 19a/19b/19c (Blocker 2, Mighty-Swing-shaped and Improved-Disarm-shaped mixed structural+unsupported / structural+external-workflow failures resolve to `hidden`, and the same definitions correctly fall through to `unsupported` once the structural gate is satisfied); new section 20 (Blocker 3, the reviewer's exact `not:{type:'futureUnknownPredicate'}` regression case); new sections 21a/21b (Blocker 4, `flatFootedTarget`/`deniedDexBonus` alias parity); new section 22 (Blocker 4, `not(any(...))` full-provenance proof using the exact shape `excludesOptions` normalizes into — 4 leaves recorded including the two branches that would have been lost to short-circuiting); new section 23 (Issue 5, the reviewer's exact value-corruption mutation test). Section 18(f)'s pre-existing nested-tree expectation was corrected from `disabled` to `hidden`, since a `not(weaponGroup)` leaf is itself structural and Blocker 2's fix makes it dominate. `tests/action-authority-groundwork-normalization-audit.test.mjs` gained a second mutation test (Issue 5, corrupted-value-with-intact-field-name) alongside its existing unrecognized-field mutation test, and re-ran clean against the stricter value-reconciliation guard (0 new failures across all 136 real records).

### Required validation (correction #3)

Full rolling test suite: **238/238 passed** (243 total test files, 5 documented pre-existing exclusions unchanged) — includes every production Round 8 / round 8 correction #1 / round 8 correction #2 regression test still green, confirming none of this round's fixes touched certified production behavior. Full rolling syntax check: **2515/2515 files** pass `node --check` (2 new files: the extracted scan module and its report-generator tool). `tools/check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, and `system.json`/`template.json` JSON-parse checks all green.

### Updated certification status (correction #3)

No certification status above changes — this correction hardens a non-production-wired groundwork layer's own internal contracts further, reporting the following as newly proven:

- Action Authority definition identity: **source-independent, content-only** — no per-item provenance embedded, no order-dependent "first source wins," genuine multi-source content divergence fails loudly rather than silently.
- Action Authority registry re-registration: **content-equal, not object-identity** — supports a future shared/global registry design without spurious conflicts.
- Action Authority availability state precedence: **any unmet structural gate dominates to hidden**, matching the live resolver's unconditional gates exactly under every mixed-failure combination tested.
- Action Authority fail-closed semantics: **hold under negation**, not just in the positive case.
- Action Authority classifier delegation: **complete for all weapon/target gates** including flat-footed/denied-Dex, with the live resolver's exact alias set restored.
- Action Authority boolean-tree provenance: **genuinely non-short-circuiting**, including the `not(any(...))` shape real `excludesOptions` data uses.
- Action Authority normalization guard: **value-level reconciliation**, not merely field-name coverage — proven against the full 136-record dataset with zero regressions.

Deliberately deferred, unchanged from correction #2's addendum: the production attack dialog is not wired to this layer; `PrerequisiteChecker`/`ActionEngine` are untouched; the NPC-flat and Damage domains are untouched; no caching or singleton registry lifecycle has been built; slider-bound (`rule.max`) divergence between sources is not yet representable in the schema (documented limitation, not a defect). Per the reviewer's explicit instruction, this correction does not begin NPC-flat math and does not begin the Damage domain.

### Addendum correction #4 — five more foundation defects: value-preserving marker gates, nested fail-closed, identity never from source item, entitlement cloning, registry canonical-object idempotence (still groundwork-only)

A fourth independent review of correction #3 (head `8d9e8d1`) accepted every fix from that round in full — Round 8 production, structural-hidden precedence, the shared target flat-footed/denied-Dex classifiers with restored aliases, the direct `not:{type:unknown}` fix, `not(any(...))`'s non-short-circuiting provenance, definition/entitlement separation, domain-qualified registry identity, the 25-observed/28-recognized inventory, and the test/report separation — but held Action Authority on five further defects, all confirmed against real code and real data before any fix was made.

**Blocker 1 — the external/unsupported marker gates still lost their real values.** Confirmed: `requiresManeuver: 'disarm'` (real value from `packs/feats.db`; `'grapple'` also observed) and `requiresSwiftActions: 2` normalized to bare `{ type: 'externalWorkflow', value: true }` / `{ type: 'unsupported', value: true }` — the specific maneuver name and the specific swift-action count were discarded during translation. This groundwork correctly refuses to *evaluate* either gate (per explicit reviewer instruction), but "we don't evaluate it" and "we don't remember what it actually requires" are different claims, and correction #3's value-level reconciliation guard never checked either field because there was no real value to reconcile in the first place. **Fix**: `buildRequirements()` now preserves `key`/`value` (`{ type: 'externalWorkflow', key: 'maneuver', value: 'disarm' }`, `{ type: 'unsupported', key: 'swiftActions', value: 2 }`); `requiresOpportunityAttack` stays a plain boolean marker since its own source field is boolean (no additional value exists to preserve). `gateValueReconciles()` now reconciles both fields' values, with dedicated mutation tests (`requiresManeuver: 'disarm'` corrupted to `'banthaRush'`; `requiresSwiftActions: 2` corrupted to `3`) proving the guard catches each. Evaluation itself is provably unchanged: `externalWorkflow()`/`unsupported()` still ignore the predicate entirely and unconditionally fail closed — a dedicated test confirms the engine still refuses to independently calculate swift-action availability after this fix.

**Blocker 2 — unknown predicates could still fail open under NESTED negation.** Confirmed by direct trace: correction #3 fixed only `not: { type: 'unknownType' }` directly, by special-casing it before the negation ran. A nested form, `not: { any: [ { type: 'futureUnknownPredicate' }, knownFalseLeaf ] }`, was NOT covered by that special case — the composite `any` evaluation collapsed the unknown leaf to ordinary boolean `false` during composition (`any(false, false) = false`), and the outer `not` then negated that composite `false` into `true` — permission by default, several layers deep, the identical failure class correction #3 believed it had closed. **Fix**: `evaluateNode()` now returns a tri-state `MET`/`UNMET`/`UNRESOLVED` instead of a plain boolean, with `NOT(MET) = UNMET`, `NOT(UNMET) = MET`, `NOT(UNRESOLVED) = UNRESOLVED` (never `MET`); `all` composition is `UNMET` if any child is `UNMET`, else `UNRESOLVED` if any child is `UNRESOLVED`, else `MET`; `any` composition is `MET` if any child is `MET`, else `UNRESOLVED` if any child is `UNRESOLVED`, else `UNMET` — both exactly matching the prior boolean `.every(Boolean)`/`.some(Boolean)` reduction for an all-known-values tree (verified: every pre-existing known-value test in the groundwork suite, sections 1-23, passes unchanged against this refactor). `UNRESOLVED` can now never be mistaken for a definite `false` a later `not` could flip into `true`, at any nesting depth. Six regression cases required by the reviewer are all proven: positive unknown, direct `not(unknown)`, `not(any(unknown, false))` (the reviewer's own targeted example), `not(all(unknown, true))`, `not(not(unknown))`, and an unknown predicate mixed into a larger nested tree alongside otherwise-fully-met requirements — none resolve `available`. A companion test confirms a fully-known, fully-met nested tree still resolves `available` unchanged.

**Blocker 3 — `ActionDefinition` identity could still fall back to the granting source item.** Confirmed: `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` fell back to `normalizeKey(sourceItem?.name)` and then the literal string `'unknown-attack-option'` when a rule carried no `option`/`id`/`key`/`name` of its own — canonical identity could still depend on which item happened to grant it, precisely the class of bug correction #3's source-independence fix (Blocker 1) was meant to close everywhere, just for the one case that fix's own code path had not actually eliminated. **Fix**: a rule with no stable identifier of its own now fails normalization loudly (`normalizeAttackOptionRule(): rule "..." has no stable action identifier...`) instead of silently borrowing or inventing one; `sourceItem` is no longer read anywhere inside the function (it remains a parameter only for call-site stability — `ActorActionResolver`, the sole caller, already builds `ActionEntitlement` provenance separately from its own `item` reference, never through this function).
