Verified against the full real dataset before assuming safety, not after: a direct scan of all 136 current shipped ATTACK_OPTION records confirmed every one already carries its own `option`/`id`/`key`/`name` — this fix rejects zero real records, exactly as the reviewer predicted. A new synthetic test proves a rule with none of those fields throws even when `sourceItem.name` is present and meaningful.

**Blocker 4 — `ActionEntitlement.configuration.rule` exposed the source item's live nested rule object by reference.** Confirmed: `buildEntitlement()` stored `rule` (the object `extractAttackOptionRules()` reads directly out of `item.system.abilityMeta.rules[]`) verbatim on `configuration.rule` — any caller mutating `entitlement.configuration.rule` would silently mutate the actor's real owned Item data through that shared reference. **Fix**: `deepClone()` (`scripts/utils/data-utils.js`'s existing plain `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))` helper — appropriate here since every ATTACK_OPTION rule field is plain JSON-serializable data, no functions/Dates/circular references) breaks the reference before storing. A new test resolves entitlements, mutates `entitlement.configuration.rule` directly (including injecting a new field), and confirms the source item's real rule data (compared by full JSON snapshot) is completely unaffected.

**Blocker 5 — `ActionRegistry.register()`'s content-equal idempotence still overwrote the canonical object.** Confirmed: `register()` correctly detected content equality (via `definitionsContentEqual()`) but still executed `this.#byKey.set(key, definition)` unconditionally on every call, replacing the stored (first, canonical) object with whichever content-equal object was passed most recently — true object-identity idempotence, which a registry claiming canonical identity requires, did not actually hold. **Fix**: on a content-equal re-registration, `register()` now returns the stored value directly without calling `set()` again, so the FIRST object ever registered under a given composite key remains canonical (`===`-stable) across any number of subsequent content-equal re-registrations. A new test registers a definition, then registers a second, independently-normalized, content-equal object, and confirms both `register()`'s return value and every subsequent `registry.get()` continue to be the exact first object, never the second.

**Documentation correction — a misleading `ActorActionResolver` comment/error-message overclaimed slider-`max` divergence detection.** An earlier comment and the conflict error message both cited "a differing slider `max`" as an example of content this conflict check catches — factually wrong, since `ActionDefinition` v1 never reads or models `rule.max` at all (confirmed directly: `normalizeAttackOptionRule()` never references `max`), so two sources disagreeing *only* on `max` still normalize to identical definition content today and are NOT caught by this check (their differing `max` values survive only on each entitlement's own `configuration.rule.max`, never unified or even compared at the definition level). This contradicted what the ledger's own correction #3 entry and `tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` section 3c already correctly documented. **Fix**: the comment and error message now cite an example the schema actually models (`requiresAttackType`); `definitionsContentEqual()` gained an explicit doc comment on its `JSON.stringify`-equality assumption (safe only because every `ActionDefinition` comes from `normalizeAttackOptionRule()`'s one fixed, deterministic construction order — a deliberate, narrow assumption a future normalizer must re-check, not a general deep-equality guarantee). No generic deep-equality framework was built, per the reviewer's explicit "do not broaden scope unnecessarily" instruction.

### Test matrix (correction #4)

`tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` extended to 35 numbered sections (from correction #3's 23): new sections 24/25 (Blocker 1, requiresManeuver/requiresSwiftActions value preservation plus their required mutation tests, plus a fail-closed/no-independent-swift-action-calculation check); new sections 26a-26g (Blocker 2, the full required regression matrix -- positive unknown, direct `not(unknown)`, `not(any(unknown,false))`, `not(all(unknown,true))`, `not(not(unknown))`, unknown mixed into a larger nested tree, plus a known-value-unchanged sanity check); new section 27 (Blocker 3, a synthetic identifier-less rule fails even with a named source item); new section 28 (Blocker 4, entitlement rule clone/no-source-mutation); new section 29 (Blocker 5, registry canonical-object preservation). `tests/action-authority-groundwork-normalization-audit.test.mjs` re-ran clean against every fix (0 new failures across all 136 real records).

### Required validation (correction #4)

136-record normalization audit (re-run against every correction #4 fix): **136 total records** (88 feats, 48 talents) — **25 observed gate fields, 28 recognized gate fields**, **195 normalized + 10 external-workflow + 3 unsupported = 208 gate-field occurrences**, **0 unrecognized, 0 untranslated, 0 value mismatches, 0 silent drops** — identical totals to correction #3, confirming none of this round's fixes changed real-data normalization outcomes, only the guard's rigor and the marker gates' preserved content. Clean-tree verification: the normalization audit test was run multiple times in this round; `docs/audits/generated/action-authority-normalization-audit-report.{md,json}` were unchanged (same hash) across repeated test runs, and only changed when the dedicated report tool (`tools/report-action-authority-normalization-audit.mjs`) was run deliberately (timestamp only; all counts identical). Full rolling test suite, production regression suites (`attack-dialog-context-authority`, `attack-dialog-context-authority-correction`, `attack-bonus-math-integrity`), `tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs`, `tools/check-combat-math-ssot.mjs`, `tools/validate-data.js`, and `system.json`/`template.json` JSON-parse checks all green. Full rolling syntax check: 2515/2515 files pass `node --check`.

### Updated certification status (correction #4)

No certification status above changes — this correction hardens a non-production-wired groundwork layer's own internal contracts further, reporting the following as newly proven:

- Action Authority marker-gate fidelity: **the externalWorkflow/unsupported markers now preserve the real requirement value** (maneuver name, swift-action count), not just a bare boolean, with both value-reconciled by the normalization guard.
- Action Authority fail-closed semantics: **hold under arbitrary `all`/`any`/`not` nesting depth**, not just the direct `not:{type:unknown}` case — proven via a tri-state MET/UNMET/UNRESOLVED evaluation model.
- Action Authority definition identity: **can never originate from the granting source item**, even as a fallback for an identifier-less rule — a rule with no stable identifier of its own now fails loudly instead of silently borrowing one.
- Action Authority entitlement safety: **`configuration.rule` is a deep clone**, never the source Item's own live nested rule object by reference.
- Action Authority registry idempotence: **canonical-object stable** (`===`), not just canonical-content stable — the first registered object always remains the one returned and stored.

Deliberately deferred, unchanged from correction #3's addendum: the production attack dialog is not wired to this layer; `PrerequisiteChecker`/`ActionEngine` are untouched; the NPC-flat and Damage domains are untouched; no caching or singleton registry lifecycle has been built; slider-bound (`rule.max`) divergence between sources is still not representable in the schema (an honestly-documented limitation, corrected in this round's own comments rather than silently left contradictory); the live resolver's `requiresAttackType: 'any'` bypass is still not replicated by this groundwork (documented, no current record uses it). Per the reviewer's explicit instruction, this correction does not begin NPC-flat math, does not begin the Damage domain, and does not wire `ActionAvailabilityEngine` into production.

### Addendum correction #5 — causal blocker propagation replaces flat diagnostic-leaf filtering (still groundwork-only)

A fifth independent review of correction #4 (head `98c84f0`) accepted every fix from that round in full but held Action Authority on one remaining defect, confirmed by direct trace against real code before any fix was made — no longer an ATTACK_OPTION-data problem, but a flaw in the generic `all`/`any`/`not` evaluation engine itself, exactly the machinery a future domain (Damage, most plausibly) would rely on more heavily than the current flat, mostly non-nested Attack Option adapter does.

**The defect.** `ActionAvailabilityEngine.evaluate()`'s state classification (`hidden`/`external-workflow`/`unsupported`/`disabled`) filtered a single FLAT `leaves` array — every leaf `evaluateNode()` had ever pushed anywhere in the tree, collected specifically so non-short-circuited diagnostic provenance was complete. That flat list conflated two different things: "evaluated somewhere in the tree, for diagnostics" and "actually caused the root to fail." Confirmed by direct trace: `all(any(weaponGroup:heavy [fails on a non-heavy weapon, structural], aim [succeeds]), charge [fails])` — the `any()` genuinely succeeds through Aim, so the ONLY real blocker is the unmet Charge gate, and the correct state is `disabled: "Requires Charge"`. But the old flat-filter code (`unmet.some(l => l.structural)`) still found the failed, losing `weaponGroup:heavy` leaf sitting in the same undifferentiated list and returned `hidden` instead — wrong, since that structural failure occurred on the LOSING side of a satisfied alternative, not a genuine blocker. The identical flattening bug let a losing `externalWorkflow`/`unsupported` branch dominate its own satisfied `any()` sibling the same way. Confirmed NOT a live regression against the current 136 real ATTACK_OPTION records — the adapter emits mostly flat `all[]` trees, with `not(any(...))` (for `excludesOptions`) the one live nested construct, and neither shape triggers this bug — and `ActionAvailabilityEngine` remains entirely unwired from production either way. But it is a real, confirmed semantic bug in the promised generic boolean-tree contract, worth fixing before any domain (Damage specifically) starts relying on genuine alternative-requirement structures.

**Fix.** `evaluateNode()` now returns `{ state, blockers }` at every node instead of a bare tri-state value. `blockers` is the causally-relevant SUBSET of `leaves` that actually contributed to that node's own non-MET state, computed by following the boolean tree's own structure, not a post-hoc flat filter:
- a leaf's or `not`-node's own blockers are `[]` if its own state is MET, else `[itself]`. A `not` node's inner sub-tree detail (for a composite `not: {any:[...]}`/`not: {all:[...]}` shape) still gets pushed into `leaves` for full diagnostics, but is deliberately NOT re-exposed as a blocker of the `not` node one level up — the `not` node's own synthesized leaf IS the blocker representing why the exclusion itself failed, matching the reviewer's own prescribed architecture ("blocker provenance should represent why the NOT node itself failed, while retaining inner details separately for diagnostics").
- `all`: every child's blockers are unioned in — a MET child's blockers are already `[]` by construction, so `all` needs no extra branching beyond flattening.
- `any`: if the combined state is MET (at least one child succeeded), blockers is `[]` — none of the failed siblings blocked anything, since the `any()` itself is satisfied. Otherwise (no child succeeded) every child's blockers are unioned in — every branch genuinely contributed to the `any()`'s own failure.

`ActionAvailabilityEngine.evaluate()`'s state classification now reads `blockers`, never the flat `leaves` array; `leaves` (still returned as `requirements`) is completely unchanged — every evaluated branch, including the losing side of a satisfied `any()`, is still exposed for full UI diagnostics.

**Verification before writing any test.** Every one of the 29 pre-existing groundwork test sections (1 through 29, spanning every correction #3/#4 structural-precedence and nested-unknown-fail-closed test) was traced by hand against the new design before implementation, then confirmed to pass unchanged after — including the section 18(f) nested `all(any(aim,charge), not(weaponGroup:heavy))` case (still correctly `hidden` in its unmet variant, since in THAT case neither `all`-child achieves MET, so the structural `not(weaponGroup)` leaf genuinely does participate) and section 22's `not(any(excludesOptions...))` shape (still correctly `disabled`/`available` per case, since the `not`-node's own synthesized leaf, not the inner `any`'s now-correctly-empty blockers, is what propagates).

### Test matrix (correction #5)

`tests/action-authority-groundwork.test.mjs` extended to 41 numbered sections (from correction #4's 35): new section 30, with five required sub-cases (30a-30e) — a failed structural/external-workflow/unsupported alternative inside a satisfied `any()` must NOT dominate to `hidden`/`external-workflow`/`unsupported` respectively (30a/30b/30c, all correctly resolve `disabled: "Requires Charge"`, the genuine sole blocker); when EVERY alternative of an `any()` genuinely fails, a structural branch among them DOES legitimately participate in the failure and drives `hidden` — an explicit, deliberately-tested state-policy decision matching the existing "structural impossibility dominates" philosophy, not an accident of flattening (30d); a successful `any()` retains full diagnostic provenance for both its succeeding and its failed sibling branch (30e).

### Required validation (correction #5)

Full rolling test suite, production regression suites (`attack-dialog-context-authority`, `attack-dialog-context-authority-correction`, `attack-bonus-math-integrity`), the 136-record normalization audit (unchanged totals — this correction touches only `ActionAvailabilityEngine`'s evaluation/classification logic, not the normalizer), and full rolling syntax check all green (exact counts in the commit's validation record).

### Updated certification status (correction #5)

No certification status above changes — this correction hardens a non-production-wired groundwork layer's own internal contracts further:

- Action Authority state classification: **driven by causal blocker propagation, not flat "any failed leaf anywhere in the tree" filtering** — a losing structural/external-workflow/unsupported branch inside a satisfied `any()` can no longer dominate state classification for its unrelated, successfully-satisfied sibling.
- Action Authority diagnostic completeness: **unchanged and re-verified** — `requirements` (full leaf provenance) still exposes every evaluated branch, including the losing side of a satisfied `any()`, independent of the blocker-propagation fix.

Deliberately deferred, unchanged from correction #4's addendum: the production attack dialog is not wired to this layer; `PrerequisiteChecker`/`ActionEngine` are untouched; the NPC-flat and Damage domains are untouched; no caching or singleton registry lifecycle has been built; slider-bound (`rule.max`) divergence and the live resolver's `requiresAttackType: 'any'` bypass remain documented, unresolved gaps. Per the reviewer's explicit instruction, this correction is scoped solely to causal blocker propagation through `all`/`any`/`not` — no new schema work, no further ATTACK_OPTION migration changes, no production wiring, and it does not begin NPC-flat math or the Damage domain.

## Domain: Damage — **SSOT-CERTIFIED (character-weapon live path). FIXED: confirmed severe live dice-shape bug.**: see `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit.md` (§18) and `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit-correction-1.md`

- **A (canonical, numeric bonus only):** `resolveDamageBonus()` (`combat-roll-math.js:567-606`, stock-droid path at 503-565): ½ level, ability, weapon enhancement, rage, Rapid Alchemy, effect-intent, combat-option damage, scoped feat damage.
- **CONFIRMED — custom weapon damage formulas are respected verbatim, never "corrected."** `scripts/combat/rolls/damage.js:186`: `dmgResult.flags?.stockDamageFormula ?? (weapon.system?.damage ?? '1d6')` — the Item's stored dice string (e.g. `4d12kh3`) is used as-is; the resolver's numeric bonus and talent/force-item dice are appended as separate formula parts, never substituted into or parsed out of the base string. `weapon-data-resolver.js:191` only supplies a fallback (`'1d8'`) when the field is **empty**, never overwrites an existing value. **This directly confirms Gar'ee's Heavy Blaster Rifle `4d12kh3` will survive unmodified** — no repair-toward-default mechanism exists anywhere in this path.
- **Full SSOT/authority-graph audit completed** (read-only investigation, no code changed, per explicit reviewer directive following Action Authority's acceptance): `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit.md`. Headline finding: **CONFIRMED severe live bug** — the actual production damage-roll path every player reaches (sheet Damage button + post-attack chat-card Damage button, both `SWSERoll.rollDamage` → `scripts/combat/rolls/damage.js#rollDamage()`) never applies `damageExtraWeaponDice`/`damageDiceStepBonus`/`damageDieStepIncreases`/`criticalDamageDieStepBonus` for an ordinary actor — Deadeye, Burst Fire, Mighty Swing, Rapid Shot's/Rapid Strike's die-step bonus, and critical-only die-step bonuses are computed correctly by `CombatOptionResolver.collectAttackModifiers()` and then silently discarded before the dice are ever rolled. A second, separate `rollDamage()` in `scripts/combat/rolls/attacks.js` applies them correctly but has no live UI caller for ordinary weapon attacks (only a narrow Force Adept talent-action path). A third, `attacks.js#rollAttackAndDamageWithNarration()`, is confirmed dead. Full authority graph, duplicate-authority table, ModifierEngine damage-target vocabulary split, critical-multiplier duplication, and proposed SSOT/migration plan are in the linked audit.
- **Correction #1 closed all six HOLD items** the review of `f95fb5b` raised, plus two related open questions, documented in `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit-correction-1.md`: (1) `RIDER_EFFECT`/`FORCE_POINT_DIE_STEP` confirmed unconsumed anywhere, out of Damage scope (unimplemented maneuver/Force-Point mechanics, not damage numbers); (2) `ResolutionContext`/`RULES.*` inventory closed at exactly 4 values, `EXTEND_CRITICAL_RANGE` confirmed overlapping `CombatOptionResolver`'s own handling (a third independent critical-range lane); bonus finding of a fourth, largely dead attack+damage pipeline (`enhanced-rolls.js#rollAutofire()`); (3) `VALID_TARGET_PATTERNS` confirmed never enforced (zero callers), `attack_and_damage` traced to one inert "Weakening Strike" combat-action record; (4) **vehicle/starship damage confirmed to share the live character `rollAttack()`/`rollDamage()` pipeline** (not a separate domain) via `crew-skill-router.js` — and a new confirmed bug was found in the same trace: `getHalfLevelDamageBonus()`/`getDamageAbilityContribution()` have zero vehicle-weapon gating, so a named gunner's personal ½-level/ability is silently added onto vehicle weapon damage today; (5) repo-wide pack scan confirms the feat/talent universe is the complete surface for declarative damage rules — a newly-discovered `combat-actions.db` (`ruleData.damageExtraWeaponDice` etc.) is confirmed inert descriptive/UI-only metadata, not a live contribution source; (6) Sneak Attack's fallback logic confirmed RAW-correct (dice-count × talent-count, denied-Dex gating, correctly avoids the D&D flanking assumption); "Skirmisher" confirmed to be an **attack-bonus** talent, not a damage talent at all — `damage.js`'s own comment citing it was simply mistaken. The correction also refines the proposed SSOT API per reviewer direction: `resolveDamageBonus()` stays an unchanged, additive-only numeric sub-authority; two new functions (`resolveDamageComposition()`, `buildDamageFormula()`) own dice/critical composition and final-formula assembly, superseding the original audit's "extend `resolveDamageBonus()` directly" recommendation.
- **Damage SSOT implemented and FIXED** (authorized following Correction #1's closure; full record in `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit.md` §18): `combat-roll-math.js` gained `resolveDamageComposition()` (structured additive/dice/critical/ledger composition) and `buildDamageFormula()` (the one pure formula-assembly function), plus `resolveCriticalMultiplier()` (the one critical-multiplier authority, replacing three independent prior implementations — weapon-only `combat-stat-rules.js`, rule-aware-but-not-option-aware `combat-utils.js`, and option-aware-but-not-rule-aware inline logic in `attacks.js#rollAttack()`). `damage.js#rollDamage()` is now a pure orchestration wrapper (workflow context → canonical composition → canonical formula → roll → post/consume state); the confirmed severe bug (Deadeye/Burst Fire/Mighty Swing/die-size-step contributions silently dropped on the live path) is **fixed**. `attacks.js#rollDamage()` is now a thin delegate to `damage.js#rollDamage()` (collapsed from a second, divergent — and Inquisition-incomplete — formula builder); `rollAttackAndDamageWithNarration()` (confirmed dead) now delegates its damage side to the same canonical pair, reordered so its critical state is known before the formula is built (previously it could never apply a critical multiplier at all on this dead path). The gunner-half-level/ability vehicle-weapon leak is **fixed** (`getHalfLevelDamageBonus()`/`getDamageAbilityContribution()` now gate on `isVehicleWeapon()`). Sneak Attack is now a real, stable, importable production module (`damage-talent-contributions.js`) with zero dependency on `runtime-bugfix-hotfixes.js`'s load order; Force Item/Inquisition dice are deduplicated into `damage-item-dice-contributions.js`. `damageExtraWeaponDice`/`damageDiceStepBonus` dual-write collapsed to a single canonical read (no behavior change, proven no-double-count). Damage-target alias spellings (`damage`/`damage.weapon`/`damage.melee`/`damage.ranged`) normalize onto `global.damage` in one `ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()` pass shared with `ModifierEngine`'s own Effect-Intent damage modifiers. `attack_and_damage` deliberately left inert (not wired live — no evidence it was ever intended to apply, per this project's "do not change SWSE rules" policy). **Certified for the character-weapon live path** — sheet Damage button, chat-card Damage button, `attacks.js`'s alternate entry, and the vehicle-weapon gunner path all delegate to the same composition/formula pair (proven by `tests/damage-modifier-ssot.test.mjs`, 29 checks: 3 fail-before/pass-after proofs, full golden matrix, ledger reconciliation, no-double-application, vehicle gating, stock-droid/NPC-flat preservation, structural caller-parity). **Not certified / explicitly deferred**: vehicle-scale damage multiplier (x2/x5/x10) and ion/special damage-type interpretation (unwired at runtime, out of scope per the authorizing command); target-mitigation/application boundary (DR/SR/shields/Damage Threshold/HP/Condition Track, untouched); Damage Threshold refactor (not begun); NPC-flat work beyond the boundary this migration already respects (not begun); Action Authority production wiring (not begun); the `scripts/dev/audit-phase10*-damage-*.mjs` developer scripts (still not read).

## Domain: Damage — correction round (independent review of `4d05a80`/`1b93e1a` held three blockers plus one hardening item)

An independent review of the pushed SSOT migration (`4d05a80`/`1b93e1a`) accepted the architecture, formula builder, ordinary-math path, roll-wrapper convergence, vehicle fix, and Sneak Attack extraction, but held three blockers plus one hardening item — full technical detail in `docs/audits/v2-damage-modifier-authority-audit.md` §18.12:

1. **Attack-option selections were not transported from the attack roll into its paired chat-card Damage roll** (Deadeye/Burst Fire/Power Attack/etc. selections were silently lost, so the new resolver had nothing to read even though its own math was correct) — **FIXED**, superseded by the more rigorous addendum correction documented immediately below.
2. **Typed Damage stacking drove only the contribution ledger, not the actual rolled total** — a same-typed collision could show a correct "highest applied / other suppressed" ledger while the real roll still summed both contributions in full. **FIXED**: `computeTypedDamageModifierPool()`'s stacked result is now the only path either the Effect-Intent pool or the item-alias pool contributes through, feeding `resolveDamageBonus()`'s/`resolveStockDroidDamageContract()`'s own numeric `total` directly. Proven by a `highestOnly` competence-collision test plus `stackUnlessSameSource`/`untyped` collision-matrix tests against the actual rolled total.
3. **Stock-droid dice modifiers (die-size step, extra weapon die) were applied twice** — once pre-baked into the published formula by `resolveStockDroidDamageContract()`, once again generically by `resolveDamageComposition()`. **FIXED**: the published formula's dice portion is now returned RAW (re-rendered only for canonical spacing); the single generic dice-mutation pass inside `resolveDamageComposition()`/`buildDamageFormula()` now runs exactly once for stock and ordinary weapons alike. `tests/stock-droid-damage-math.test.mjs` tests 8-12 rewritten accordingly.
4. **Hardening — a carried critical multiplier could override a currently-active rule.** `resolveCriticalMultiplier()` no longer returns a carried value immediately; it is one more input to the same `Math.max(...)` composition (a floor, never a short-circuit).

All four items were fixed and tested in the same working session that produced this correction round, **before** the addendum described below was written.

## Domain: Damage — Attack-to-Damage Option Context Certification (addendum correction)

A follow-up addendum, reviewed independently against head `1b93e1a` (i.e. written before the correction round above existed), scoped a further, much more rigorous correction to exactly one of the three blockers above — attack-option activation context surviving losslessly from the attack dialog through the attack chat card into the later Damage roll — and explicitly instructed that the other two blockers (typed stacking driving only the ledger; stock-droid double-application) "remain for the next correction unless a tiny shared change is strictly necessary," on the stated belief that both were still open.

**Factual correction, stated plainly and not silently absorbed:** both of those blockers were already fixed and tested earlier in this same session, before the addendum arrived (see the correction-round subsection immediately above). This addendum did not touch, revert, or otherwise interact with either fix — it added only the transport hardening below, scoped strictly to Blocker 1. The two blockers are **not** independently open at the head this addendum was pushed against; the ledger states this explicitly rather than complying with the addendum's now-outdated premise.

**What this addendum certifies:** `combat-context-serializer.js#summarizeCombatWorkflowContext()` now captures a canonical `attack.selectedOptions` map — booleans and numeric slider values alike, merged from `combatOptions`/`attackOptions` under either historical spelling into one representation, with only JSON-safe primitives (`boolean`/finite `number`/`string`) surviving (`isSerializableOptionValue()` — an object, function, or Foundry-document reference is dropped, never serialized, and never throws). The map round-trips losslessly through `encodeCombatWorkflowContext()`/`decodeCombatWorkflowContext()` and is restored by `mergeCombatWorkflowContextIntoRollOptions()` onto `rollOptions.combatOptions`/`rollOptions.attackOptions`, with an explicit caller-supplied value always taking precedence over a carried one. No inference from high-level flags is performed (Aim true does not imply Deadeye selected; Autofire true does not imply Burst Fire selected), and no entitlement is granted for an option the actor does not own even if a stored selection claims it — `CombatOptionResolver.getAvailableAttackOptions()`'s own feat-ownership gate still governs.

Proven by a dedicated suite, `tests/attack-damage-option-context-transport.test.mjs` (18 checks, not folded into the generic formula-math suite): canonical round-trip + historical alias normalization + non-primitive value rejection (Section 1); one named round-trip test per required option — Deadeye (`damageExtraWeaponDice=1`), Burst Fire (`=2`), Rapid Shot (`+1` once, not double-counted against its `damageDiceStepBonus` alias), Rapid Strike (`+1` die), Mighty Swing (`+1` die), Power Attack (slider value `3` reaching `damageBonus=3` exactly, proving non-boolean values transport) (Section 2); snapshot immutability against later source mutation, an actor/UI-mutation-can't-retroactively-change-an-earlier-snapshot proof, a negative test (Aim true, Deadeye not selected — must not apply), and an unowned-option test (a stored selection for an option the actor does not own is never granted) (Section 3); a check that the pre-existing target/aim/autofire/range/critical/damage-type transport is not regressed by the additive option snapshot (Section 4); and four structural tests proving the real production hop-by-hop chain — attack dialog → `_runCanonicalAttackWithPreroll()`/`rollAttack()` → `damageWorkflowContext` attached to the chat message → `encodeCombatWorkflowContext()` embedded on the chat card's Damage button (`data-workflow-context`) → `runtime-bugfix-hotfixes.js#rollDamageFromButton()`'s decode → `damage.js#rollDamage()`'s `mergeCombatWorkflowContextIntoRollOptions()` restoring the map before `resolveDamageComposition()` is ever called (Section 5).

The 136-record `ATTACK_OPTION` normalization/discovery surface (`docs/audits/v2-action-authority-groundwork-architecture.md`) was not altered by this addendum.

**Current status of all four originally-held items:** all three blockers (attack-option transport, typed stacking, stock-droid double-application) and the hardening item are now fixed and independently tested. Per the addendum's own explicit instruction, this ledger entry does not self-declare the overall Damage SSOT re-certified — that determination is left to the next independent review pass, which now has a materially different (and more complete) picture than the addendum assumed when it was written.

## Domain: Grapple — CONFIRMED DEFECT (roll-time formula diverges from displayed formula) — **FIXED, SSOT-CERTIFIED, RULES-FIDELITY-CERTIFIED, STATIC-MODIFIER-COMPOSITION-CERTIFIED, STACKING-CERTIFIED, DEFENSE-CONTEXT-CERTIFIED, OPPOSED-CHECK-CERTIFIED, STATE-EFFECT-CERTIFIED (defense-side AND attack-side, round 8), TARGET-DEFENSE-AUTHORITY-CERTIFIED (one shared function, round 8)**

Three to four independent implementations, not one:
- **A (canonical/derived, ORIGINALLY BROKEN — see below):** `derived-calculator.js` — `bab.total + max(strMod,dexMod) + sizeMod + speciesGrapple` → `system.derived.grappleBonus`. Matched RAW in shape, but see the newly-discovered NaN defect below.
- **D (guarded duplicate, currently consistent, not yet touched):** `PanelContextBuilder.js:1428-1438` — identical formula, own copy of the size table, used as a fallback when `derived.grappleBonus` is unset **or non-finite** (this guard is what silently masked the NaN defect below — see that writeup). Numerically consistent today but a second hand-maintained copy that can silently fork from the canonical table on a future edit. Not yet touched by this SSOT extraction (out of scope for this pass — flagged as a follow-up).
- **D — CONFIRMED live bug — FIXED:** `scripts/houserules/houserule-grapple.js:58-60` (`GrappleMechanics.performGrappleCheck()`), the code that actually builds the `1d20 + grappleBonus` roll for the grapple house-rule action: `bab + strMod` **only** — no size modifier, no species bonus, no STR/DEX "better of" comparison (hardcodes STR), and doesn't read `system.derived.grappleBonus` at all. **Classification: CONFIRMED.** Any Small/Large creature, DEX-based grappler, or species with a grapple racial bonus gets a materially wrong number on the actual roll while the sheet displays the correct one. Already tracked as a known gap in `docs/audits/combat-phase-1a-ssot-decision-matrix.md:30` ("Grapple... Keep and later fix RAW seams") — not a fresh regression, but unresolved until now. **Fixed** to read `system.derived.grappleBonus`. Live-verified end-to-end through the real async `performGrappleCheck()` pipeline (`RollEngine.safeRoll` mocked to capture the formula string). See `tests/houserule-grapple-authority.test.mjs`.
- A fourth, chargen-preview-only formula exists (`follower-deriver.js:320`, STR mod alone) — lower stakes, scoped to the follower-creation wizard, not yet touched.
- Related: `getGrappleDC()`'s target-BAB read (`houserule-grapple.js:29`, was `target.system?.attributes?.bab?.value`) is a field this schema never populates (grep-confirmed zero writers anywhere) — always silently read 0, so the DC-scaling-with-target-BAB house rule (`grappleDCBonus`) never actually applied. **Fixed** to `SchemaAdapters.getBAB(target)`.

**Gar'ee's certified Grapple = +12 (BAB 7 + DEX +5 + size 0)** requires the DEX-vs-STR "better of" comparison (his DEX +5 > STR +2) — `houserule-grapple.js`'s STR-only formula previously computed `7 + 2 = 9` instead; now correctly reads the canonical `+12` when available.

**Certification-correction addendum 1 (post-review):** the first pass of this fix kept the exact known-wrong BAB+STR-only formula as its own fallback for when `system.derived.grappleBonus` is unavailable — a second review correctly flagged this as "use the right answer normally, but fall back to a known-wrong one," precisely what the freeze charter forbids. **Fixed in an intermediate pass**: extracted `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)` into `combat-stat-rules.js` (BAB via `SchemaAdapters.getBAB()`, best-of-STR/DEX via `SchemaAdapters.getAbilityMod()`, a new `GRAPPLE_SIZE_MODIFIERS` table, the same species-bonus path). This closed the fallback-formula problem but — per addendum 2 — did **not** yet make this the only grapple formula in the codebase.

**Certification-correction addendum 2 (post-review, SSOT completion): a THIRD review correctly found `derived-calculator.js`'s inline grapple block was still a second, independent implementation of the identical formula** (own copy of the size table, own best-of-ability comparison) — the intermediate fix above added a correct standalone resolver but never removed the original duplicate it was extracted from, so "one grapple formula" was not yet true despite being stated as such. **Fixed properly this time**: extracted the actual arithmetic into `combat-stat-rules.js#computeGrappleBonus({bab, strMod, dexMod, sizeMod, speciesBonus})` — a pure function over already-resolved numeric inputs, now the **only** grapple arithmetic expression in the codebase. `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)` resolves those inputs from a live actor (via `SchemaAdapters` + `getGrappleSizeModifier()`) and delegates to it; `derived-calculator.js` supplies its own freshly-computed current-pass inputs directly to the same function (deliberately *not* via `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)`, since some of those values may not be written back onto `actor` itself yet mid-pass, and re-reading them via `SchemaAdapters` could see stale prior-cycle data).

**Bonus discovery while doing this extraction: `derived-calculator.js`'s grapple computation has never actually worked.** It read `bab.total` for the BAB term, but `BABCalculator.calculate()` always returns a plain number (confirmed by reading every return path in `bab-calculator.js` and live-executing it), never an object — so `bab.total` was always `undefined`, and `system.derived.grappleBonus` was always `NaN`. This had **zero visible player impact** because `PanelContextBuilder.js`'s sheet-display fallback already guards with `Number.isFinite(grappleCandidate)` (line ~1435) and silently falls through to its own correct duplicate formula whenever the canonical value is `NaN` — exactly the kind of silent-fallback-masks-a-broken-canonical-source risk this migration's earlier ability-schema work also found repeatedly. Fixed as a natural side effect of passing the correct `bab` (not `bab.total`) into `computeGrappleBonus()`.

**Live-verified invariant, as of round 2** (`tests/grapple-bonus-ssot-parity.test.mjs`, 5 input combinations — STR>DEX, DEX>STR/Gar'ee-shaped, equal STR/DEX, Large size, a species grapple bonus): `computeGrappleBonus()` === `DerivedCalculator`'s real `system.derived.grappleBonus` (now a finite number, not `NaN`) === `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)` === the actual grapple roll modifier (`houserule-grapple.js`), across every case. This is the required parity the freeze charter's SSOT standard calls for — not "these currently agree," but "these cannot disagree because they're the same function."

**Certification-correction addendum 3 (post-review, round 3): SSOT was finally true, but the single source of truth was itself wrong, and two more live consumers still bypassed it.** A fourth review made the point the whole freeze exists to enforce: *agreement is not the standard the single source itself must also be correct.* Three separate findings, all fixed in this round:

**1. `GRAPPLE_SIZE_MODIFIERS` (`combat-stat-rules.js`) was never checked against a published rule and was wrong.** It used a step-of-4 table (`fine:-8 … colossal:+16`), copied forward from `derived-calculator.js`'s original inline formula with no book citation anywhere. Checked two independent ways:
- Two real, published creature stat blocks already present in this repo's own compendium data (`packs/beasts.db`): **Aiwha** (Gargantuan, BAB +3, STR 25/+7 mod, published Grapple modifier **+25** → back-solved size modifier = 25 − 3 − 7 = **+15**) and **Bantha** (Huge, BAB +2, STR 28/+9 mod, published Grapple modifier **+21** → back-solved size modifier = 21 − 2 − 9 = **+10**).
- Two independent SWSE rules-reference lookups of the Core Rulebook's Grapple size modifier table, both agreeing on the same values.

Both confirm the correct table is step-of-5 (`fine:-20, diminutive:-15, tiny:-10, small:-5, medium:0, large:+5, huge:+10, gargantuan:+15, colossal:+20`), **not** the step-of-4 table that had just been centralized. **Fixed**: `GRAPPLE_SIZE_MODIFIERS` corrected with a source-citation comment; golden tests for all 9 categories plus the Aiwha/Bantha cross-check live in `tests/grapple-size-modifier-book-values.test.mjs` (the Aiwha/Bantha check re-reads `packs/beasts.db` directly at test time and asserts the compendium's own BAB/STR/size fields haven't drifted, so the test can't silently stop meaning anything if that data changes later). This also required a companion fix to `tests/houserule-grapple-authority.test.mjs`'s Test 3, which had hardcoded the old (wrong) Large = +4 expectation.

**2. Two more live consumers were still independently reconstructing the formula, not reading the canonical value — SSOT was not actually complete:**
- **`PanelContextBuilder.buildResourcesPanel()`** (the live context builder for the v2 character sheet's displayed Grapple box) still computed its own `BAB + max(STR,DEX) + local size table + species` and only used the canonical `derived.grappleBonus` when it read as non-zero — `Number.isFinite(grappleCandidate) && (grappleCandidate !== 0 || grappleFallback === 0)`. **This meant a legitimate canonical Grapple of exactly `0` could be silently discarded in favor of the private fallback formula whenever that formula returned something non-zero** — a real player-visible correctness bug, not just a duplication risk. **Fixed**: removed the private formula and local size table entirely; the box now shows `system.derived.grappleBonus` whenever finite (including `0`), falling back to the shared `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)` resolver — never a private formula — only for the brief window before derived data exists.
- **`scripts/combat/systems/grappling-system.js#_rollGrappleBonus()`**, the base every opposed Grab/Grapple/Pin/Trip/Throw/Crush/Escape check in `SWSEGrappling` (and its `grapple-runtime-patches.js` overrides, which call `this._rollGrappleBonus` and therefore inherit this fix automatically) is built on, was the most serious of the three: it computed `bab + ability + halfLevel + sizeMod + speciesGrapple`, adding **a half-heroic-level term the published Grapple formula does not have at all**, on top of yet a **third**, independently-wrong local size table (`_sizeMod()`: step-of-8, `fine:-16 … colossal:+16`) that agreed with neither the old centralized table nor the correct one. For Gar'ee (BAB +7, DEX +5, medium size 0 → certified +12), this path would have produced `12 + halfLevel(4) = 16` — a real, live mechanical discrepancy in actual opposed-grapple combat rolls, not a display-only issue. **Fixed**: `_rollGrappleBonus()` now starts from `system.derived.grappleBonus` when finite, else `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)`, and applies only genuinely contextual, non-static additions on top (talent/feat grapple-rule bonuses via `swseTalentGrappleBonus()`, and `MetaResourceFeatResolver.getGrappleResistanceBonus()` for `resistGrapple` mode) — the half-level term, the local `_sizeMod()` table, and the duplicate BAB/ability/species computation were removed entirely (dead code, no other caller referenced `_sizeMod` or the `useDex` context option it also supported, which nothing ever passed).

**3. Repository-wide search (per the review's item F) found a fourth, previously unclassified duplicate: `scripts/ui/combat-stats-tooltip.js#getGrappleBreakdown()`**, the hover-tooltip breakdown shown on the character sheet's Grapple stat. It computed `BAB + Strength-only + a local size table (step-of-4, the same wrong one as #1) + a dead, never-set system.grapple.miscMod field` (grep-confirmed zero writers anywhere in the codebase — an orphaned schema field), and never showed a species-bonus row despite the canonical formula including one. A DEX-based grappler like Gar'ee would see a tooltip breakdown crediting Strength (+2) instead of Dexterity (+5). **Fixed**: now decomposes the same `computeGrappleBonus()` inputs (BAB, whichever of STR/DEX is actually higher — correctly labeled, size via the corrected `getGrappleSizeModifier()`, species bonus when present) and displays the canonical `system.derived.grappleBonus` as the total whenever finite. See `tests/combat-stats-tooltip-grapple-authority.test.mjs`.

**Repository-wide search — remaining occurrences classified (none left unclassified):**
- `scripts/sheets/v2/character-sheet/concept-context.js:1849` and `scripts/apps/progression-framework/steps/follower-steps/follower-confirm-step.js:168` — **Category B (legitimate consumers)**: both display an already-computed `grappleBonus`/`stats.grappleBonus` value passed into them; no formula, no action needed.
- `scripts/migration/migrate-species-to-structured-rules.js` — **out of scope**: a one-time species-data-authoring tool that parses descriptive text like "+X species bonus on grapple checks" into `speciesCombatBonuses.grapple` metadata. Not a live formula; feeds the canonical formula's species-bonus input rather than competing with it.
- `scripts/apps/progression-framework/steps/summary-step.js:578` (`afterGrapple = beforeGrapple - beforeBAB - currentStrMod + afterBAB + afterStrMod`, the level-up wizard's before/after preview) — **Category C, low severity, not fixed this round**: a delta computed on top of the canonical `_getCurrentGrapple(actor)` (which itself correctly reads `system.derived.grappleBonus`), not an independent from-scratch formula. It only omits a size/species delta, which essentially never changes during a level-up. Flagged, not blocking certification.
- `scripts/apps/progression-framework/steps/summary-step.js:1166` (`grapple = bab + strMod`, a chargen-time "projected stats" preview shown before any actor exists) and `scripts/apps/progression-framework/adapters/follower-deriver.js:320` (`grappleBonus = abilities.str.mod`, follower-template stat generation) — **CONFIRMED defects, deliberately not fixed in this round**: both omit the DEX "better of" comparison, size modifier, and species bonus, structurally identical in kind to the bugs fixed above. Both are chargen/follower-creation-time previews with no live actor to read `system.derived.grappleBonus` from (unlike every other fix in this round, which touched live sheet/roll consumers on an existing, already-prepared actor) — fixing them correctly means threading size/species data through the chargen and follower-creation pipelines, which is out of scope for a Grapple-domain-only pass. **Recommended**: fold into Batch 2's already-queued follower-authority work (expanding item 3 from "follower Fortitude" to "follower/chargen combat-stat preview authority").
- `scripts/data/background-events.js:96` (the "Enslaved" background's "Grapple Survivor" special ability, `target:'grapple', value:2, requiresRuntime:false`) — **flagged here as an unresolved modifier-pipeline wiring question; fully traced, confirmed CONFIRMED DEFECT, and FIXED in round 4** — see "Certification-correction addendum 4" below.

**Live-verified invariant, round 3 (`tests/grapple-bonus-ssot-parity.test.mjs`, now 6 input combinations — STR>DEX, DEX>STR/Gar'ee-shaped, equal STR/DEX, Large size, a species grapple bonus, and a legitimate exact `0`)**: `computeGrappleBonus()` === `DerivedCalculator`'s `system.derived.grappleBonus` === `resolveGrappleBonus(actor)` === the grapple roll modifier (`houserule-grapple.js`) === the displayed sheet Grapple box (`PanelContextBuilder`) === `SWSEGrappling`'s opposed-check base (`_rollGrappleBonus`) — six call sites, one function, across every case including the `0`-discard edge case that motivated adding it.

**Wording standard, reaffirmed**: "one grapple formula" is claimed only once every consumer demonstrably calls the same function; **"rules-fidelity-certified" is claimed only once that one function's constants have been checked against a published source**, not merely asserted or pattern-copied forward from an earlier version of the code. Both standards are now met for Grapple.

**Certification-correction addendum 4 (post-review, round 4): the core formula was correct, but permanent static Grapple bonuses never reached it at all.** A fifth review pointed out that round 3 certified the *arithmetic* of Grapple but never audited whether every legitimate source of a Grapple bonus actually feeds that arithmetic. Tracing the "Enslaved" background's "Grapple Survivor" (+2 competence, permanent, not mode-gated) end-to-end through the real pipeline found it never reached a character's Grapple total at all, for two independent reasons that would have interacted dangerously if fixed in the wrong order:

1. **Duplicate representation at the source.** `data/backgrounds.json`'s Enslaved record authors the same +2 grant twice — once in `mechanicalEffect` (`{type:'bonus', target:'grapple', value:2}`) and again in `specialAbilities[0]` (`{type:'bonus', target:'grapple', value:2, bonusType:'competence', abilityId:'enslaved-grapple-bonus'}`). `hydrateBackgroundEvent()` (`scripts/data/background-events.js`) doesn't introduce this — both fields already coexist in the raw authoring data. `BackgroundGrantLedgerBuilder._mergeBonuses()` faithfully turned this into **two** `flat` bonus entries for one real grant.
2. **The consumer silently dropped it entirely, independent of (1).** `ModifierEngine._getBackgroundModifiers()`'s flat-bonus loop required every record to have `Array.isArray(bonus.applicableSkills)` — a skill-bonus-only shape. Enslaved's records are `{value, target:'grapple'}` (no `applicableSkills`), so they always failed the guard and were `continue`d past. **Live-verified via the real pipeline** (`hydrateBackgroundEvent()` → `BackgroundGrantLedgerBuilder.build()` → `applyCanonicalBackgroundsToActor()` → `ModifierEngine._getBackgroundModifiers()`, reading the actual record from `data/backgrounds.json`, not a hand-typed fixture): the +2 was authored, merged (twice), and materialized onto `actor.flags.swse.backgroundBonuses.flat` — then discarded at the very last step. An Enslaved character's Grapple was silently 2 points low, on every roll and every display, with no code path anywhere adding it back.

**The trap the review named explicitly, and why fix order matters:** fixing (2) alone — teaching `ModifierEngine` to understand generic `target`-shaped flat bonuses — without first fixing (1) would have flipped a silently-missing +2 into a silently-wrong +4. **Fixed in the correct order:**
1. `BackgroundGrantLedgerBuilder._mergeBonuses()` (`scripts/engine/progression/backgrounds/background-grant-ledger-builder.js`) now establishes semantic identity per background: `specialAbilities`-derived flat bonuses (richer metadata: `abilityId`, `bonusType`) are collected first; a `mechanicalEffect`-derived bonus is only added if no `specialAbilities` entry for the same background already matches its `target`+`value`. Live-verified: Enslaved now produces exactly one `flat` entry.
2. `ModifierEngine._getBackgroundModifiers()` (`scripts/engine/effects/modifiers/ModifierEngine.js`) now handles the generic `target`-shaped record (creating one modifier targeting that raw key, e.g. `'grapple'`, with `type` taken from `bonusType` when it's a recognized `ModifierType`), in addition to the pre-existing `applicableSkills`-shaped one. **Defensively dedupes a second time**, by `backgroundId + target + value` (deliberately excluding `bonusType` from the identity key, since the two duplicate representations don't agree on it) — this protects any actor whose `flags.swse.backgroundBonuses` was already materialized and persisted *before* fix (1) existed, which would otherwise still carry a stale duplicate pair forever (materialization only re-runs on background reselection, not on every `prepareData()`). **Live-verified against exactly that stale-duplicate shape**: the defensive dedup collapses it to one +2, not two.

**Layering point and the static/contextual boundary audit (per explicit review requirement — every `GRAPPLE_BONUS`-shaped source individually classified):**
- `DerivedCalculator`'s grapple block (`derived-calculator.js`) now adds `'grapple'` to the same `allTargets` list already feeding `ModifierEngine.buildModifierBreakdown()` for skills/defenses/HP/BAB/initiative, and layers the resulting `modifierMap['grapple']` on top of `computeGrappleBonus()`'s core result **exactly once**, storing an explicit breakdown (`system.derived.grappleBonusParts = {core, staticModifiers, total}`) other consumers can decompose from rather than rediscovering.
- **Species grapple bonus** (`speciesCombatBonuses.grapple`) — **STATIC**, already correctly part of the core formula (unchanged).
- **Background grapple bonus** (Enslaved's Grapple Survivor) — **STATIC** (permanent, not mode-gated in its authored data) — now correctly layered as above.
- **Expert Grappler** (talent, `packs/talents.db`) — audited directly against its actual compendium data, not assumed: authored as a `GRAPPLE_BONUS` rule with `mode:'attackGrapple'` and an explicit `staticSheetPolicy:'manual_only'` annotation — **CONTEXTUAL by the data's own authorial intent**, correctly excluded from the static total. It is *not* dead code (an earlier pass of this same review mistakenly concluded `GRAPPLE_BONUS`/`swseTalentGrappleBonus()` had no live producer, having checked only the feat-normalization hooks and missed that this talent's compendium item ships the rule pre-authored) — it is the sole live `GRAPPLE_BONUS` source in the entire compendium (grep-confirmed against `packs/feats.db`/`packs/talents.db`/`packs/feat-catalog.db`) and was already being correctly consumed by `SWSEGrappling._rollGrappleBonus()`'s existing `swseTalentGrappleBonus()` call since round 3, unchanged. Live-verified: applies +2 only when `context.mode === 'attackGrapple'`, never in `resistGrapple` mode, never folded into `system.derived.grappleBonus`.
- **Grapple Resistance** (feat) — `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE`, `modes:['resistGrab','resistGrapple']` — **CONTEXTUAL**, correctly excluded from the static total and already correctly consumed via `MetaResourceFeatResolver.getGrappleResistanceBonus()` since round 3, unchanged. Live-verified: applies +5 only in `resistGrapple`/`resistGrab` modes.
- No source is eligible in both layers — confirmed by construction (the static layer only ever reads `ModifierEngine`'s aggregated `'grapple'` target, which nothing mode-gated writes to, since `GRAPPLE_BONUS`/`RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE` rules are consumed exclusively by contextual roll-time logic, never by `ModifierEngine`'s generic item-modifier collection) and live-verified with both a background bonus and Expert Grappler present on the same actor simultaneously — static total includes only the background's +2; `resistGrapple` mode shows only the static +2. **(This bullet originally also claimed `attackGrapple` mode correctly added Expert Grappler's +2 "on top, for +4 total" — that specific claim was wrong and is corrected by round 5 below: because both bonuses share the same bonus type (`competence`), the correct RAW result is +2, not +4. See "Certification-correction addendum 5.")**
- **Also newly found while auditing feats for other `target`-shaped grapple bonuses**: "Improved Grapple" (+5 on grapple checks, per its description) exists only as a `value:0, enabled:false, staticSheetPolicy:'exclude'` metadata placeholder — a genuinely missing feature (like Energy Shield ACP), not a math-authority duplication. Flagged for Batch 2, not fixed here.

**Tooltip ledger fix — the required `SUM(rows) === total` invariant.** `combat-stats-tooltip.js#_getModifiersForTarget()` had an independent, pre-existing bug found while wiring this: it read `actor.system.derived?.modifiers?.[target]?.modifiers`, but `DerivedCalculator` actually persists this at `system.derived.modifiers.breakdown[target].breakdown` (`ModifierEngine.buildModifierBreakdown()`'s real shape) — so this helper had always returned `[]` for every target that called it (`Grapple`, `BaseAttackBonus`, `Initiative`), regardless of any real modifier. **Fixed** the path/shape read. `getGrappleBreakdown()` now sources its static-modifier rows from this same corrected path — i.e., from the identical ledger `DerivedCalculator` used to build the canonical total — rather than any independent computation, so the rows can never disagree with the displayed total. The old test's synthetic `derivedGrappleBonus: 999` fixture (which only proved the tooltip *preferred* the canonical total, not that its rows summed to it) is superseded by `tests/grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`, which asserts `SUM(rows) === total` directly against real computed values across every golden case. Two related, pre-existing target-key bugs were found and left unfixed as explicitly out of scope for a Grapple-only pass: `BaseAttackBonus`'s modifier row remains inert because `'attack.bonus'` was never added to `DerivedCalculator`'s `allTargets` list at all, and `Initiative`'s modifier row queries the wrong key (`'initiative'` instead of the actual `'initiative.total'`) — both flagged for a future audit.

**Live-verified invariant, round 4** (`tests/grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`): across 6 static-composition golden cases (plain, Enslaved alone, species alone, Enslaved + species together, Large size, and a pre-fix duplicate-representation input proving the dedup) — `derived Grapple` === `sheet box` === `SUM(tooltip rows)` === `tooltip total` === `SWSEGrappling`'s neutral-mode opposed-check base === the grapple roll formula's static base, and the Enslaved contribution is exactly `+2` in every case, never `+4`. Separately, Expert Grappler and Grapple Resistance are each verified to add their bonus only in their gated mode, on top of the (unaffected) static total, exactly once. A fourth check reproduces the full real background pipeline end-to-end and confirms exactly one +2 grapple modifier reaches `ModifierEngine`.

**Wording standard, extended**: "static-modifier-composition-certified" is claimed only once (a) every legitimate static source of a Grapple bonus has been traced from its authoring data through to `system.derived.grappleBonus` and confirmed to arrive exactly once, (b) every contextual source has been confirmed to apply only at roll time and never leak into the static total, and (c) the tooltip's displayed parts have been proven — not merely asserted — to sum to the displayed total. All three are now true for Grapple.

**Certification-correction addendum 5 (post-review, round 5): static and contextual modifiers were being composed in two separate stacking universes.** A sixth review found the one remaining defect in round 4's design: `_rollGrappleBonus()` combined the static total and the contextual total as two plain **numbers** —

```js
let bonus = staticGrappleTotal;       // already stacking-resolved, but now just a scalar
bonus += swseTalentGrappleBonus(actor, mode);  // also just a scalar
```

— which discards each side's bonus `type` before they ever meet. This codebase's own modifier-stacking rules (`ModifierUtils.STACKING_RULES`) say `competence: 'highestOnly'` — two competence bonuses from different sources should never both apply, only the higher. But because the static Enslaved competence bonus (+2) had already been collapsed into a bare number by the time Expert Grappler's contextual competence bonus (+2) was added, the actual live result was **+4** in an `attackGrapple` roll, not the RAW-correct **+2**. **Live-reproduced exactly as described**: an actor with both sources present rolled `core + 4` instead of `core + 2`. The bug is specific to same-type collisions — an actor with only one of the two sources, or with sources of two *different* stacking types, was already correct, which is why rounds 1–4's testing never caught it.

**Fixed**: contextual Grapple contributions (`GRAPPLE_BONUS` talent/feat rules, `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE`) are no longer pre-summed to a number before combining with the static side. `collectContextualGrappleModifiers()` (renamed from `swseTalentGrappleBonus()`, in `grappling-system.js`) now returns modifier **objects** (`{source, sourceId, sourceName, target:'grapple', type, value, enabled, description}`, built with the same `createModifier()` factory `ModifierEngine` uses) for every mode-applicable rule on the actor's items. `_rollGrappleBonus()` now:
1. Reads the core term (BAB + higher of STR/DEX + size + species — which never stacks against anything) directly from `system.derived.grappleBonusParts.core`, not the combined total.
2. Reads the actor's already-resolved static modifier objects from `system.derived.modifiers.breakdown.grapple.applied` (the same array `DerivedCalculator` used to build the canonical total — not re-derived).
3. Unions the static and contextual modifier arrays and runs them through `ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()` — the **same shared stacking authority** every other domain in this codebase uses — followed by `ModifierUtils.sumModifiers()`.
4. Returns `core + modifierTotal`.

Re-running `resolveStacking()` over the union of an already-resolved static set and a freshly-built contextual set is safe: the function groups strictly by `type` and re-derives each group's result from scratch, so it doesn't matter that the static half was already reduced to its own per-type winner(s) before contextual entries of the same or different types are added to the pool. No Grapple-specific stacking logic was hand-coded anywhere — the fix is entirely "collect real modifier objects, hand them to the existing authority."

`Grapple Resistance`'s `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE` rule carries no explicit `bonusType` in its authored data, so it's treated as `untyped` (the same default this codebase applies everywhere else a bonus's type isn't stated) — `untyped` stacks with everything, matching its RAW behavior as an unlabeled feat bonus. `MetaResourceFeatResolver.getGrappleResistanceBonus()` itself was left untouched (it has one other, unrelated caller for a Reflex Defense bonus at grab-attempt time); `_rollGrappleBonus()` now derives its own `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE` modifier objects directly from the same `abilityMeta.grappleRules` data instead of calling that number-returning helper for the Grapple-check use case specifically.

**Live-verified invariant, round 5** (`tests/grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs`, rewritten): a full stacking-collision matrix — equal competence values (+2 vs +2 → +2, not +4, the exact round-4 regression), a higher contextual value replacing a lower static one (+4 beats +2), a lower contextual value failing to replace a higher static one (+2 beats +1), and a different-typed contextual bonus (`circumstance`) correctly composing additively alongside the static competence bonus rather than being capped by it. Every prior invariant (SSOT, book values, static-modifier composition, mode gating) is re-verified unaffected.

**Wording standard, extended again**: "stacking-certified" is claimed only once every contextual Grapple contribution is proven to be resolved against the actor's static contributions through the same shared stacking authority as everything else in the codebase — not merely added as a number on top of an already-collapsed total — across same-type collisions in both directions and at least one cross-type case. That is now true for Grapple.

**Certification-correction addendum 6 (post-review, round 6): the stacking arithmetic was correct, but one feat's rule metadata was itself semantically wrong, and the Grab attack that starts a grapple read the wrong Reflex Defense entirely.** A seventh review, reading the **real, current `packs/feats.db` data** (not a synthetic fixture), found two further confirmed defects at the boundary between Grapple and target-defense metadata — not in the stacking arithmetic itself, which this review explicitly confirmed solid.

**1. The `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE` rule type was overloaded to mean two different SWSE mechanics with one field.** Its real compendium text for two different feats:
- **Grapple Resistance**: "+5 Reflex Defense against Grab/Grapple attacks AND +5 to all opposed Grapple checks" — both channels, coincidentally the same value.
- **Grab Back**: "+2 Reflex Defense against Grab and Grapple attacks. If the enemy misses, [a reaction] counter-grab." — **the Reflex channel only, no opposed-check bonus at all.**

Both were authored with the identical single-field shape (`{type:'RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE', bonus:N}`), so round 5's `collectContextualGrappleModifiers()` — which reads this type for the *opposed-check* channel — incorrectly credited a Grab Back defender with +2 on their own opposed Grapple checks, a bonus that feat does not grant. This was invisible through round 5 only because Grapple Resistance's two channels happen to share a value.

**Fixed** by splitting the channels explicitly in the rule shape itself — `GRAB_GRAPPLE_RESISTANCE: {reflexBonus, opposedGrappleBonus, source}` — rather than special-casing either feat's name anywhere in consuming code. Every live producer of this metadata was migrated, all three found by repository-wide search, not assumed to be singular:
- `packs/feats.db` / `packs/feat-catalog.db` (the shipped compendium NDJSON records themselves).
- `data/feat-catalog.json` — a separate, independently-loaded live JSON array (confirmed consumed by `feat-registry.js`, `feat-pack-seeder.js`, `feature-index.js`, `compendium-pack-registration-repair.js`), carrying its own copies of the same two records.
- Two independent normalization hooks that can also *produce* this metadata at runtime: `grapple-feat-normalization-hooks.js#rulesForFeat()` (fires on item create/update) and a second, separate hook in `hp-recompute-hooks.js#HPRecomputeHooks.featureRuleNormalizationPatch()` (guarded by a non-source-specific `hasExistingGrappleRules()` check, confirmed dormant in practice for these two feats since the compendium already ships non-empty `grappleRules` — fixed anyway for consistency, in case it ever fires against a bare/legacy item).

Both consumers now read the correct channel: `MetaResourceFeatResolver.getGrappleResistanceBonus()` reads `reflexBonus` (its one live caller is `attemptGrab()`'s Reflex-Defense-vs-incoming-Grab check); `collectContextualGrappleModifiers()` reads `opposedGrappleBonus` (the wielder's own opposed-check roll). A legacy `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE`-shaped item (e.g. one already embedded on a persisted actor before this fix) is still read for backward compatibility, but **only for the Reflex channel** — deliberately never for the opposed-check channel, a conservative under-grant-never-over-grant choice for stale data rather than risking reintroducing the exact over-grant bug this fixes.

**Required tests, satisfied directly against real production data, not hand-authored fixtures alone**: `tests/grab-grapple-resistance-channel-split.test.mjs` reads the actual `packs/feats.db`/`packs/feat-catalog.db`/`data/feat-catalog.json` records for both feats and asserts their real, shipped `reflexBonus`/`opposedGrappleBonus` values, in addition to unit-level checks of both consumers (including the legacy-shape backward-compatibility path) and an end-to-end `SWSEGrappling._rollGrappleBonus()` check proving Grab Back contributes exactly `0` to `resistGrapple` mode while Grapple Resistance contributes `+5`, together or alone.

**2. `SWSEGrappling.attemptGrab()` — the Grab attack that starts every grapple — computed the target's Reflex Defense with its own broken fallback chain, never reaching the canonical V2 authority.** The exact code:

```js
Number(target.system?.defenses?.reflex?.total
    ?? target.system?.defenses?.reflex ?? 10)
```

`system.defenses.reflex` is a field the V2 character pipeline never populates (`DerivedCalculator` writes only to `system.derived.defenses.reflex`) — so for an ordinary V2 PC, this silently collapsed to the hardcoded fallback of `10`, regardless of the character's real Reflex Defense. A PC with Reflex 29 could have their Grab defense evaluated against 10 — a materially wrong hit/miss outcome, live-reproduced directly: with the broken code, a total of 20 always hit any V2 PC regardless of their real Reflex.

Separately, this was **a duplicate hit computation**. `SWSERoll.rollAttack()` — audited directly per this round's requirement — already wraps the canonical `attacks.js#rollAttack()`/`AttackOutcomeResolver.resolve()` pipeline ("the single authority for hit/critical/natural-1/natural-20 interpretation... every consumer reads the same verdict," per that resolver's own header) and already returns `isHit` and `targetReflex` on its result, computed via the same canonical `system.derived.defenses.reflex.total` path (`attacks.js#getTargetReflex()`). `attemptGrab()` discarded both and recomputed hit/miss itself against the broken Reflex read above — exactly the "second independent hit calculation" this round's audit asked to rule out.

**Fixed**: `attemptGrab()` no longer recomputes hit/miss at all. The Grab/Grapple-specific Reflex bonus (Grapple Resistance/Grab Back's `reflexBonus` channel, above) is passed **into** `SWSERoll.rollAttack()` via a new, minimal, purely additive `targetContext.defenseAdjustment` option on `attacks.js#resolveTargetContext()` (defaults to `0`; every pre-existing caller that doesn't pass it is unaffected — zero behavior change elsewhere), so the canonical resolver computes the hit **once**, against the correct base defense plus the contextual bonus. `attemptGrab()` then reads `attackResult.isHit` and `attackResult.targetReflex` directly, deriving `baseReflex = targetReflex - grappleResistance` for display/logging rather than any independent recomputation. The existing `'manual'` targetContext mode was deliberately *not* reused for this, since it nulls the resolved `target` reference entirely — which would have broken `buildReactionContextForAttack()`'s reaction-context building for the real target, a regression this round identified and avoided rather than introduced.

**Flagged, confirmed, deliberately out of scope**: while live-verifying this fix, `attacks.js#getTargetReflex()` (the canonical resolver every weapon attack in the game already uses, not something this round introduced) was found to check `system.defenses.reflex.total` **before** `system.derived.defenses.reflex.total` — whereas `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal(actor, defense)` (`scripts/utils/schema-adapters.js:415-419`), a separate helper used elsewhere, reads **only** `system.derived.defenses[normalized].total`. These two canonical-ish helpers disagree on priority order. This is pre-existing, shared by every attack in the game (not Grapple-specific), and out of scope for this pass — but is recorded here, not silently dropped, as a confirmed finding for a future audit. (It does not affect this round's fix: every real V2 PC actor has an empty/absent `system.defenses`, per `DerivedCalculator` never writing to that path at all, so `getTargetReflex()` already falls through to the correct derived value for the population this fix targets.)

**Required test cases, satisfied**: `tests/attempt-grab-reflex-defense-authority.test.mjs` live-executes the **real** production pipeline end-to-end (`attemptGrab` → `SWSERoll.rollAttack` → `attacks.js#rollAttack` → `AttackOutcomeResolver`, with only genuinely-external Foundry framework side effects stubbed: chat-card template rendering and a random-ID generator) across the required golden matrix: an ordinary target (derived Reflex 29), a Grab Back target, a Grapple Resistance target, both feats together, derived Reflex exactly `0`, derived Reflex exactly `10` (proving this is read as a real value, not the old coincidental hardcoded fallback), and a half-initialized `system.defenses = {}` object (present but empty) correctly falling through to the canonical derived value via the same shared resolver, not a second independent chain in `attemptGrab()` itself. Every case asserts both a miss at `expectedReflex - 1` and a hit (meet-or-beat) at exactly `expectedReflex`.

**Round 5's stacking arithmetic is unaffected and re-verified.** Both new test files, plus a fixture correction to `tests/grapple-static-modifier-composition.test.mjs` (its "Grapple Resistance applies contextually" case still used the pre-round-6 `RESIST_GRAB_AND_GRAPPLE` shape, which after this round's fix no longer contributes to the opposed-check channel — updated to the new `GRAB_GRAPPLE_RESISTANCE` shape, no logic changed), confirm Enslaved's static +2 competence and Expert Grappler's contextual +2 competence still resolve to +2 total via `ModifierUtils.resolveStacking()`, never +4 — the exact invariant round 5 established.

**Wording standard, extended once more**: this round's findings sat specifically at the boundary between Grapple's (already-certified) modifier arithmetic and target-defense metadata correctness — a distinct failure class from anything rounds 1–5 addressed. Both are now fixed and covered by tests that read real production data (compendium records, not only hand-authored fixtures) and live-execute the real attack pipeline (not a reimplementation). Grapple's certification is extended to cover this boundary; no open defect remains in this domain as of round 6.

**Certification-correction addendum 7 (post-review, round 7): the "no open defect remains" claim in round 6 was premature — the deeper review that round 6 itself invited found three further live calculation/rules defects, plus one normalization completeness gap.** An eighth review confirmed round 6's own fixes (channel split, `attemptGrab()`'s canonical delegation) and round 5's stacking arithmetic are sound, but found the Grapple domain could not yet be called fully certified. All four items below are fixed in this round.

**1. Opposed Grapple ties resolved backwards.** `SWSEGrappling.grappleCheck()` used `atkRoll.total > defRoll.total` for `attackerWins`, and — worse — a separate `if (isTie) return result;` early return that discarded state advancement even when `attackerWins` would already have been `true` on a tie under a corrected `>=` comparison. `_opposedGrappleForManeuver()` (the shared helper Trip and Throw both call) had the identical strict-`>` bug. SWSE's opposed Grapple check uses the general d20 "meets or beats" rule: the attacker succeeds when their result equals **or exceeds** the defender's — this is not an edge case; a tie is a common, unremarkable roll outcome. This exact defect was already flagged in the repository's own pre-existing audit, `docs/audits/combat-phase-0f-grapple-ion-seam-ledger.json`'s `grapple-opposed-meets-beats` entry (severity: high), which had gone unreconciled through rounds 1–6. **Fixed**: a single shared decision authority, `resolveOpposedGrappleOutcome(attackerTotal, defenderTotal)` (exported from `grappling-system.js`), used by both `grappleCheck()` (and therefore `attemptPin()`, which reuses it directly) and `_opposedGrappleForManeuver()` — no `>=` re-coded independently at either site, and the tie-specific early return removed entirely. `isTie` is still computed and reported for chat-card wording (updated so a tie reads "X wins! (tie goes to the attacker)" rather than the now-incorrect "Tie — no state change."), but never gates the outcome. Live-verified end-to-end through the real `grappleCheck()`, `attemptPin()`, `_opposedGrappleForManeuver()` (both `'trip'` and `'throw'`), and `tripGrappledOpponent()` (both a tie-success and a genuine-loss case, proving no false positive was introduced) — see `tests/opposed-grapple-tie-authority.test.mjs`.

**2. Grabbed/Grappled/Pinned applied a bogus flat `-5 Reflex Defense` penalty that appears nowhere in the published rules and that `DefenseCalculator`'s canonical Reflex total does not even read as an input.** `GrappleStateEngine`'s `stateConfig()` applied a Foundry ActiveEffect `ADD`-mode change (`system.defenses.reflex.bonus += -5`) to all three states. Grep-confirmed: `reflexState.bonus` (the field this change targets) is never consulted anywhere in `defense-calculator.js`'s Reflex computation — this was, at best, an inert write for any prepared V2 actor, and at worst a landmine for any future/legacy code path that did read it. The real rules: Grabbed and Grappled carry **no** Reflex penalty at all (their real restriction is "cannot move; -2 on attack rolls except natural/light weapons" — an attack-roll effect, not a defense one, and still unimplemented as its own separate, deferred item); Pinned loses its **positive** Dexterity bonus to Reflex Defense specifically — a component-aware reduction, not a flat number (a Dex -1 character loses nothing further; a Dex +5 character loses exactly 5). This also reconciles two more entries in the same pre-existing audit: `grapple-grabbed-reflex-penalty` (the flat -5 on Grabbed) and half of `grapple-pinned-condition-track-override` (that entry's condition-track-override half was already superseded before this round — `GrappleStateEngine.advancePair()`'s `'pinned'` branch already carries an explicit "do not mutate condition track" comment and touches no condition-track field at all; only its Reflex-penalty half, which had already drifted from -10 to -5 by round 6 without becoming correct in kind, remained open). **Fixed**: all three states' `changes` arrays are now empty (no ActiveEffect Reflex change at all); Pin's real, component-aware Dex-bonus removal is computed directly in `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` — `pinnedReflexDexReduction = actorHasGrappleState(actor,'pinned') ? Math.max(0, reflexAbilityMod) : 0`, applied only to the primary Reflex total (never double-applied to `flatFootedTotal`, which is computed from the same pre-Pin baseline since flat-footed already achieves the same "no positive Dex" result on its own) — mirroring the exact philosophy already certified for flat-footed Reflex's own Dex-bonus removal, not a second, competing formula. The reduction is exposed as an explicit, provable `reflex.pinnedDexReduction` breakdown field so `base + abilityMod + ... + pinnedDexReduction` still sums to `total`, per this project's own "every displayed number must be provably composed from its parts" standard. A new dependency-free leaf module, `scripts/engine/combat/grapple-state-query.js`, holds the actual state-lookup logic (`normalizeGrappleState`, `getGrappleEffects`, `actorHasGrappleState`, `getGrappleStateInfo`) so `GrappleStateEngine` (actions/mutation) and `DefenseCalculator` (read-only defense math) share one state-lookup authority instead of each maintaining a copy — required specifically because `GrappleStateEngine` transitively imports `ActorEngine → DerivedCalculator → DefenseCalculator`, so `DefenseCalculator` importing `GrappleStateEngine` directly would have created a circular import. Live-verified via `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` directly (not a reimplementation) across Grabbed/Grappled (unaffected) and Pinned at Dex contributions of +5, +1, +0, and -1 (proving a Dex penalty is never removed, matching flat-footed's own precedent) — see `tests/grapple-state-defense-effects.test.mjs`, which also includes a source-text regression guard against any grapple state ever reintroducing a quoted `system.defenses.reflex.bonus` ActiveEffect key.

**3. `attacks.js#getTargetReflex()`/`getTargetDefense()` — flagged in round 6 as a confirmed-but-deferred, "not Grapple-specific" finding — is fixed this round rather than left deferred further, since it is a live authority violation affecting every attack in the game.** The priority order checked the legacy `system.defenses.<key>.total` **before** `system.derived.defenses.<key>.total` — backwards from `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()`, the V2 canonical authority, which reads only the derived path. A stale legacy value (surviving an import or a prior sheet edit — V2's own `DerivedCalculator` never writes `system.defenses.<key>.total` at all) could therefore beat the real, current, derived defense for **any** attack against that target — the same category of stale-field risk this whole freeze exists to catch (the ability-schema migration's own `system.abilities` precedent is the same shape of bug). **Fixed**: derived-first for Reflex, Fortitude, and Will alike, exactly matching `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()`'s priority for a prepared V2 character actor; the legacy field remains a fallback only when derived is absent/non-finite — the genuinely-legacy actor-type case (a bare NPC/vehicle statblock that never runs the V2 derived pipeline). Separately, `roll.swseAttackContext.defenseAdjustment` (the audit-trail field a future reviewer would use to verify what contextual adjustment an attack actually applied) was hardcoded to `0` even when `targetContext.defenseAdjustment` (round 6's Grab/Grapple-Resistance mechanism) was genuinely applied — an untruthful audit trail. **Fixed**: `resolveTargetContext()` now returns the `adjustment` it actually applied, and `roll.swseAttackContext.defenseAdjustment` reads that value instead of a hardcoded constant.
