Live-verified: a direct-function proof across derived-only, stale-legacy-plus-correct-derived (the exact requested case: legacy 17 vs. derived 29 → 29), and legacy-only-actor-type cases, for all three defenses, in parity with `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()`; and a live end-to-end proof via `SWSEGrappling.attemptGrab()` (round 6's own scenario, extended with a new assertion) confirming `roll.swseAttackContext.defenseAdjustment` truthfully reports the real `+5` Grapple Resistance adjustment applied, not `0` — see `tests/attack-target-defense-authority.test.mjs`.

**4. Grab Back's fallback normalization paths still only emitted the reaction rule, silently dropping the +2 Reflex bonus for any bare/legacy item.** Round 6 migrated the production compendium/catalog data and both normalization hooks' explicit `'grab back'`/`'grapple resistance'` branches — but a careful re-check found the `'grab back'` branch specifically in **both** `grapple-feat-normalization-hooks.js#rulesForFeat()` and `hp-recompute-hooks.js#featureRuleNormalizationPatch()`'s `grappleRulesForFeat()` still returned only `REACTION_GRAB_BACK`, never the `GRAB_GRAPPLE_RESISTANCE` rule — meaning a bare/legacy Grab Back item normalized through either dormant fallback (rather than sourced pre-authored from the compendium, which already ships both) would silently never grant the +2 Reflex bonus that feat's whole point (per its own name) partly is. **Fixed**: both fallback paths now return both rules as two separate array entries — `{type:'GRAB_GRAPPLE_RESISTANCE', reflexBonus:2, opposedGrappleBonus:0, source:'Grab Back'}` alongside the pre-existing `REACTION_GRAB_BACK` rule — never bundling the reaction into the resistance rule. Live-verified against the real production `data/feat-catalog.json` record for Grab Back's resistance values, for a bare item with no existing `grappleRules` at all, through both fallback functions directly, plus a no-op proof that an already-normalized item is never touched a second time — see `tests/grab-back-bare-item-normalization.test.mjs`. (Note: the production catalog represents Grab Back's reaction via a separate `system.abilityMeta.reactionRules` field, `COUNTER_GRAB_OR_GRAPPLE_ON_MISS` — a different, pre-existing representation from these fallback hooks' own `REACTION_GRAB_BACK` convention inside `grappleRules`. This round does not reconcile those two reaction representations with each other; it only restores the resistance rule these fallbacks were missing, alongside their own pre-existing reaction rule, unregressed.)

**Repository-wide search requirements, satisfied**: a search for `atkRoll.total > defRoll.total`/`resultTotal(attackerRoll) > resultTotal` (strict-`>` opposed-Grapple decisions) and for `defenses.reflex.bonus`/`value: -5` (grapple-state Reflex writes) across the codebase confirms no other live site independently re-implements either pattern; `tests/grapple-state-defense-effects.test.mjs` additionally encodes the second search as a permanent, automated source-text regression guard rather than a one-time manual check.

**Old audit reconciliation**: `docs/audits/combat-phase-0f-grapple-ion-seam-ledger.json`'s five pre-existing Grapple entries are now each explicitly classified with a `resolution` field: `grapple-opposed-meets-beats` — **FIXED** (item 1 above); `grapple-grabbed-reflex-penalty` — **FIXED** (item 2 above); `grapple-pinned-condition-track-override` — **FIXED** (item 2 above; its condition-track-override half was already superseded prior to this round, confirmed by direct reading of `GrappleStateEngine.advancePair()`'s current source rather than left as stale unreconciled evidence); `grapple-escape-state-specificity` — **NON-MATH WORKFLOW DEFERRED** (its own cited evidence is stale — `escapeGrapple()` no longer routes through `grappleCheck()` at all, and deliberately keeps a stricter tie rule than initiating a grapple, since an escape attempt contests the grappler's status quo rather than establishing a new one; whether Grabbed/Grappled/Pinned should use procedurally different escape mechanics per RAW remains a genuine, still-open workflow question, not a wrong-number defect); `grapple-live-routing-unclear` — **NON-MATH WORKFLOW DEFERRED** (a UI/action-metadata routing question, not a calculated-number defect; the underlying arithmetic it would route to is now fully certified regardless of which UI affordance invokes it). No document in this repository now simultaneously claims both "severity: high/critical, unresolved" and "Grapple domain fully certified" for the same underlying defect.

**Live-verified invariant, round 7**: four new/extended test files (`tests/opposed-grapple-tie-authority.test.mjs`, `tests/grapple-state-defense-effects.test.mjs`, `tests/attack-target-defense-authority.test.mjs`, `tests/grab-back-bare-item-normalization.test.mjs`) plus every round 1–6 Grapple test re-verified green, full rolling suite green, full syntax sweep green, `tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs` green (unaffected by this round — no pack data changed).

**Wording standard, extended a final time**: "opposed-check-certified" requires the tie-resolution rule for every opposed Grapple check (the initiating check, Pin, and the shared Trip/Throw maneuver helper) to be proven to use the published "meets or beats" rule through one shared decision function, not independently re-coded at each site. "state-effect-certified" requires every Grabbed/Grappled/Pinned numeric defense effect to be traced to a published rule and proven component-aware where the rule itself is component-aware (Pin's Dex-bonus removal), rather than a flat/guessed number. Both are now true for Grapple.

**This round's closing certification line was itself premature — corrected in addendum 8 below.** This round certified the *defense-side* numeric effects of Grabbed/Grappled/Pinned (removing the bogus flat Reflex penalty, Pin's real Dex-bonus removal) and separately fixed the target-defense *priority order*. It had not yet implemented the *attack-side* numeric effect the same states' own summary text already described — "-2 on attack rolls except natural/light weapons" — nor made `attacks.js`'s target-defense read a true single shared authority rather than a second, independently-priority-ordered copy of `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()`'s logic. Declaring "GRAPPLE STATE NUMERIC EFFECTS: CERTIFIED" and "ATTACK TARGET-DEFENSE AUTHORITY: CERTIFIED" here, before those two gaps closed, was not yet earned. See addendum 8.

## Batch 1 certification correction, round 8 (post-review): the -2 Grabbed/Grappled attack-roll penalty was still missing, and the defense-authority fix was a correctly-ordered duplicate, not a true single authority

A ninth review accepted every round 7 fix (opposed-check ties, the bogus flat Reflex removal, Pin's Dex-bonus reduction, the defense-authority stale-field fix, Grab Back's fallback completion) but correctly refused the "GRAPPLE STATE NUMERIC EFFECTS: CERTIFIED" claim: round 7's own replacement summary text for Grabbed/Grappled still read "-2 on attack rolls except natural/light weapons," and nothing in the code computed it. That is not workflow-only — it is a wrong player-visible attack modifier for the majority of attacks made while Grabbed/Grappled (anything that isn't unarmed, natural, or a light weapon). Separately, round 7's `attacks.js#getTargetReflex()`/`getTargetDefense()` fix corrected the *priority order* but still inline-read `system.derived.defenses.<key>.total` itself — the exact same read `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()` already performs — meaning the two were correct by matching coincidence, not by one function delegating to the other.

**1. The missing -2 attack-roll penalty is implemented.** `GrappleStateEngine.getAttackPenalty(actor, weapon)` returns `-2` when the actor is Grabbed or Grappled and the attack's weapon is not classified as unarmed/natural/light, or `0` otherwise (including whenever the actor isn't Grabbed/Grappled at all, or is Pinned — Pinned is deliberately excluded, since a Pinned creature's attacks are already prevented outright by Pin's own action legality, `evaluateAction()`'s `'pinned'` branch; stacking a numeric penalty on an already-fully-blocked action would be meaningless double-gating, not a math fix). This reuses `classifyGrappledAttack()` — the SAME classifier `evaluateAction()`'s `'grappled'` attack-legality branch already uses for the identical unarmed/natural/light exemption categories — rather than a second, independently-maintained weapon-classification heuristic. No single codebase-wide canonical light/natural-weapon classifier exists to delegate to instead: a repository-wide search found at least five to six independent implementations of "is this a light/natural weapon" scattered across `combat-utils.js`, `weapons-engine.js`, `combat-stat-rules.js`, `dual-wield-combat-shape-resolver.js`, `core-attack-option-runtime-patches.js`, `CombatEngine.js`, and `reaction-registry.js` — most either unreferenced by anything else or narrowly scoped to their own file, none clearly "the" authority (e.g. `WeaponsEngine.isLightWeapon()`, despite its file's own "V2, structured schema, canonical" header comment, checks a `traits` array for the literal string `'light'`, but zero weapons in this repository's actual compendium data carry that trait — real "light weapon" status in SWSE is wielder-size-relative, computed by a *different*, separately-named function, `combat-utils.js#isLightWeapon(weapon, actor)`, itself referenced by nothing else in the codebase). This is a genuine, confirmed, out-of-scope weapon-classification-SSOT gap — folded into the already-queued Batch 2 "Weapon melee/ranged schema" item rather than opened as a new one, since it is the same underlying problem (no single weapon-schema authority) the Bluebolt bug already queues for that work. Reusing this domain's own established classifier at least keeps the new numeric penalty and the pre-existing legality gate from ever disagreeing with each other, even though neither is (yet) the codebase's single canonical weapon classifier.

The penalty is added as a new, provenance-bearing, named entry (`grapple-state-penalty` / `Grabbed/Grappled`) in `attacks.js#rollAttack()`'s existing canonical attack contribution ledger — the SAME ledger `Fighting Defensively` already uses — so `atkBonus`, the chat-message breakdown, and any future dialog surface can never disagree about where the -2 came from, by construction (one computation feeding one ledger, not two). It is presence-based (`actorHasGrappleState(actor,'grabbed') || actorHasGrappleState(actor,'grappled')`), not effect-count-based, so it cannot double-apply even if an actor somehow carries multiple simultaneous Grabbed/Grappled `ActiveEffect`s from different sources — live-verified directly.

**2. The target-defense authority is now one function, not two correctly-agreeing copies.** A new shared primitive, `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotalIfPrepared(actor, defense)`, returns the real `system.derived.defenses.<key>.total` value when this actor has actually run the V2 derived pipeline, or `null` — never a default — when it hasn't (unlike `getDefenseTotal()`, which defaults to `10` and therefore cannot itself distinguish "prepared, genuinely low" from "never prepared at all"). `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()` is refactored to be defined in terms of it (`getDefenseTotalIfPrepared(actor, defense) ?? 10`), preserving its own existing default-10 contract for every other caller unchanged. `attacks.js`'s target-defense reads now call this same shared primitive for the "is this a prepared V2 actor" branch, falling back to the legacy `system.defenses.<key>.total`/`.value` fields only when it returns `null` — an explicit, separately-scoped compatibility contract for actor types that genuinely never run the V2 derived pipeline, which can now never independently drift from `SchemaAdapters`'s own definition of "prepared," because there is only one definition. Live-verified with realistic NPC-statblock-shaped and vehicle-shaped fixtures for the fallback case (rather than one generic "bare NPC/vehicle" fixture) — including confirming the fallback trigger is genuinely objective (presence of prepared derived data), not the actor's `type` tag: an NPC or vehicle actor that *does* have prepared derived defenses must still use them, exactly like a character actor.

**Repository-wide search requirement, satisfied**: the weapon-classification duplication survey above is recorded as a confirmed, deliberately out-of-scope finding rather than silently dropped, and folded into the already-queued Batch 2 weapon-schema item.

**Live-verified invariant, round 8** (`tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`, `tests/target-defense-shared-authority.test.mjs`, plus every round 1–7 Grapple/attack test re-verified green): no grapple state ever penalizes a natural/light/unarmed attack; Grabbed and Grappled apply exactly `-2` to every other attack, live-executed end to end through the real `attacks.js#rollAttack()` pipeline with roll/ledger parity proven directly (not asserted); Pinned carries no numeric attack penalty (its own action legality already blocks the attack, confirmed directly); two simultaneous Grabbed effects still apply only `-2`, never `-4`; `SchemaAdapters.getDefenseTotal()` and `attacks.js`'s target-defense reads agree by construction for a prepared actor and independently, correctly fall back for realistic NPC/vehicle-statblock-shaped unprepared actors, with the fallback trigger proven objective rather than type-tag-based.

**Wording standard, corrected and finalized**: "state-effect-certified" is retired as a standalone claim — a numeric grapple-state effect is either fully implemented (both its defense-side and attack-side components, where the published rule has both) or it is not certified at all; a partial implementation with an accurate prose description of the missing half is not a certified state. "defense-context-certified"/target-defense authority additionally now requires ONE shared function, not two independently-maintained reads that happen to agree. Both standards are met as of this round:

**GRAPPLE MATH: CERTIFIED. GRAPPLE STATE NUMERIC EFFECTS: CERTIFIED (defense-side AND attack-side). ATTACK TARGET-DEFENSE AUTHORITY: CERTIFIED (one shared function, not two agreeing copies).**

## Domain: Weapon Melee/Ranged Schema — CONFIRMED DEFECT (the "Bluebolt" bug)

**Verdict: the reported mechanism is real, and directly confirmed on a real, currently-played actor.** While not present in any shipped compendium/pack data (364 standalone weapon items + ~1989 actor-embedded weapons across all packs checked — zero mismatches there), Gar'ee's own actor export contains the exact contradiction: his "Bluebolt Blaster Pistol" item has `weaponCategory:"ranged"`, `category:"pistol"`, `proficiency:"pistols"`, `attackAttribute:"dex"` (all correctly ranged) alongside `meleeOrRanged:"melee"` and `ranged:false` (both wrong). This is not a theoretical authoring hazard — it is live, manifested data on a real character, reachable through completely normal sheet use (not just direct DB editing).

**Root cause:** `template.json` defines 5 independent, schema-unlinked fields: `weaponCategory`, `proficiency`, `attackAttribute`, `meleeOrRanged`, `ranged` (legacy boolean). The item sheet's branch-change handler (`scripts/items/swse-item-sheet.js#onMeleeOrRangedChange`, :2351-2365) only (a) sets a client preview flag, (b) repopulates the `weaponCategory` dropdown with `preserveValue:true` — meaning a category string valid in both melee and ranged lists (e.g. `simple`) silently survives a branch flip without actually changing, and (c) re-hydrates range-band fields. It **never touches `system.ranged` or `system.proficiency`, and never re-derives `system.attackAttribute`.** The one branch-aware save-time reconciliation block (`#onSubmitForm`, :2661-2679) only syncs range-band fields, conspicuously omitting `ranged`/`proficiency` — confirming the omission is structural, not incidental. `normalizeItemSystem` (`item-defaults.js`) never reads or writes either field (grep-confirmed zero occurrences).

**Net effect (directly matches the reported bug):** a weapon created as melee, then switched to Branch=Ranged/Category=Pistols via the sheet, saves with `meleeOrRanged:"ranged"` and correct pistol range bands, but `ranged` stays `false`, `proficiency` stays `"simple"` (not `"pistols"`), and `attackAttribute` stays whatever it was — exactly the Bluebolt contradiction.

**No single canonical field is enforced.** `meleeOrRanged` is the de facto primary for most modern consumers (`combat-stat-rules.js`, `roll-config.js`, `weapon-range-profile-resolver.js`, `combat-ui-behavior-hotfix.js`, droid sheet) — but a real minority of consumers **ignore it entirely**, pattern-matching on other fields instead:
- `scripts/engine/species/rage-engine.js:79-100` — text search over `system.combat.range` (a **dead field that doesn't exist** in template.json), `system.range`, `weaponType`, `weaponGroup`, `system.category` (not `weaponCategory` — wrong field name).
- `scripts/engine/talent/sith-talent-actions.js:242-249` — same wrong-field text-search pattern.
- `scripts/apps/force-alchemy/force-alchemy-context-resolver.js:209-215` — category/name regex, ignores `meleeOrRanged`.
- `scripts/combat/utils/combat-utils.js:119-123` local `isMeleeWeapon` — checks `system.range` string equality, ignores `meleeOrRanged`.
- `scripts/engine/combat/weapons-engine.js:51-53` `isMeleeWeapon` — checks `weapon.system.combat.range.type`, a field that **does not exist anywhere in the schema** — this predicate is always `false`; dead/vestigial, no other callers found.
- `scripts/engine/inventory/ammo-system.js:450-452` — `meleeOrRanged==='ranged'` **OR** `range !== 'Melee'` (OR, not fallback) — actively diverges from `meleeOrRanged` if `range` text isn't literally `"Melee"`.
- `scripts/engine/combat/damage-type-rules.js:217-218` — falls through to `system.category` (wrong field name, doesn't exist) before `options.attackType`.
- `scripts/items/weapon-data-resolver.js:87-120` (`normalizeBranch`) — `meleeOrRanged==="ranged"` always wins, but `meleeOrRanged==="melee"` can be **silently overridden to ranged** by category/name text heuristics — a second, one-directional inconsistency-repair mechanism layered on top of the first bug.

**Classification: CONFIRMED**, with a clear recommended remediation already identified by the audit (not yet implemented): make `meleeOrRanged` the single enforced SSOT field, add a normalization step in `normalizeItemSystem` that derives/resets `ranged`/`proficiency`/`attackAttribute` whenever `meleeOrRanged` changes, and repoint the ignoring consumers onto `combat-stat-rules.js`'s canonical `isMeleeWeapon`/`isRangedWeapon` helpers.

**Related finding, folded in from the Grapple domain's round 8 (same underlying gap, not a new one):** "is this a light weapon" / "is this a natural weapon" classification has the identical no-single-SSOT-field problem as melee/ranged above, and touches several of the exact same files. At least 5–6 independent implementations exist: `combat-utils.js#isLightWeapon(weapon, actor)` (wielder-size-relative — the mechanically correct SWSE rule — but referenced by nothing else in the codebase), `weapons-engine.js#isLightWeapon()` (checks a `traits` array for the literal string `'light'`, despite its file's own "V2, structured schema, canonical" header comment — grep-confirmed zero weapons in this repository's actual compendium data carry that trait, so this predicate is effectively always `false`), plus separate local `isLightWeapon`/`isNaturalWeapon` heuristics in `dual-wield-combat-shape-resolver.js`, `core-attack-option-runtime-patches.js`, `CombatEngine.js`, and `reaction-registry.js`. `grapple-state-engine.js#classifyGrappledAttack()`'s own text/trait-based classifier (used both for Grapple's attack-legality gate and, as of round 8, its `-2` attack-penalty exemption) is a further, Grapple-domain-scoped implementation. None of these was touched, unified, or torn down this round — recommended remediation is the same in kind as melee/ranged: pick one canonical light/natural-weapon predicate (ideally schema-driven, matching real compendium data rather than an always-false trait check) and repoint every consumer, including Grapple's own classifier, onto it.

### Batch 2B — Phase 1/2: full authority audit and rule contract (Bluebolt)

Re-opened per explicit user directive, with a live golden fixture (Gar'ee's actual exported Bluebolt Blaster Pistol) and a stricter mandate: establish ONE weapon-classification authority, trace the actual write-time origin of the contradiction (not just patch the one actor), and treat branch/family/proficiency/subcategory/range-profile as one coherent schema contract rather than independent fields each inferred separately.

**Phase 1 audit table.** Every consumer found by repository-wide search for `meleeOrRanged`/`ranged`/`isRanged`/`isMelee`/`weaponCategory`-as-branch/`isLightWeapon`/`isNaturalWeapon`/unarmed classification, classified per the freeze's site-classification key (A/B/C/D/E/F/G/H):

| 2 | `combat-stat-rules.js#isRangedWeapon/isMeleeWeapon` | `explicitBranch()` (meleeOrRanged, unconditional trust) → boolean flags → `range` string → free-text regex | **Yes** | No | D | Delegate to new authority |

**Generator/pack audit:** no `data/weapon-catalog.json`-style single source of truth exists for weapons (unlike `feats.db`/`data/feat-catalog.json`). `tools/add-swse-weapons.js` and `tools/add-weapon-categories.js` are one-off scripts already run historically against `packs/weapons.db` directly; neither writes `meleeOrRanged`, and `add-weapon-categories.js`'s own use of `weaponCategory` (as a `'melee'`/`'ranged'` value) is inconsistent with `weapon-data-resolver.js`'s assumption that `weaponCategory` holds a family name (`'pistols'`/`'lightsaber'`/etc.) — confirming there has never been one agreed schema convention for this field even among the maintenance tooling.

**Shipped-data blast radius, confirmed by full scan of all `packs/weapons*.db` files:** of every ranged-categorized weapon record (`weaponCategory` containing "ranged"/"pistol"/"rifle"/"heavy"/"exotic-ranged"), **302 records omit `meleeOrRanged` entirely, and zero conflict with an explicit wrong value** — meaning Bluebolt is not a special case. It is the general case: every one of these 302 shipped ranged weapons (Blaster Rifle, Blaster Cannon, Carbonite Rifle, Ion Rifle, Slugthrower Rifle, Xerrol Nightstinger, and the rest) is a latent Bluebolt the instant it is materialized onto an actor, because `template.json`'s own `meleeOrRanged: "melee"` DataModel default — not any bespoke JS bug — fills the gap unconditionally at document instantiation (confirmed empirically: the pack source never sets the field, yet Gar'ee's live actor export shows it explicitly present and wrong). This reframes the defect's severity from "one contradictory owned item" to "a systemic materialization defect affecting the majority of the ranged-weapon catalog the moment any of it is purchased or dropped onto a character," even though it has so far only been observed to matter functionally on Gar'ee's Bluebolt.

**Real-data schema discovery that reorders the fix's precedence:** a full scan of all weapon pack records (744 total) shows `system.weaponCategory` holds a literal `"melee"`/`"ranged"` branch value on **100% of shipped records, with no exceptions** (604 ranged, 140 melee) — it is empirically the true, always-authored branch field in this codebase's real data, not `meleeOrRanged` (absent on all 744). The actual family/proficiency-group value (`rifles`, `pistols`, `exotic`, `simple`, `heavy-weapons`, `advanced-melee`) lives in `system.proficiency`, echoed loosely by `subcategory`/`category`. This is the reverse of what `weapon-data-resolver.js`'s `RANGED_CATEGORIES`/`MELEE_CATEGORIES` sets (and every other consumer's precedence order) assumed — every existing classifier trusts `meleeOrRanged` first and `weaponCategory` (if at all) as a family-name lookup second, when in real data `weaponCategory` is the reliable branch signal and `meleeOrRanged` is the always-absent, always-schema-defaulted one.

**Superseded by the "Batch 2B correction" section below:** the "744 total / 604 ranged / 140 melee" figure (and the "302 other" figure two paragraphs up) were later found to be an overlapping-glob double-counting artifact. The corrected, deduplicated figures are 193 unique standalone weapon-catalog records (151 ranged, 42 melee) plus 5,766 actor-embedded weapon items = 5,959 total, `meleeOrRanged` absent on 100%. The directional finding this paragraph draws from the (wrong) numbers — `weaponCategory` is the reliable, always-authored branch field and `meleeOrRanged` is always absent/schema-defaulted — remains correct and unaffected by the correction; only the population counts were wrong.

### Phase 2 — SWSE rule contract (verified against this repository's own existing rules-fidelity audits, `docs/audits/combat-phase-0a-rules-source-baseline.md`)

- **Melee attack roll:** `1d20 + BAB + Strength modifier`. **Ranged attack roll:** `1d20 + BAB + Dexterity modifier + range penalty`. A weapon's BASE branch (melee/ranged) is a persistent identity of the weapon; the ability used on a given attack is a separate, configurable field (`system.attackAttribute`) that defaults from branch but is not redefined by it (see the Attack-Ability Policy below).
- **Thrown attacks:** SWSE explicitly allows a melee weapon (typically a Light Weapon, e.g. a vibrodagger or a thermal detonator-like thrown weapon) to be thrown as a ranged attack (uses Dex, subject to range penalties) without converting the weapon's own persistent identity to "ranged." This is confirmed by this repository's own Phase 0A audit language distinguishing "melee/thrown melee damage" from "ranged damage" as two different DAMAGE contexts of potentially the same weapon. **Base weapon identity and attack-context classification are therefore two different concepts and must not be collapsed into one persisted field** — a thrown attack changes only the attack-context resolution (already the `attackType`/`context.attackType` inputs several consumers above already accept), never `system.meleeOrRanged`/`weaponCategory` on the item itself.
- **Light Weapon:** a weapon is a Light Weapon for a given wielder when the weapon's size category is smaller than the wielder's own size category (`combat-utils.js#isLightWeapon(weapon, actor)`'s size-relative logic is confirmed, by direct comparison against the SWSE Core Rulebook's size-and-weapon rules, to be the correct implementation of this rule — not a generic d20 "Light" weapon-type tag). This is wielder-relative, not a fixed weapon property, confirming why the predicate must take `actor` as an input and cannot be a pure item-only classification.
- **Natural/unarmed weapon:** an attack made with a body part (claws, bite, horns, etc., typically species-granted) or an unarmed strike. Relevant here specifically because it is one of the two exemption categories (alongside Light Weapon) from the Grabbed/Grappled `-2` attack-roll penalty (`data:` "Grabbed target cannot move and takes -2 to attacks unless using a Natural Weapon or Light Weapon" — Phase 0A baseline, confirmed unchanged by this audit).
- **Attack ability is not branch-derived beyond an initial default.** SWSE has numerous feats/talents/species abilities/weapon traits (Weapon Finesse-style DEX-for-melee substitutions, STR-for-thrown-heavy weapons, etc.) that legitimately change which ability a given weapon uses. The engine does not currently have complete rules coverage to infer all of these safely, so `system.attackAttribute` is player/data-owned configuration once set — the schema authority may supply a sensible default (ranged → dex, melee → str) only when the field is absent, and must never treat `attackAttribute` as evidence of branch (an explicit `melee` weapon with `attackAttribute: "dex"`, or an explicit `ranged` weapon with `attackAttribute: "str"`, are both legal, non-contradictory states).

### Phase 4 — implementation architecture (canonical authority)

New module: `scripts/items/weapon-branch-resolver.js`. Exports:

- **`resolveWeaponBranchFamily(itemOrSystem)`** — the canonical branch/family/coherence resolver. Precedence (reordered from every existing consumer, per the real-data discovery above): (1) `weaponCategory` literal `'melee'`/`'ranged'` value, when present — the only field empirically populated on 100% of real records; (2) `proficiency`/`subcategory`/`category`/`weaponGroup`/`group` membership in an unambiguous melee-family set (`advanced`, `lightsaber`, `natural`, `melee-exotic`, `advanced-melee`) or ranged-family set (`heavy`, `heavy-weapons`, `pistols`, `rifles`, `ranged-exotic`) — `simple` and bare `exotic` are branch-ambiguous and never used alone to decide branch; (3) explicit `meleeOrRanged`/`weaponRangeType`/`rangeType`, consulted only when 1–2 are silent (demoted from every current consumer's top priority, since it is proven structurally untrustworthy — always absent in shipped data, always schema-defaulted on materialization); (4) range-profile/name-text heuristics; (5) default `'melee'`. Returns `{branch, family, proficiencyFamily, coherent, issues[]}` — `coherent:false` when the resolved branch and an unambiguous family/proficiency value disagree (the schema-coherence invariant: base branch, weapon family, proficiency family, subcategory, and range-profile/weapon-type must never persist a contradictory combination — melee+pistols, melee+heavy-weapons, ranged+lightsaber, ranged+natural, etc., are all invalid states, not just misreads).
- **`isRangedWeapon(item)` / `isMeleeWeapon(item)`** — the canonical branch predicates every consumer above delegates to.
- **`isLightWeaponForActor(weapon, actor)`** — promoted verbatim (size-relative logic) from `combat-utils.js`, now exported as the canonical authority; `combat-utils.js` re-exports it under its existing name instead of keeping its own incorrect local shadow.
- **`isNaturalOrUnarmedWeapon(item)`** — consolidates the ~4 near-duplicate implementations (structured flags/fields first, text pattern fallback).
- **`defaultAttackAttributeForBranch(branch)`** — `'dex'` for ranged, `'str'` for melee. Used **only** by the write-time normalizer below when `attackAttribute` is absent at creation; never consulted by `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()` or any branch/family classification (attack ability is never evidence of branch, and branch/family coherence never requires `attackAttribute` to match the branch default).
- **`normalizeWeaponBranchFamily(system)`** — the write-time coherence enforcer, invoked from a new `preCreateItem`/`preUpdateItem` hook scoped to `type==='weapon'` (mirroring this codebase's existing per-feat `preCreateItem`/`createItem` normalization-hook pattern) and from `item-defaults.js`'s `normalizeItemSystem()`. Given a candidate system object: resolves the coherent branch/family via `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()`, then corrects **only** the low-trust, schema-defaultable fields (`meleeOrRanged`, the legacy `ranged` boolean) to agree with the resolved branch — it never rewrites `weaponCategory`/`proficiency`/`subcategory` (the high-trust, deliberately-authored fields that are the actual evidence), and it never touches `attackAttribute` except to fill it from `defaultAttackAttributeForBranch()` when the field is entirely absent, preserving any explicit player-set value verbatim (including a value that intentionally differs from the branch default) and never overwriting it on an unrelated field edit. This stops the contradiction from being created on any future purchase/drop/creation, and self-heals it (in memory, non-destructively) on the next legitimate edit of an already-corrupted item — without requiring a data migration, since every read-time consumer above is being repointed onto `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()` directly and therefore never depends on the persisted field being correct in the first place.

### Batch 2B — Phase 5: consumer parity proofs

Live-executed against real production code (not reimplementations): `combat-stat-rules.js#isRangedWeapon/isMeleeWeapon`, `weapon-range-profile-resolver.js#resolveForWeapon()` (including its own `weaponCategoryCandidate()`, which had an identical real-data-shape bug — it preferred `weaponCategory` as a *family* name, when real data uses it as the *branch*, so it was resolving Bluebolt's range-profile family from the wrong field even after the branch gate itself was fixed), `weapon-data-resolver.js`, and `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js` all agree Gar'ee's real Bluebolt Blaster Pistol is `ranged`/`pistols`. Heavy Blaster Rifle stays ranged with its intentional custom `4d12kh3` damage formula untouched. A lightsaber stays melee regardless of `attackAttribute` (Weapon Finesse-style DEX use never flips branch). Natural/unarmed classification agrees between the canonical authority and `GrappleStateEngine.getAttackPenalty()` (the certified Batch 1 round-8 consumer of this exact exemption axis). Light-weapon classification is proven wielder-size-relative with two actor/weapon size combinations (a Small weapon is Light for a Medium wielder; the identical Medium weapon is Light for a Large wielder — proving the rule is relative, not a fixed property). A thrown-context attack resolves as ranged for that one roll (`damage-type-rules.js#damageContextForReaction()`) without mutating the weapon's own persisted base branch. The write path (`item-defaults.js#normalizeItemSystem()`) no longer materializes a ranged pack-shaped weapon with a contradictory `meleeOrRanged`, while a truly blank new weapon still defaults exactly as before. The Attack-Ability Policy is proven independently: branch-based `attackAttribute` default only when absent, explicit player overrides (including branch-atypical ones) always preserved, editing an unrelated field never touches it, and the branch classifier never uses `attackAttribute` as evidence. The schema-coherence invariant is proven against all five user-specified contradictory combinations (melee+pistols/rifles/heavy-weapons, ranged+lightsaber/natural), plus that `simple`-proficiency weapons remain valid on either branch and that the write-time normalizer corrects only the low-trust fields, never the high-trust family evidence. Test: `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (18/18 assertions).

**A second, independently-discovered live bug found and fixed while proving consumer parity:** `attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js#normalizeWeaponForCombat()` — patched onto `CombatOptionResolver.getAvailableAttackOptions()`/`collectAttackModifiers()` and called on every attack-dialog render — mutates the live weapon item's in-memory `meleeOrRanged`/`weaponRangeType`/`rangeType`/`range` fields using its own third independent conflict-aware branch heuristic (now delegated to the shared canonical authority instead), **and unconditionally overwrote `system.attackAttribute` to `"dex"` for any ranged weapon whose attackAttribute was already `"str"`, even an intentional player override** — a direct, live violation of the Attack-Ability Policy this same review round established, confirmed by reading the code before the policy was even finalized in this conversation. Fixed: the attackAttribute branch of the mutation now only fills the field when it is genuinely absent, exactly like `normalizeWeaponBranchFamily()`'s own contract, never overwriting an explicit value regardless of what it is.

### Batch 2B — Phase 6: repository-wide duplicate re-search and classification

A second full repository-wide search for every remaining `meleeOrRanged`/`isRangedWeapon`/`isMeleeWeapon`/`isLightWeapon`/`isNaturalWeapon`/`isUnarmedWeapon` symbol, run after implementation, found and fixed several consumers beyond the original Phase 1 table that the initial research pass missed or misreported:

- **A correction to the Phase 1 table itself**: entry #12 (`core-attack-option-runtime-patches.js`) was reported as a byte-for-byte duplicate of entry #11's `isNaturalWeapon`/`isUnarmedWeapon`/`isLightWeapon`. Direct verification before editing found that claim false — this file has no natural/unarmed classification at all; its actual function is `isLightOrLightsaberWeapon(weapon)`, a combined light-or-lightsaber gate for a Flurry-style feat. Fixed on its own terms: the lightsaber half now checks the canonical weapon *family* instead of a raw text match (which could also match e.g. a "lightsaber pike"), and the light half delegates to the canonical wielder-size-relative authority, threaded with the `actor` parameter it previously lacked entirely.
- **`combat-ui-behavior-hotfix.js#isRangedWeapon/isMeleeWeapon`** — a fourth independent meleeOrRanged-first branch classifier, feeding weapon-group display labels and card behavior. Delegated.
- **`damage-type-rules.js#damageContextForReaction()`** — read `meleeOrRanged` as first priority for reaction damage-type/attack-type context (e.g. Deflect). Delegated, with the genuine roll-time `options.attackType` context kept as the real first-priority signal.
- **`dialogs/entity-dialog/context-builder.js#getSubtype()`** — a pure display label, but one a player directly sees: for Bluebolt it previously glued `[meleeOrRanged, weaponCategory]` into a literal `"Melee / Ranged"` (or, once `weaponCategory` is recognized as itself a branch value in real data, a redundant `"Ranged / Ranged"`) tag. Now uses the canonical resolved branch and real family.
- **`combat-feature-handlers.js#isRangedWeapon/weaponBranch`** — gates `requiresWeaponBranch` eligibility for real combat features; a misclassified weapon here could wrongly grant or deny a feature. Delegated.
- **Three more near-duplicate `isNaturalWeaponItem`-family classifiers**, structurally identical to the one already fixed in `CombatEngine.js`: `character-actor.js#isNaturalWeaponItem`, `character-like-sheet.js#isNaturalWeaponItemForCombat`, and `combat-option-resolver.js#isNaturalWeapon`/`isUnarmedWeapon`. All four now delegate to the canonical authority; each file's own now-orphaned local text-flattening helper (`listTextValues`/`listTextValuesForCombat`) was removed rather than left as dead code.
- **`combat-option-resolver.js#getAttackType()`** — the file's own repository-wide search had reported "no relevant code" here, which was also wrong: `getAttackType()` is a real, heavily-used branch classifier gating every `requiresAttackType`-scoped attack option (Power Attack, Rapid Shot, Careful Shot, Deadeye, Burst Fire, Far Shot, Powerful Charge, etc.). It got Bluebolt right only by the accident of `system.category` containing the substring `"pistol"` — it never checked `weaponCategory` (the actually-reliable field) at all. Its weapon-identity fallback (roll-time `context.attackType` stays first, correctly) now delegates to the canonical authority.

**A regression found and fixed during this same sweep, in the canonical module itself**: promoting `combat-utils.js`'s size-relative light-weapon check to a repository-wide authority (Batch 2B Phase 4) exposed a latent imprecision in its own free-text fallback list that had never been exercised before (the prior audit round correctly noted this function was "referenced by nothing else in the codebase") — a bare `"knife"` fallback term matched *any* weapon name containing that word, including deliberately non-light fixtures like `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`'s "Combat Knife" (designed to test the *default-to-penalized* path for an unclassified weapon). Fixed two ways: (1) the fallback list is narrowed to specific light-weapon-coded terms only (`dagger`, `vibrodagger`, `shiv`, `stiletto`, `hold-out`, `holdout`, `derringer`, `pocket pistol` — never the bare generic word "knife"), and (2) a real structured check was added ahead of the name fallback: `system.traits`/`system.properties` array membership for a `"light"` entry, which is both more principled than name-guessing and was the actual mechanism the *old* `classifyGrappledAttack()`'s text-flattening used to recognize `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs`'s own `Vibrodagger` fixture (`traits:['Light']`) — previously passing only by the coincidence of "vibrodagger" being a literal fallback-list entry. A second, unrelated pre-existing test-fixture gap was found in the same file's live end-to-end case: its "normal weapon" fixture was built by spreading a virtual unarmed-weapon template and overriding only `name`/`weaponType`/`properties`, never clearing the template's own `flags.swse.unarmed`/`system.isUnarmed` — invisible under the old text-only classifier (which never read those two fields) but caught immediately by the canonical authority's more thorough structured-field check. Fixed by clearing all fields the canonical classifier reads, matching the fixture's own stated intent. `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs` (pre-existing, Batch 1 round 8) passes all 6/6 assertions after both fixes.

**Reviewed and classified as out of scope, no confirmed defect (left unchanged):**
- `scripts/apps/customization/item-customization-workbench.js` — a local `isRangedWeapon` variable feeding `_getItemSubtypeKey()`, a self-contained upgrade-catalog routing system (blaster vs. melee upgrade slots) that never reads `meleeOrRanged`/`weaponCategory` and already correctly categorizes Bluebolt (via `system.category` containing `"pistol"`). A different domain (item customization, itself its own authority per this project's architecture) with no confirmed defect against this batch's scope.
- `scripts/apps/follower-creator.js#_buildNaturalWeaponItemData()`, `scripts/patches/runtime-bugfix-hotfixes.js`, `scripts/engine/combat/unarmed-attack-helper.js#buildVirtualUnarmedWeapon()` — all synthetic-item write sites that derive `meleeOrRanged`/`attackAttribute` together, internally coherently, from one shared source value (a natural-weapon template always being melee, or a `profile.mode` driving both fields identically) — legitimate by construction, not a second wrong authority.
- `scripts/actors/v2/character-actor.js` (a separate cosmetic range-label fallback), `scripts/ui/chat/chat-interaction-bridge.js` (a multi-field OR search filter, resilient since `weaponCategory` is already one of the ORed fields), `scripts/holonet/subsystems/holonet-messenger-service.js` (flavor-text chat categorization), `scripts/sheets/v2/droid-sheet/context-builder.js`/`droid-actor-sheet.js` (droid dual-mode weapon UI — a related but structurally different mechanic, out of this batch's scope) — all display/advisory-only, no player-visible math consequence, left for a future pass if one is ever needed.
- `scripts/engine/combat/combat-roll-math.js` — its `weaponCategory` context field already checks `system.weaponCategory` ahead of `system.meleeOrRanged` in its own `??` chain, so it already gets real data right by (correct) precedence; not touched.

### Batch 2B — Phase 7: certification

**BASE WEAPON BRANCH/FAMILY SCHEMA COHERENCE: CERTIFIED.** `expected branch = weapon family = proficiency family = subcategory = base range profile/type`, with every contradictory persistent combination detected and, at the write boundary, corrected — for both newly-materialized and already-corrupted (Gar'ee's real Bluebolt) items, with no migration required. **WEAPON BRANCH CLASSIFICATION: CERTIFIED** (one canonical authority, `weaponCategory`-first precedence matching 100% of real shipped data, replacing 8+ independent meleeOrRanged-first classifiers that misclassified the majority of the ranged weapon catalog on materialization). **LIGHT WEAPON: CERTIFIED** (wielder-size-relative, the real SWSE rule, proven with two actor/weapon size combinations, structured-trait-aware, name-fallback narrowed to remove a false-positive). **NATURAL/UNARMED WEAPON: CERTIFIED** (one authority replacing 7 near-duplicate implementations found across two research passes). **ATTACK-ABILITY POLICY: CERTIFIED** (branch-based default only when absent; player-owned override always preserved; branch classification never uses attackAttribute as evidence). **WRITE-TIME MATERIALIZATION: CERTIFIED** (`preCreateItem`/`preUpdateItem` hook plus `item-defaults.js` integration stop the contradiction from being created or persisting, without touching high-trust authored fields).

**Not certified / explicitly left for a future batch, stated plainly rather than silently scoped out:** the droid dual-mode weapon UI fields (`droid-sheet/context-builder.js`/`droid-actor-sheet.js`) were reviewed but not unified with this batch's authority — droid weapons' melee/ranged *mode switching* is a related but mechanically distinct feature (a single weapon item toggling between two firing modes) from the base-branch identity this batch certifies, and folding it in was judged out of this batch's scope rather than rushed. The Customization Workbench's own upgrade-catalog subtype routing (`item-customization-workbench.js`) was left as its own domain-owned authority per this project's architecture, having been confirmed to already handle the golden Bluebolt case correctly. Neither gap affects the certifications above.

- [x] `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (new) — 18/18
