### Batch 2B correction: independent review findings

An independent review of commit `9077e4995a5c3317b086019fba81780bef0d3b9e` (one commit ahead of the `0ae630f` state certified above, CI green) found the Phase 7 certification premature and identified three correction blockers, all confirmed valid by direct re-reading of the code before any fix was made.

**Blocker 1 — schema coherence was detected but not enforced.** `normalizeWeaponBranchFamily()` as shipped in the original Batch 2B round corrected only `meleeOrRanged`/`ranged`/`attackAttribute` at write time — never `proficiency`/`subcategory`/`category`/`weaponGroup`/`group`/`rangeProfile`/`weaponType`. The batch's own test file asserted this as correct behavior (`rangedToMelee.proficiency === 'pistols'` surviving a melee-branch normalization), which is exactly the contradictory persisted state the schema-coherence invariant exists to prevent. Fixed: `normalizeWeaponBranchFamily()` now scans every family-bearing field (`FAMILY_FIELDS`) and every range-descriptor field (`RANGE_DESCRIPTOR_FIELDS`) against the resolved branch and resets any field whose implied branch disagrees to `BRANCH_NEUTRAL_FAMILY = 'simple'` (family fields) or `''` (range-descriptor fields) — `'simple'` chosen because it is the one proficiency/category value valid on *both* `MELEE_WEAPON_CATEGORY_OPTIONS` and `RANGED_WEAPON_CATEGORY_OPTIONS` in `weapon-data-resolver.js` (the editor's own fallback). This single function now correctly handles both an explicit branch-changing edit and a legacy-corrupted item's next save, uniformly, since the only real difference between those two cases (already-agreeing fields are left untouched; disagreeing fields are corrected) falls out of the same resolved-branch-wins precedence already used for reads. `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()` itself remains unchanged — a tolerant, read-only classifier that never mutates and is safe to call against legacy data (Bluebolt) before any edit. A second, independently-discovered gap surfaced while implementing this fix: `MELEE_FAMILIES`/`RANGED_FAMILIES` only recognized plural spellings (`pistols`, `rifles`); real pack data uses singular forms in `subcategory`/`category` (`pistol`, `rifle`, `heavy`, `advanced`) for the identical record that uses plural/compound forms in `proficiency` — confirmed directly against Gar'ee's real Bluebolt (`proficiency:"pistols"` alongside `subcategory:"pistol"`, `category:"pistol"`). Both sets were expanded to include every real observed spelling of each family, or the write-time enforcer would have silently corrected `proficiency` while leaving `subcategory`/`category` holding the contradictory value.

**Blocker 2 — the save-path validation gate silently destroyed non-STR/DEX attack-attribute choices.** `item-defaults.js` retained `if (!['str', 'dex'].includes(merged.attackAttribute)) merged.attackAttribute = 'str';` — a direct, live violation of the Attack-Ability Policy this same domain established (SWSE has feats/talents, e.g. Noble Fencing Style, that legitimately grant CHA or another non-STR/DEX ability for an attack roll). Verified before fixing that `combat-stat-rules.js#getWeaponAttackAbility()` already correctly passes through an arbitrary explicit ability key (`else resolved = explicit;`, with existing CHA precedent via Noble Fencing Style) — the defect was isolated to this one validation gate, not the consumption path. Fixed: the allow-list now accepts all six ability keys (`str`/`dex`/`con`/`int`/`wis`/`cha`); only a genuinely invalid/garbage value falls back to `str`.

**Blocker 3 — base/intrinsic vs. effective/contextual light-weapon scope was conflated.** `isLightWeaponForActor()` implements only intrinsic, wielder-size-relative lightness. SWSE's Weapon Finesse + Weapon Focus combined rule ("when wielding a single one-handed weapon for which you have Weapon Focus, you can treat that weapon as a Light Weapon") is a separate, contextual reclassification this function does not and should not implement directly. Investigated before deciding scope: a repository-wide search found this combined rule is **partially** implemented — `combat-option-resolver.js#collectCombinedFeatModifiers()` already grants its attack-ability consequence (substituting the better of Dex/Str, gated on Weapon Finesse + a matching Weapon Focus choice + a one-handed non-heavy/non-two-handed weapon) entirely independently of `isLightWeaponForActor()` — correcting an earlier, imprecise claim in this same correction round that no such layer existed at all. What is genuinely absent is a shared *effective-lightness* layer for the three other mechanics that key off "is this weapon Light": Flurry of Blows eligibility (`core-attack-option-runtime-patches.js#isLightOrLightsaberWeapon`), Two-Weapon Fighting/dual-wield shape (`dual-wield-combat-shape-resolver.js`, feeding `combined-full-attack-planner.js`), and the grapple natural/light exemption (`grapple-state-engine.js`). A fourth consumer, `weapons-engine.js`'s Two-Handed Strength Bonus gate, is unaffected by the gap by construction — the combined rule's own precondition ("a single one-handed weapon") makes it mutually exclusive with the two-handed check that consumer performs. A fifth, `combat-utils.js#isLightWeapon`, has no live caller in the repository today. Per the explicit escape hatch this correction round was given, effective/contextual lightness is **not implemented** this round; the scope is instead narrowed and stated plainly in `isLightWeaponForActor()`'s own JSDoc (full per-consumer audit inline there), and anchored by a new regression test (`tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs`, assertion 23) proving a weapon that would only qualify as light under the unimplemented combined rule still resolves to `false` for base lightness.

**Documentation reconciliation.** The original Batch 2B round's "744 total (604 ranged, 140 melee)" and "302 other ranged records" pack-scan figures were an artifact of an overlapping glob scan (`weapons*.db` + `melee*.db` + `*weapon*.db`, where the third, broader pattern re-matched every file the first pattern already matched, double-counting each record, and also pulled in out-of-scope vehicle-weapon packs) — reproduced exactly to confirm this root cause. A corrected, deduplicated (by `_id`), properly-scoped scan found: **193 unique standalone weapon-catalog records** across `packs/weapons*.db` (151 ranged, 42 melee — every category-specific pack file's records are also duplicated into the aggregate `packs/weapons.db`, the same pre-existing structural duplication already noted for `packs/armor.db`/`packs/armor-shields.db`), **plus 5,766 actor-embedded weapon items** across the NPC/heroic/nonheroic/droid packs (0 of which carry `meleeOrRanged` either) — **5,959 total weapon-type records, `meleeOrRanged` absent on 100% of all of them.** `scripts/items/weapon-branch-resolver.js`'s header docstring and `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs`'s header comment were both corrected to the true figures; the original figures are left in the Phase 1/2 section above with an inline superseding note rather than silently rewritten, so the audit trail stays legible.

**Revised certification.** **BASE WEAPON BRANCH/FAMILY SCHEMA COHERENCE: CERTIFIED**, now including full write-time enforcement across every family-bearing and range-descriptor field, not just `meleeOrRanged`. **ATTACK-ABILITY POLICY: CERTIFIED**, now including all six ability keys at every save-path validation gate, not just STR/DEX. **BASE/INTRINSIC LIGHT-WEAPON CLASSIFICATION: CERTIFIED.** **EFFECTIVE/CONTEXTUAL TREAT-AS-LIGHT CLASSIFICATION: NOT CERTIFIED** — Flurry of Blows eligibility, dual-wield/Two-Weapon Fighting shape, and the grapple light-weapon exemption do not yet recognize a weapon "treated as Light" solely via the Weapon Finesse + Weapon Focus combined feat; this is a documented, pre-existing gap (not a regression introduced by this batch) queued for a future batch, not silently absent.

Revalidation: `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (rewritten this round; 23/23), `tests/weapon-branch-classification-authority.test.mjs` (3/3), `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs` (6/6), `node tools/run-rolling-tests.mjs` (232 passed, 0 failed, of 232 run; 5 pre-existing documented exclusions), `node tools/run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2500/2500), `node tools/validate-data.js` (pass), `node tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs` (390/390 unique feat documents, exact parity — unaffected, no pack data touched), `system.json` JSON-parse check (pass), and a repeated repository-wide duplicate-classifier search (no new duplicate branch/light/natural classifiers introduced this round; the local `isMeleeWeapon`/`isRangedWeapon`/`isNaturalWeapon` functions found are the same pre-existing set already classified in Phase 6 above).

### Batch 2B correction #2: `system.weaponCategory` was overloaded with two incompatible meanings (branch vs. family) — the real architectural blocker

A third independent review, checking commit `6f348f72a78435bd1ad556933c354930071ac7ea` directly, accepted both prior fixes (Attack-Ability Policy's six abilities; the honest Base-vs-Effective lightness non-certification) but found a deeper, previously-unnoticed architecture defect: **`system.weaponCategory` had been carrying two incompatible meanings simultaneously.** Shipped pack data authors it as the literal BASE BRANCH (`"melee"`/`"ranged"`) on 100% of records — the very fact `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()`'s tier-1 precedence relies on. But the V2 item editor's own Category `<select name="system.weaponCategory">` (`templates/dialogs/entity/parts/header.hbs`) had been writing FAMILY values into that exact same field the whole time (`advanced`/`lightsaber`/`melee-exotic`/`natural`/`simple` for melee; `heavy`/`pistols`/`ranged-exotic`/`rifles`/`simple` for ranged) — values that are never a literal `"melee"`/`"ranged"` string.

**Confirmed mechanism, reproduced exactly.** Because an editor-submitted family value never matches tier 1, `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()` fell through to tier-2 family evidence — read from `proficiency`/`subcategory`/`category`, none of which were live editor form fields (no `<input name="system.proficiency">`/etc. existed anywhere in the template) and were therefore silently carried over UNCHANGED from the item's prior, pre-edit state on every real form submission. On an explicit Branch change (e.g. Ranged → Melee), `swse-item-sheet.js#onMeleeOrRangedChange()`'s own `#populateWeaponCategoryOptions()` rebuilds the Category select's option list for the new branch and, when the previously-selected value has no match in the new list (the common case — a ranged family like `"pistols"` never appears among the melee options), silently defaults to the new list's first option (`"advanced"` for melee) — which then gets submitted as `weaponCategory`. The old, single-blob `normalizeWeaponBranchFamily(merged)` (both the item-sheet's `normalizeItemSystem()` call and the `weapon-branch-coherence-hotfix.js` preUpdateItem hook independently had this same shape) had no way to tell "the player just explicitly changed the Branch selector" apart from "the family fields are merely stale carryover" once current+submitted state had already been flattened into one object — so the just-submitted `meleeOrRanged:"melee"` was silently outvoted and reverted back to `"ranged"` by the stale, untouched `proficiency:"pistols"`. Reproduced exactly in `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` section 15 before any fix (confirmed to fail with the pre-fix code), matching the reviewer's description precisely.

A second, related symptom: opening a real, never-edited pack-shipped ranged weapon (`weaponCategory:"ranged"` literal) put that value into a Category `<select>` whose options for the ranged branch are `heavy`/`pistols`/`ranged-exotic`/`rifles`/`simple` — none of which is literally `"ranged"` — so the browser's own `<select>` element defaulted to its first option with no `selected` match, meaning even a completely unrelated field edit (e.g. renaming the item) could silently reclassify a real Bluebolt-shaped weapon's family from Pistols to Heavy on save.

**Field contract established (chosen to require zero pack-data migration, preserving the convention already authored in all 5,959 shipped weapon-type records):**

| Field | Canonical meaning going forward | Who writes it |
|---|---|---|
| `system.meleeOrRanged` | Canonical, player-facing BASE BRANCH (`"melee"`/`"ranged"`) | The editor's Branch `<select>`; every consumer should ultimately trust this field |
| `system.weaponCategory` | LEGACY/COMPATIBILITY MIRROR of the branch only, matching every shipped pack record's existing convention | No longer independently editable in the V2 editor; always kept in sync with the resolved branch at write time (`normalizeWeaponForWrite()`) |
| `system.subcategory` | Canonical, player-facing WEAPON FAMILY | The editor's Category `<select>` is now bound here (repointed from `weaponCategory`), using the real shipped singular vocabulary (`pistol`, `rifle`, `heavy`, `exotic`, `advanced`, `lightsaber`, `natural`, `simple`, `grenade`) confirmed by a direct scan of `packs/weapons*.db` |
| `system.category` | Pure alias, always mirrored from `subcategory` | Write-time normalizer only |
| `system.proficiency` | Pure alias, DERIVED from `subcategory` via a mapping table matching the real shipped proficiency vocabulary (confirmed by pack scan: melee = `exotic`/`advanced-melee`/`simple`/`natural`; ranged = `exotic`/`simple`/`heavy-weapons`/`pistols`/`rifles`) | Write-time normalizer only, whenever `subcategory` is explicitly set |
| `system.weaponGroup` / `system.group` | Untouched, read-time-only evidence | Never written by the normalizer |

`resolveWeaponBranchFamily()` (read-time, tolerant) is unchanged — a literal `weaponCategory:"melee"/"ranged"` remains its tier-1 signal, correct both for un-migrated legacy pack data (where it always holds branch) and for anything saved through the fixed write boundary (where it is now guaranteed to always hold branch, never family).

**Implementation.** A new function, `normalizeWeaponForWrite(currentSystem, submittedSystem, target)` (`scripts/items/weapon-branch-resolver.js`), receives the item's prior persisted state and the update's actual submitted keys SEPARATELY — never a pre-merged blob — so it can tell deliberate intent apart from stale carryover. Precedence: a literal `weaponCategory:"melee"/"ranged"` explicitly submitted wins first (matching `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()`'s own tier-1 authority and keeping single-object materialization/self-heal calls correct, since real pack-shaped data has both fields simultaneously "present" but only `weaponCategory` is trustworthy there); otherwise an explicitly submitted `meleeOrRanged` wins (the real, fixed editor's only live branch signal, since it no longer submits `weaponCategory` at all). For family: an explicitly submitted `subcategory` (or, for API/macro callers, any other explicitly submitted family-bearing field) is canonicalized and propagated to `subcategory`/`category`/`proficiency` together; absent any explicit family submission, existing family fields are preserved verbatim if already coherent, reset to the branch-neutral `"simple"` fallback only if they disagree. `normalizeWeaponBranchFamily(system)` (the prior single-argument entry point) is now a thin wrapper — `normalizeWeaponForWrite(system, system, system)` — preserving its exact external contract for every caller that never had a current-vs-submitted distinction to make (materialization, simple full-object API updates).

**Consumers fixed:** `item-defaults.js#normalizeItemSystem()` now passes its own already-available `currentSystem`/`submittedSystem` parameters into `normalizeWeaponForWrite()` instead of flattening them first (the actual item-sheet save-path fix). `weapon-branch-coherence-hotfix.js`'s `preCreateItem`/`preUpdateItem` hooks had the identical flatten-then-normalize defect at the Foundry-hook layer (affecting sparse API/macro-level updates that bypass the item sheet entirely) — fixed the same way, and its `correctedFields()` now diffs and propagates the FULL set of fields the normalizer can touch (`meleeOrRanged`, `weaponCategory`, `ranged`, `subcategory`, `category`, `proficiency`, `weaponGroup`, `group`, `rangeProfile`, `weaponType`, `attackAttribute`) — previously only 3 of these were diffed, meaning a computed family correction was silently discarded rather than persisted. `templates/dialogs/entity/parts/header.hbs`'s Category `<select>` is repointed from `system.weaponCategory` to `system.subcategory`, with option values updated to the real shipped singular vocabulary; its two other `weaponCategory`-gated checks (the Customize Lightsaber button, the Blaster Color button's "not simple" gate) are repointed to `subcategory` too. `weapon-data-resolver.js`'s `MELEE_WEAPON_CATEGORY_OPTIONS`/`RANGED_WEAPON_CATEGORY_OPTIONS` (consumed by `swse-item-sheet.js#populateWeaponCategoryOptions()`) updated to the same vocabulary; `resolveWeaponData()`'s display `category` field (previously reading `weaponCategory` first — a pre-existing cosmetic bug this same audit surfaced, since it would have shown e.g. "Ranged" as a weapon's "Group" label instead of its real family) now reads `subcategory` first. `swse-item-sheet.js#hydrateCurrentWeaponRange()`'s live range-band preview now reads/passes `subcategory` instead of `weaponCategory`, and also overrides `category`/`proficiency` in its constructed lookup object (since `resolveWeaponBranchFamily()`'s family-candidate scan checks `proficiency` before `subcategory` — without this, a live, not-yet-saved Category change would preview against the item's still-stale, pre-edit proficiency). Three real-persisted-write sites that set `weaponCategory` to a family value (`item-defaults.js`'s `BLANK_ITEM_DEFAULTS.weapon`, `safe-item-factory.js#buildSystemData('weapon')`, both `weaponCategory:"simple"`; `follower-creator.js#_buildNaturalWeaponItemData()`, `weaponCategory:"natural"`) are corrected to write the branch literal instead, moving their family value onto `subcategory`/`category`/`proficiency`.

**Repository-wide write-site audit** (per the review's explicit request) found every other write to these seven fields (`weaponCategory`/`meleeOrRanged`/`proficiency`/`subcategory`/`category`/`weaponGroup`+`group`/`rangeProfile`+`weaponType`) falls into one of: (a) synthetic/virtual weapon objects never persisted via `.update()`/`.create()` (droid-part virtual weapons, the unarmed-strike helper — already internally coherent by construction, confirmed in a prior round), (b) one-off compendium-source maintenance scripts (`tools/add-swse-weapons.js`, `tools/add-weapon-categories.js`, `tools/fix-weapon-proficiencies.js`) that already write `weaponCategory` as branch and `proficiency`/`subcategory` as family — already exactly matching this batch's field contract, no change needed, or (c) `scripts/apps/progression-framework/shell/progression-finalizer.js`'s starter-lightsaber grant, which already writes `weaponCategory:"melee"` (branch) and `category`/`subcategory:"lightsaber"` (family) as correctly separate fields — also already compliant, confirmed by direct reading, no change needed. `scripts/patches/attack-dialog-combat-corrections-hotfix.js#normalizeWeaponForCombat()` mutates a live weapon item's in-memory `meleeOrRanged` (never persisted, never touches `weaponCategory`/family fields) via the already-canonical `getWeaponBranch()` — unaffected by this round. `scripts/apps/customization/item-customization-workbench.js#itemHasWorkbenchAlias()` reads `weaponCategory` as one of eleven `.some()`-matched candidate tokens for upgrade-catalog category routing (never a write); confirmed no false-positive risk — the bare strings `"melee"`/`"ranged"` do not collide with any entry in `WORKBENCH_CATEGORY_ALIASES` (which only contains compound tokens like `"meleeweapon"`/`"rangedweapon"`), and `system.category` (kept correctly in sync as the family alias) is checked first regardless.

**Fail-before/after proof, through the real save boundary.** `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` section 15 reproduces the actual `swse-item-sheet.js` submit shape (`normalizeItemSystem(type, currentSystem, submittedSystem)` with the two states kept separate, exactly as the real `#onSubmitForm` → `sanitizeItemSheetUpdate()` path receives them): (a) an explicit editor Branch change (Ranged → Melee) with the old broken editor's `weaponCategory:"advanced"` resubmission and untouched stale `proficiency:"pistols"`/`subcategory:"pistol"`/`category:"pistol"` now correctly persists melee with no stranded ranged-family fields — confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix code (`meleeOrRanged` silently reverted to `"ranged"`) before the fix landed; (b) an unrelated field edit (rename only) on an already-coherent, never-touched real Bluebolt disturbs nothing; (c) a branch-only API/macro update (`{meleeOrRanged: "melee"}`, no family field touched at all) reaches the same deterministic, coherent result. Section 16 exercises `weapon-branch-coherence-hotfix.js`'s real `preCreateItem`/`preUpdateItem` hooks through actual `Hooks.on()` registration (not a reimplementation) — an API-level branch-only `preUpdateItem` is not overridden by stale family evidence, and real pack-shaped `preCreateItem` materialization (the original Bluebolt root cause) still self-corrects. All existing sections re-verified and updated where the new field contract changed an expectation (section 19's "editor branch+category update" now asserts the real shipped-data proficiency alias, e.g. `subcategory:"lightsaber"` correctly derives `proficiency:"exotic"`, not the editor-vocabulary spelling verbatim — the old assertion had itself been testing an incorrect, fictional data shape, since real lightsaber records were confirmed by direct pack scan to never carry a literal `"lightsaber"` proficiency value).

**Revised certification.** **BASE WEAPON BRANCH/FAMILY SCHEMA COHERENCE: CERTIFIED**, now including the corrected field contract (`weaponCategory` is a pure branch mirror; `subcategory` is the canonical, editor-bound family field) and an explicit-submission-aware write boundary that cannot be silently outvoted by stale carryover data, at both the item-sheet save path and the Foundry-hook materialization/API path. Every other certification from the prior two rounds (Attack-Ability Policy, Base/Intrinsic Light-Weapon Classification, the explicit Effective/Contextual non-certification) is unchanged and unaffected by this round.

Revalidation: `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (27/27, including 4 new sections), `tests/weapon-branch-classification-authority.test.mjs` (3/3), `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs` (6/6), `node tools/run-rolling-tests.mjs`, `node tools/run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2500/2500), `node tools/validate-partials.mjs` (532 `.hbs` files scanned, OK), `node tools/validate-data.js` (pass), `node tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs` (390/390, unaffected), `system.json` JSON-parse check (pass), and the repository-wide write-site audit above (no new duplicate branch/family authority introduced; every real persisted write site now matches the corrected field contract).

### Batch 2B correction #3: `template.json` itself still violated the new contract, and `preCreateItem` could not distinguish a template-fabricated default from genuine authored intent

A fourth independent review, checking commit `a8f42e85740d339444ac8ad2def5fb4f2af1b31a` directly, accepted the branch/family editor separation and the current-vs-submitted write provenance from correction #2, and confirmed CI green — but found two remaining data-boundary defects, both confirmed valid by direct inspection before any fix was made.

**Blocker 1 — `template.json`'s own weapon defaults still violated the field contract it declares.** Correction #2 established `system.weaponCategory` as a pure branch mirror (`"melee"`/`"ranged"` only, never family again). But `template.json`'s actual `Item.weapon` block still declared `"weaponCategory": "simple"` — a family value — with no `subcategory`/`category` fields at all. This is the lowest Foundry DataModel default boundary; every weapon document, including ones created entirely outside any of this batch's normalizer/hook code paths, inherits from it. Confirmed directly by parsing the real `template.json`, not assumed. Fixed: `weaponCategory` corrected to `"melee"` (matching its own `meleeOrRanged: "melee"` default), and explicit `subcategory: "simple"` / `category: "simple"` added, so the template's own declared blank weapon is internally coherent by itself, not merely "happens to resolve correctly once the write-time normalizer runs on top of it." `BLANK_ITEM_DEFAULTS.weapon` (`item-defaults.js`) and `safe-item-factory.js#buildSystemData('weapon')` were already corrected to this same shape in correction #2 — all three now describe the identical conceptual blank weapon (branch melee, family simple, default attack ability str).

**Blocker 2 — `preCreateItem` could not distinguish a template-fabricated default from genuine authored intent.** By the time Foundry's `preCreateItem` hook fires, `document` has already been constructed by merging the raw creation payload (`data`) onto `template.json`'s schema — so `document.system` contains `attackAttribute: "str"` for ANY creation whose raw payload never authored that field at all, purely because the DataModel had to fill something in. `weapon-branch-coherence-hotfix.js`'s `correctedFields()` (as fixed in correction #2) treated `document.system` as genuine "current" state for both `preCreateItem` and `preUpdateItem` alike — correct for `preUpdateItem` (a real prior save genuinely exists there) but wrong for `preCreateItem` (there is no genuine prior state for a brand-new document; `document.system`'s un-authored fields are Foundry's own fabrication, not player input). Consequence: a raw API/macro/import creation of a ranged weapon that genuinely omitted `attackAttribute` would see `normalizeWeaponForWrite()`'s "fill only when genuinely absent" check find a non-empty `"str"` (the fabricated template default) already present in the merged state, and preserve it — persisting STR on a weapon that should have defaulted to DEX. This is the exact same provenance-blindness class as the original Bluebolt defect, now on `attackAttribute` instead of branch. Confirmed by direct trace of the hook's logic before any fix, and reproduced as a fail-before test (confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code: a ranged creation with `attackAttribute` genuinely omitted persisted `"str"` instead of `"dex"`).

Fixed by splitting `correctedFields()`'s notion of "current state" per hook: `preCreateItem` now passes an empty `currentSystem` (`{}`) into `normalizeWeaponForWrite()` — only `data.system` (the caller's actual raw payload) counts as genuine submitted intent — while still diffing the computed correction against the real, currently-in-memory `document.system` (which does need correcting via `updateSource()`, since that's what Foundry will actually persist). `preUpdateItem` is unchanged: `document.system` there is real, previously-persisted state, never fabricated, so it correctly remains the "current" side. Branch resolution was unaffected by this specific gap (a literal `weaponCategory` submitted in `data.system` was already checked directly, regardless of what `document.system` held) — the defect was isolated to the "fill absent field from default" logic, which only `attackAttribute` uses.

**Fail-before/after proof.** `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` section 17 parses the real `template.json` (not a fixture) and asserts its weapon defaults are internally coherent under the canonical resolver — confirmed to fail against the pre-fix template (`weaponCategory: "simple"` where `"melee"` was required). Section 18 exercises the real, registered `preCreateItem`/`preUpdateItem` hooks with a harness that reproduces Foundry's actual construction order (`document.system` = template defaults merged with the raw payload, exactly as a real `Item.create()` call hands the hook): a ranged creation with `attackAttribute` genuinely omitted now correctly defaults to `dex` (confirmed to fail — persisted `str` — against the pre-fix hook); a melee creation with it omitted defaults to `str`; an explicit `str` on a ranged creation and an explicit `cha` on a melee creation both survive verbatim; a real, previously-persisted `attackAttribute` survives both an unrelated-field `preUpdateItem` and a same-branch family-only update (pistol → rifle) untouched.

**Write-site re-search.** A repeated repository-wide search for any remaining `weaponCategory: "simple"`/`"natural"`/`"pistols"`/`"rifles"`/`"heavy"` (persisted-write shape) found exactly one hit: `scripts/engine/crew/follower-talent-config.js`'s `AKK_DOG_FIXED_PROFILE.naturalWeapons[0].weaponCategory: "natural"`. Classified as internal profile vocabulary, not a persisted-schema write: `follower-creator.js#_buildNaturalWeaponItemData()` (the actual materialization boundary that turns this profile into a real embedded Item, fixed in correction #2) hardcodes `weaponCategory: "melee"` on the persisted item regardless of what the input profile's own `weaponCategory` field says, and propagates the profile's family value (`"natural"`) onto `proficiency`/`subcategory`/`category` instead — confirmed by direct reading. No further pack, script, or config data was found holding a live persisted `system.weaponCategory` family value anywhere in the current HEAD.

**Revised certification.** **BASE WEAPON BRANCH/FAMILY SCHEMA COHERENCE: CERTIFIED**, now including `template.json`'s own weapon defaults (the lowest schema boundary, previously the one remaining place the field contract wasn't yet true). **ATTACK-ABILITY POLICY: CERTIFIED**, now including `preCreateItem`'s authored-vs-fabricated-default distinction — a genuinely omitted `attackAttribute` correctly receives the branch default (`dex` for ranged, `str` for melee) at creation, while an explicit choice of any of the six abilities is preserved verbatim, both at creation and on every subsequent update. `preUpdateItem`'s player-owned-attack-ability handling is unchanged and unaffected. Every other certification from the prior three rounds is unchanged. Effective/contextual "treat as Light" lightness remains explicitly NOT CERTIFIED, unaffected by this round.

Revalidation: `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (34/34, including the 2 new sections above), `tests/weapon-branch-classification-authority.test.mjs` (3/3), `tests/grapple-state-attack-penalty.test.mjs` (6/6), `node tools/run-rolling-tests.mjs`, `node tools/run-rolling-syntax-check.mjs` (2500/2500), `node tools/validate-partials.mjs` (532 `.hbs` files scanned, OK), `node tools/validate-data.js` (pass), `node tools/verify-feats-pack-source.mjs` (390/390, unaffected), `system.json` and `template.json` JSON-parse checks (pass), and the repository-wide write-site re-search above (one remaining hit, classified as internal profile vocabulary, not a schema-contract violation).

### Batch 2B final documentation reconciliation

A fifth independent review, checking commit `e4318124c4e5c1d2ee9b19e8dbd0a25c2922918a` directly, found correction #3's runtime fixes correct with no new behavioral defect, and confirmed CI green — but flagged one stale production-source documentation block left over from the module's original authorship: `weapon-branch-coherence-hotfix.js`'s module-level JSDoc still cited the superseded "744/744" pack-scan figure (corrected to 193 standalone + 5,766 embedded = 5,959 in the first correction round, but never updated here), and still claimed the hook "never rewrites the high-trust, deliberately-authored fields (weaponCategory, proficiency, subcategory, category)" — no longer true since correction #2 made `normalizeWeaponForWrite()` intentionally reconcile exactly those fields for coherence (`DIFFED_FIELDS` proves it directly). No production logic was changed; the JSDoc now cites the corrected figures and accurately separates read-time (tolerant, non-mutating) from write-time (coherence-enforcing) authority, plus the create-vs-update provenance distinction from correction #3. `tests/weapon-branch-family-schema-authority.test.mjs` (34/34, unchanged) and the full rolling syntax check (2500/2500) confirm no behavior change.

**Batch 2B final certification, as scoped:**

- **BASE WEAPON BRANCH/FAMILY SCHEMA COHERENCE: CERTIFIED**
- **ATTACK-ABILITY POLICY: CERTIFIED**
- **BASE/INTRINSIC LIGHT-WEAPON CLASSIFICATION: CERTIFIED**
- **EFFECTIVE/CONTEXTUAL TREAT-AS-LIGHT CLASSIFICATION: NOT CERTIFIED** — Flurry of Blows eligibility, dual-wield/Two-Weapon Fighting shape, and the grapple light-weapon exemption still do not recognize a weapon "treated as Light" solely via the Weapon Finesse + Weapon Focus combined feat. This is a documented, pre-existing gap (not a regression introduced by any Batch 2B round), left as its own queued future domain rather than folded into this certification.

## Domain: Weapon Modifier Source Contract — CONFIRMED DEFECT — **FIXED**

Found live, as a side effect of building a real actor golden-case fixture (Vexa'na Fen'Orr'kess Tessik) to verify the certified Grapple formula against a second, independently-shaped character. Running her real actor export through `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` threw a caught-but-logged error from `WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers()`: `createModifier: missing required fields: source=undefined, sourceName=Lightsaber (Attuned)...`. Initially misdiagnosed as a data-quality issue on that one item; a follow-up review correctly identified it as a code defect.

**Root cause, confirmed directly against the source**: three `createModifier()` call sites in `weapons-engine.js` — the attuned-lightsaber +1 attack bonus, and both modifier builders in `#gatherLightsaberUpgradeModifiers()` (the standard-modifiers-array path and the direct damage-bonus path) — passed `source: ModifierSource.WEAPON`. `ModifierTypes.js`'s canonical `ModifierSource` enum has no `WEAPON` member (`FEAT, TALENT, SPECIES, BACKGROUND, ENCUMBRANCE, CONDITION, ITEM, EFFECT, DROID_MOD, VEHICLE_MOD, CUSTOM`), so `ModifierSource.WEAPON` always evaluated to `undefined` — which `createModifier()`'s own required-field validation correctly rejected. This is not malformed actor/item data: every other weapon-sourced modifier in this same file (enhancement bonuses, proficiency penalty, two-handed bonus, keen/flaming traits) already uses `ModifierSource.ITEM` — a weapon, and a lightsaber upgrade, are both ordinary Foundry Items — so the three `WEAPON` sites were simply inconsistent with the file's own established convention, never a second, deliberately-different category.

**A second, independent defect compounded this**: `getWeaponModifiers()` wrapped its entire multi-weapon `for` loop in one `try/catch`. A single malformed modifier from any one weapon (any weapon after the first, or the first weapon's own later checks) therefore threw out of the whole loop, silently discarding every other equipped weapon's modifiers collected in the same pass — not just the offending contribution. Live-reproduced: two equipped weapons, the first carrying a malformed lightsaber-upgrade modifier, the second an ordinary valid enhancement bonus — before the fix, the second weapon's modifier was never even attempted, since the loop aborted on the first weapon's throw.

**Fixed**: all three `ModifierSource.WEAPON` sites corrected to `ModifierSource.ITEM`, matching the file's existing convention (repository-wide search confirms these were the only three occurrences of `ModifierSource.WEAPON` in the codebase). Every `createModifier()` call in `getWeaponModifiers()`/`#gatherLightsaberUpgradeModifiers()` is now routed through a new `#pushModifierSafe(modifiers, data)` helper that catches and logs a single malformed contribution in isolation, so it can never affect any other modifier — from the same weapon or a different one — rather than relying on the outer, loop-wide `try/catch` as the only safety net.

**Live-verified**: Vexa's real actor export no longer produces the error when run through the real `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` pipeline; her attuned lightsaber's +1 bonus is now correctly collected. `tests/weapons-engine-modifier-source-authority.test.mjs` proves, against the real `WeaponsEngine.getWeaponModifiers()` (not a reimplementation): an ordinary weapon, an attuned lightsaber (the specific fixed defect), a lightsaber upgrade with a standard-modifiers-array contribution, an attuned lightsaber plus upgrade together, and the required multi-weapon isolation case — a first weapon with a genuinely malformed upgrade modifier (a non-numeric `value`, not the already-fixed `WEAPON` bug specifically) must not erase a second, unrelated weapon's valid modifier. Every emitted modifier across every case is asserted against the full required shape: a canonical `source`, plus `sourceId`, `sourceName`, `target`, a canonical `type`, and a finite numeric `value`.

**Classification: CONFIRMED and FIXED.** Kept separate from the Weapon Melee/Ranged Schema domain above (a different kind of defect — a missing single-SSOT-field for branch/category consistency, not a modifier-construction contract violation) and from any other actor-specific audit findings from the same Vexa review — this entry is specifically the `ModifierSource.WEAPON` contract defect and its blast-radius fix.

## Domain: Armor / ACP / Max Dex / Energy Shield

**A1 — ACP has no single canonical site (3 overlapping implementations):**
- `ModifierEngine.js#_getItemModifiers()` (:1250-1547) and `scripts/engine/skills/rules/armor-rule.js:25-54` agree on intent (explicit comment: proficiency does NOT remove base ACP, only prevents an *additional* non-proficiency penalty) and both correctly exclude energy shields from their "equipped armor" lookup (see A2).
- `scripts/rolls/skills.js:299` is a **third, SSOT-bypassing path**: `actor.system.armorCheckPenalty || 0` — a flat actor-level field, not read from `resolveArmorData()` at all. Candidate for deprecation/removal.
- (Cross-reference: the skills-domain audit above separately confirmed `armor-rule.js` is dead/broken due to an unpopulated `CONFIG.SWSE.skills` — so in practice only `ModifierEngine.js` is live.)

**A2 — Energy Shield ACP/Max-Dex-while-activated exception: CONFIRMED absent, and structurally excluded (independently confirmed by two agents).** `defense-calculator.js:719`, `ModifierEngine.js:1258`, and `armor-rule.js:35` all explicitly filter energy shields OUT of the "equipped armor" lookup that drives both ACP and Max Dex. `resolveArmorData()` *does* compute `armorCheckPenalty`/`maxDexBonus` for shield items (armor-data-resolver.js:321-331,346) and even an `activated` flag (:356) — but nothing downstream ever reads a shield's values for ACP/Max Dex. The intended rule is documented as UI copy only (`templates/apps/customization/partials/workbench-content.hbs:381`) and never wired into math. The only shield-ACP logic that exists at all is advisory/scoring code (`armor-benefit-simulator.js:158-166`) and it encodes **the opposite of the correct rule** (`if (isProficient) return 0`). **Classification: CONFIRMED — a missing feature, not a miscomputed formula.** This directly blocks Gar'ee's golden Stealth/Initiative shield-active values (+22/+17) — no code path currently produces the -2 shield ACP term at all, active or inactive.

**A3 — Armor talent bonuses (Armored Defense, Improved Armored Defense, Second Skin, Armor Mastery): not written back into armor item fields (the specific write-back mechanism hypothesized was NOT found)**, but a different, real drift risk exists instead: `scripts/patches/armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js` monkey-patches `DefenseCalculator.calculate` and `PanelContextBuilder.prototype.buildDefensePanel`, and its `applyArmorDefenseCorrectionsToResult`/`effectiveArmorDefenseState` independently re-derive the same proficiency/Second-Skin/Armored-Defense/Armor-Mastery logic and **overwrite** the canonical result — not additive double-counting, but a second, independently-maintained implementation of the identical rule that already lacks one alias the canonical calculator has (`hasKnightArmorMastery`, a Jedi Knight talent-tree variant) — a Jedi Knight with that specific talent gets inconsistent Reflex math depending on whether this hotfix's post-processing runs.

**A4 — Max Dex:** computed twice with consistent logic (`defense-calculator.js:784-790`, `ModifierEngine.js:1307-1332`, both +1 for Armor Mastery) — but since both derive `equippedArmor` by excluding energy shields (same exclusion as A2), there is no active/inactive toggle to evaluate: **the restriction is never applied for a shield at all, in any state.**

### Batch 2A — implementation (fixes A1, A2, A4; supersedes the "3 overlapping implementations" framing)

While reproducing A1 before fixing it, the generic body-armor ACP formula was found to be independently wrong on its own terms, not just missing the shield case: `ModifierEngine._getItemModifiers()` computed `acp = armor.armorCheckPenalty; if (!proficient) acp += categoryPenalty;` — for Light armor (own listed ACP -2, category default also -2) this gave proficient → **-2** (wrong; SWSE RAW: proficiency suppresses ordinary armor's ACP to 0) and nonproficient → **-4** (wrong; double-counted the armor's own listed penalty against the category default; expected -2). The corrected rule, fixed together with the Energy Shield exception so the shield fix wasn't built on the wrong armor formula:

```
ordinary body armor: effectiveACP = proficient ? 0 : (item's own listed ACP, or the light/medium/heavy
                                                        category default only when the item has none)
Energy Shield, active: effectiveACP = the item's own listed ACP (or category default), ALWAYS, proficient or not
Energy Shield, inactive: no ACP, no Max Dex restriction, no Reflex penalty, no speed penalty, at all
```

**New shared authority:** `scripts/engine/effects/armor-usage-resolver.js#resolveArmorUsageEffects(actor)` — a pure resolver returning `{bodyArmor, activeEnergyShields, attackCheckPenalty, skillCheckPenalty, maxDexCap, reflexPenalty, denyPositiveDexToReflex, parts}`, each `parts[]` entry carrying `{semanticKey, sourceId, sourceName, value, effect, active, proficient}` for provenance. Wired into every live consumer found:

- `ModifierEngine._getItemModifiers()` — no longer double-counts body-armor ACP; now also emits shield ACP skill modifiers (previously shields were excluded from this function's `equippedArmor` lookup and it returned early with zero modifiers if no body armor was worn, which also silently skipped a shield-only actor entirely). The ACP-affected skill list was also reconciled to exactly the 7 canonical skills (Acrobatics, Climb, Endurance, Initiative, Jump, Stealth, Swim) — the previous list additionally included `athletics` (not in the rule contract), and the separate armor-*upgrade* ACP list used a third, different 8-skill set (`escapeArtist`, `sleightOfHand`, `useRope` in place of `endurance`/`initiative`/`athletics`). Both now use the one canonical list.
- `combat-roll-math.js#resolveAttackBonus()` — previously had **zero** armor/shield ACP term of any kind (a gap for ordinary armor too, not just shields). Now applies the same shared ACP, with each contributing item named explicitly in the `components` breakdown (e.g. `"Energy Shield (SR 10) (Armor Check Penalty)": -2`) rather than folded into an unlabeled number.
- `defense-calculator.js` — added a separate active-Energy-Shield Reflex layer alongside (never replacing) body armor's own: a second Max Dex clamp taking the more restrictive of body-armor-cap vs. shield-cap in either direction; a flat -5 Reflex penalty for an active nonproficient shield; and a component-aware denial of the *positive* Dex bonus only (mirroring the existing Pin/flat-footed pattern — a Dex penalty is never removed), unified with the existing Pin-reduction logic so an actor who is somehow both Pinned and wearing an active nonproficient shield isn't double-subtracted for the same Dex bonus. Armor Mastery (a body-armor talent) was deliberately **not** extended to shields — no rule text supports it, so this was not guessed.
- `armor-benefit-simulator.js#effectiveArmorCheckPenalty()` — previously `if (isProficient) return 0` for every armor type, correct for ordinary armor but backwards for an active shield. Now returns the shield's ACP unconditionally (simulated as active/worn, since an inert shield has no combat effect and would make every shield score identically to no shield at all); the result object now states `simulatedState: 'active'` explicitly and separately reports `nonproficiencyReflexPenalty`/`denyPositiveDexToReflex`.
- `scripts/engine/suggestion/equipment/scoring/armor-axis-b-engine.js` — a third, independent advisory ACP calculation not previously listed in A1: `computeMobilityCostAxis()` unconditionally forced `proficient = true` for every Energy Shield, silently zeroing its ACP in this scoring axis regardless of real proficiency. Fixed to apply the shield's ACP unconditionally, consistent with the shared rule, while leaving its other (out-of-scope) proficiency-gated mobility heuristics (e.g. Armor Mastery's Max-Dex bonus in this same axis) untouched.
- `scripts/rolls/skills.js#calculateSkillModifier()` — a macro-exposed utility (registered as `game.swse.rolls.skills`, not part of the live derived-skill pipeline) read `actor.system.armorCheckPenalty`, a flat actor-level field confirmed (by repository-wide search) to never be written anywhere — this ACP term was always silently 0. Repointed to the shared resolver.
- `scripts/engine/skills/rules/armor-rule.js` — confirmed dead in practice (`CONFIG.SWSE.skills` is never populated anywhere in this codebase, so its own `skillDef.armorPenalty` gate is always falsy and it always early-returns before reaching its math). Left dead but corrected to the same shared authority anyway, per the freeze principle of not leaving a second, wrong implementation lying around in case this ever becomes live again.
- `scripts/patches/armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js` — **a newly-discovered, live regression risk to this exact fix**: this hotfix unconditionally monkey-patches `DefenseCalculator.calculate` and `PanelContextBuilder.prototype.buildDefensePanel` (registered unconditionally at module load via `runtime-bugfix-hotfixes-init.js`), and its `applyArmorDefenseCorrectionsToResult`/`applyArmorDefenseCorrectionsToPanel` independently **recompute** Reflex from scratch and overwrite `result.reflex`. Before being fixed here, it had no knowledge of the new shield fields at all, so it would have silently discarded the entire active-shield Reflex layer above (Max Dex clamp, nonproficiency -5, positive-Dex denial) whenever body armor was ALSO worn — live-verified: registering the hotfix and calling `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` on a body-armor+active-nonproficient-shield actor produced a Reflex total with the shield's -5 penalty and Dex denial silently absent. Fixed by folding the same shield adjustment into both of this hotfix's recomputations.
- **A second, independent defect found in the course of fixing the hotfix above, present in both `defense-calculator.js` and the hotfix's own `effectiveArmorDefenseState()`:** `resolveArmorData()` reports an "uncapped" Max Dex as exactly `null` (including normalizing legacy 99/999 sentinel values to `null`) — but both sites wrapped that value in `Number(...)` before `Number.isFinite(...)`, and `Number(null) === 0` (finite), silently turning "uncapped" into "capped to +0" and stripping the actor's *entire* positive Dex bonus for any body armor with no Max Dex limit. Fixed in both places by reading the already-normalized value directly instead of routing it through a lossy `Number()` coercion first.

**Pack/source data fix (item 11 — Energy Shields never reduce speed):** `packs/armor.db` and the duplicate `packs/armor-shields.db` (both separately registered, live compendiums — a pre-existing, out-of-scope structural duplication left untouched) carried a stale `speedPenalty: 2` on the SR 15/20/25/30 Energy Shield records, despite each one's own description text explicitly stating "Does not reduce speed." No generator script produces these packs from a separate canonical source (unlike `feats.db`/`data/feat-catalog.json`), so the raw NDJSON records were corrected directly, in both packs, and `resolveArmorData()` was additionally hardened to zero `speedPenalty` for any Energy Shield at the SSOT level (mirroring how `reflexBonus`/`fortitudeBonus` are already zeroed for shields there) so store/entity-dialog/suggestion consumers reading it are protected even against a future bad import.

**Golden-case verification (item 12):** Gar'ee's real exported Energy Shield (SR 10) and M1-10 Stalker Armor item data (his Medium body armor's own listed `armorCheckPenalty` is literally `false`/0 — a real example proving proficiency suppression is checked before any category fallback) were used directly (not a synthetic-only fixture) to prove: proficient body armor ACP = 0 despite proficiency; active shield ACP = -2 applies regardless of his proficiency; exactly the 7 canonical skills shift by -2 when the shield activates and no others; his attack resolver drops by exactly 2; his speed is identical active or inactive.

**Certification status (superseded by the Batch 2A correction below — see that section for the current status):** ModifierEngine, resolveAttackBonus, DefenseCalculator, armor-benefit-simulator, armor-axis-b-engine, `scripts/rolls/skills.js`, armor-rule.js (dead), and armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js all verified in agreement against the shared authority, including the Gar'ee real-actor case and the full activation-idempotence cycle (`InventoryEngine.toggleActivated()`, inactive→activate→deactivate→reactivate, exactly one contribution per active state, charges consumed once per activation). Tests: `tests/armor-shield-acp-authority.test.mjs`, `tests/energy-shield-defense-and-activation-authority.test.mjs`.

### Batch 2A correction — a second, independently-maintained defense authority was found (and removed) underneath the fix above; the Gar'ee golden case is now proven end to end

A second review of Batch 2A, against the real `#972` head, accepted the new `armor-usage-resolver.js` authority and every rule/consumer fix above, but refused certification on two grounds: (1) `armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js` — already known to monkey-patch `DefenseCalculator.calculate()`, and already taught the new shield adjustment above — was **still a second, independently-maintained Reflex/Fortitude formula**, not a consumer of the canonical one, and (2) the Gar'ee golden case had only been proven at the modifier-object level (ModifierEngine emits the right `skill.<key>` entries), never driven through the real `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` → `system.derived.skills` → roll-dialog pipeline.

**Ground 1, confirmed live against the real hotfix before this fix, with concrete wrong numbers:**
- **Defect 1 — Gar'ee's flat-footed Reflex regressed.** The hotfix's `result.flatFooted.total = result.reflex.total - Math.max(0, abilityMod)` stripped only the positive Dex bonus, never a dodge bonus. Gar'ee's certified flat-footed Reflex (29 normal → 24 flat-footed, per the Flat-Footed Reflex domain fix earlier in this ledger: base 24 + Dex +4 + a +1 Martial-Arts-style dodge bonus, both stripped) would have come out to 25 through this hotfix — silently resurrecting the dodge bonus that fix already certified removed.
- **Defect 2 — active-nonproficient-shield Dex denial double-subtracted when also flat-footed.** DefenseCalculator's canonical `reflex.total` already has the shield's positive-Dex denial baked in once. The hotfix's reconstruction derived `flatFooted.total` from that already-denied `reflex.total`, then subtracted the (pre-denial) `abilityMod` a second time.
- **Defect 3 — Pin's Dex-bonus removal could be silently undone.** The hotfix's reconstruction never read `reflex.pinnedDexReduction` at all; it rebuilt the total from raw component fields using the canonical (undenied) `reflex.abilityMod`, which could restore the Dex bonus Pin had just removed — a direct cross-domain regression against the Grapple domain's own certified fix.

**Root cause:** the hotfix was taught the shield rule (Batch 2A's own fix, above) instead of being stopped from reconstructing the answer at all — an unsustainable pattern (every future defense rule change would need to be taught to this file by hand, forever, or silently regress through it). **Fixed by removing the reconstruction entirely.** The hotfix's own module doc comment describes a narrower, legitimate problem: some armor/shield records mark equipped state via a legacy/alternate field (`system.isEquipped`, `system.readied`, `system.equippable.equipped`, `flags.swse.equipped`) that `DefenseCalculator`'s own `equippedArmor` lookup (`item.system?.equipped` only) doesn't recognize, so the canonical calculator would think nothing is equipped and skip armor/shield math entirely. That belongs on the **input** side, not the output side:

```
hydration/equip-state normalization → DefenseCalculator.calculate() → consumers
```

`armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js` now only normalizes `system.equipped` (in-memory only, never persisted) for any armor/shield item that's equipped by the broader legacy definition but not yet flagged canonically, immediately before calling the real, untouched `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` — then returns its result unmodified. The separate `buildDefensePanel` patch, which had the identical reconstruction bug, is removed outright: `PanelContextBuilder.js`'s own `buildDefensePanel()` already prefers `system.derived.defenses.<key>.total` (the same canonical output `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` persists there) as its `authoritativeTotal`, so once that persisted data is correct, no separate panel correction is needed at all. Verified: `tests/armor-hydration-hotfix-shadow-authority-removal.test.mjs` proves the required invariant — registering the hotfix must never change `DefenseCalculator`'s output for an actor whose armor is already correctly flagged, across ordinary armor, Armored Defense/Improved Armored Defense, flat-footed + dodge, Pin, active proficient/nonproficient shields, and body armor + shield together — plus the three fail-before scenarios above (now proven fixed) and a positive test that the hotfix's real remaining job (recognizing a legacy alternate equip-flag shape) still works.

**Ground 2, closed:** `tests/garee-full-skill-derivation-parity.test.mjs` drives Gar'ee's real armor/shield/feat records through the actual `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` pipeline (not a modifier-existence proxy), for both shield states, and proves the full required chain: `expected === system.derived.skills[key].total === getSkillTotal(actor, key) === buildRollConfigModel({rollType:'skill', skillKey}).baseTotal` (the same functions a real "Roll Stealth" click resolves through, per the Skills domain's own `skill-roll-dialog-base-authority.test.mjs` precedent) — for every one of the 7 ACP-affected skills, in both shield states, plus `system.derived.skills.initiative.total === system.derived.initiative.total` in both states, plus bit-for-bit identity of every unaffected skill (Perception, Knowledge Tactics, Use Computer) between states. All values matched the required golden matrix exactly on the first live run.

This investigation also surfaced (and ruled out as a live numeric defect, though it is dead/misleading code) a **fourth apparent ACP site**: `DerivedCalculator`'s own per-skill loop has a dedicated `armorPenalty` breakdown line reading `actor.system.derived?.armor?.checkPenalty || actor.system.armor?.checkPenalty` — two flat fields confirmed, by repository-wide search, to never be written anywhere. That line is always 0 in practice; the real, live ACP contribution reaches the skill total exclusively through the same `modifierMap['skill.<key>']` channel every other feat/equipment/effect modifier uses (computed unconditionally, not gated by this dead field), which is exactly the channel `tests/garee-full-skill-derivation-parity.test.mjs` proves end to end. Left as-is (matching this ledger's established "dead code documented, not silently left as a second wrong authority" treatment elsewhere) since it contributes nothing and isn't reachable; renamed nowhere, changed nowhere — noted here for the record.

**Ground 3 — advisory consumers, corrected to match the live ruling, not just partially:** the prior pass fixed `armor-axis-b-engine.js`'s unconditional `proficient = true` for shields but left two related defects in the same function: every shield was still classified as category `'light'` regardless of its real required proficiency (`armorProficiencyRequired`) — misclassifying SR15/20 (Medium-required) and SR25/30 (Heavy-required) shields alike — and Armor Mastery's +1 Max Dex was still applied to shields, contradicting the live `DefenseCalculator` ruling that Armor Mastery does not extend to Energy Shields. Both fixed: `category` now reads the shield's real `proficiencyRequired`, and the Armor Mastery bonus is gated to non-shield armor only. Separately, `Number.isFinite(Number(maxDex))` — the exact "uncapped `null` silently becomes capped `0`" defect already fixed in `defense-calculator.js` and the hotfix — was found and fixed in three more sites: `armor-benefit-simulator.js` (both `simulateBodyArmorContribution()` and `evaluateEnergyShield()`) and, critically, **in `armor-usage-resolver.js` itself** — the new shared authority this entire batch is built on had the identical bug in both `resolveBodyArmorContribution()` and `resolveActiveShieldContribution()`, silently capping any uncapped armor/shield's Max Dex to +0 in the live `DefenseCalculator` shield layer too, not just in advisory code. All four sites now read the already-normalized `number | null` value directly instead of routing it through a lossy `Number()` coercion first. Verified: `tests/armor-axis-b-shield-authority.test.mjs`.

**Certification status (superseded by the second Batch 2A correction below):** `ORDINARY ARMOR ACP: CERTIFIED. ENERGY SHIELD ACP: CERTIFIED. ENERGY SHIELD REFLEX/MAX-DEX: CERTIFIED (single authority — the shadow DefenseCalculator is gone). ENERGY SHIELD SPEED: CERTIFIED. GAR'EE ACTIVE/INACTIVE SKILL PARITY: CERTIFIED (proven through the real derived-skill and roll-dialog pipeline, not a modifier-object proxy).** Tests (in addition to the two from the prior pass): `tests/armor-hydration-hotfix-shadow-authority-removal.test.mjs`, `tests/garee-full-skill-derivation-parity.test.mjs`, `tests/armor-axis-b-shield-authority.test.mjs`.

### Batch 2A correction #2 — the equip-state fix had its own ordering bug; a single equip-state authority replaces it

A third review of Batch 2A accepted the shadow-DefenseCalculator removal and every other correction, but found one remaining architectural ordering bug: the first correction fixed the hotfix's equip-state normalization (`normalizeArmorEquipState()`) by moving it to run just before `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` — but `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` runs `ModifierEngine.getAllModifiers()`/`aggregateAll()` (which drive ACP/skill modifiers via `armor-usage-resolver.js`) **before** it calls `DefenseCalculator.calculate()`. Confirmed by direct code reading (`derived-calculator.js` lines ~231-263): on the actor's first derived pass, a legacy-equipped item (`system.isEquipped`/`system.readied`/`system.equippable.equipped`/`flags.swse.equipped` instead of `system.equipped`) would be invisible to ModifierEngine — ACP/skill contributions silently omitted — even though Defense math, computed after the hotfix's mutation ran, came out correct. `system.derived.skills` and derived Initiative could be wrong for that one pass, "mysteriously" self-correcting on a later recomputation purely because the mutation had already happened by then — exactly the order-dependent behavior this freeze exists to eliminate.

A smaller, related defect in `ModifierEngine.getActorModifierSourceSignature()`'s cache-invalidation signature was also confirmed: `item?.system?.equipped ?? item?.system?.isEquipped ?? ''` uses `??`, which only skips `null`/`undefined` — so `system.equipped: false, system.isEquipped: true` hashed identically to `system.equipped: false` alone, silently losing the `isEquipped` signal from the cache key.

**Fix:** rather than relocating the mutation again, established one true equip-state authority. `isArmorItemEquipped(item)` (new export, `scripts/items/armor-data-resolver.js`) is now the single canonical "is this armor/shield item equipped" check — implementing the same broad legacy-shape recognition the hotfix used to apply via a mutation, but as a pure, read-time predicate every consumer calls directly instead:

- `armor-usage-resolver.js` — both `resolveBodyArmorContribution()`'s and the shield filter's equip checks.
- `ModifierEngine._getItemModifiers()`'s `equippedArmor` lookup.
- `defense-calculator.js`'s own `equippedArmor` lookup.
- `armor-benefit-simulator.js`'s `equippedBodyArmor()` (advisory consumer, fixed for full consistency).
- `armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js`'s `isWornArmor()`/`findEquippedWornArmor()` (used only by its two UI panel patches — `buildArmorSummaryPanel`/`buildInventoryPanel` — a display concern unrelated to defense/skill math) now delegate to the same shared check instead of maintaining a fourth duplicate copy of the same logic.

No consumer depends on any other subsystem having run first, and no mutation of shared actor/item state happens anywhere. **`armor-hydration-defense-hotfix.js`'s `normalizeArmorEquipState()` and `installDefenseCalculatorPatch()` are removed entirely** — DefenseCalculator now recognizes a legacy equip-flag shape on its own, with zero help from any hotfix, so the hotfix no longer touches `DefenseCalculator.calculate()` in any way. Its only remaining responsibilities are the two UI panel patches and the unrelated Effect Builder partial registration.

The modifier-source signature bug is fixed by giving each legacy equip-flag field its own slot in the signature array (`system.equipped`, `system.isEquipped`, `system.readied`, `system.equippable.equipped`, `flags.swse.equipped`, each independently), rather than collapsing two of them through a lossy `??`.

**Verified:** `tests/legacy-equip-state-single-pass-authority.test.mjs` — a single `DerivedCalculator.computeAll()` pass on a Gar'ee-shaped actor whose shield and body armor are BOTH equipped via legacy/alternate flag shapes (including the specific adversarial case `system.equipped: false` / `system.isEquipped: true` for the shield, and `system.equippable.equipped: true` with no `system.equipped` at all for the armor) produces the correct ACP-affected skill totals, correct derived Initiative (with the `system.derived.skills.initiative.total === system.derived.initiative.total` parity check), correct defenses, and correct roll-dialog totals (`getSkillTotal()`/`buildRollConfigModel()`) — all on that first pass, no second recomputation needed. `tests/armor-hydration-hotfix-shadow-authority-removal.test.mjs` was updated to match: it now proves the hotfix's registration has zero effect on `DefenseCalculator`'s output (trivially true now that the hotfix never touches it) and that `DefenseCalculator` recognizes a legacy equip-flag shape with no mutation of the item at all.

**Certification status:** all five Batch 2A domains remain certified, now additionally proven immune to equip-detection call-order — `ORDINARY ARMOR ACP: CERTIFIED. ENERGY SHIELD ACP: CERTIFIED. ENERGY SHIELD REFLEX/MAX-DEX: CERTIFIED. ENERGY SHIELD SPEED: CERTIFIED. GAR'EE ACTIVE/INACTIVE SKILL PARITY: CERTIFIED. EQUIP-STATE DETECTION: CERTIFIED (single authority, order-independent).**

## Domain: Speed / Movement — CLEAN, no defect found

Clean two-layer split confirmed: base authority `system.speed` (species/droid/vehicle grants set this via declarative rules, never overwritten by armor/talent logic), effective authority `system.derived.speed.{base,total,walk,adjustment}` (character-actor.js:38-47). Armor speed penalty and **Juggernaut** (correctly requires proficiency, zeroes the penalty modifier rather than mutating base speed) are both modifier-based, not mutations — `ModifierEngine.js:1369-1406`. No code path found that writes an armor/talent-adjusted number back into the persisted base `system.speed` field. Sheet correctly displays the effective (post-penalty) speed as primary, with base shown only as a secondary "base N ±adj" annotation when there's a delta (`resources-panel.hbs:11-14`). **No action needed for this domain.**

## Domain: Condition Track — mostly clean authority, but the lookup table is copy-pasted 6+ times

Single canonical writer confirmed: `BaseActor.getConditionPenalty(step)` (`scripts/actors/v2/base-actor.js:307-313`, table `[0,-1,-2,-5,-10,0]`) → `system.derived.damage.conditionPenalty`. Most high-stakes consumers (skills, defenses, attacks) correctly read this single field (`derived-calculator.js:671-672`, defense breakdowns, `combat-roll-math.js:422`) — this part of the architecture is sound and matches the target shape.

**D — CONFIRMED: the step→penalty table itself is independently hand-copied in at least 6 places**, with an inconsistent helpless-step value (`0` in most, `-999` in one):
- `scripts/engine/combat/ConditionEngine.js:379-389` (`#getConditionPenalty`, own literal table, helpless=`-999`)
- `scripts/utils/calc-conditions.js:5-13` (writes to `actor.conditionPenalty`, a **different, seemingly-dead property path** than the canonical `system.derived.damage.conditionPenalty` — grep found zero importers)
- `defense-calculator.js:745-751` (`getConditionPenalty()` closure — fallback-only, prefers canonical field first)
- `scripts/ui/combat-stats-tooltip.js:207-221` — **CONFIRMED live divergence risk**: builds its own table AND computes the Initiative tooltip's total independently, reading `system.conditionTrack?.current` **raw**, completely bypassing `system.derived.damage.conditionPenalty`. Since the numeric table (0,-1,-2,-5,-10) isn't currently house-rule-configurable (confirmed via `houserule-condition-track.js`, which only proxies to `ConditionEngine.calculateConditionStep`), these don't disagree *today* — but if the canonical table is ever edited in one place, Initiative's tooltip would silently diverge from Skills/Defenses/Attacks.
- `scripts/ui/defense-tooltip.js:332-343` — a fifth table (positive magnitudes), display-text only, doesn't feed the shown total.
- `scripts/engine/effects/adapters/effect-card-utils.js:87-96` — a sixth, text-only (not additive) step→label mapping.

**Classification: CONFIRMED architectural debt, narrow current exposure** (no live numeric disagreement today since the table is static, but the redundancy is real and the Initiative tooltip's raw-field read is a genuine bypass of the canonical accessor).

## Domain: UI/Sheet Math Reconstruction — 3 confirmed live divergence bugs found

Beyond the skill/initiative fallbacks already covered above, the sheet-reconstruction sweep found:

- **CONFIRMED live bug:** `scripts/sheets/v2/npc/npc-sheet-helpers.js:238-240` (`buildFollowerDefenseValues()`) computes follower-NPC Fortitude as `10 + level + max(strMod,conMod) + bonus` — but the actual follower-stat generator (`follower-deriver.js:290-294`) has an explicit comment stating *"Fortitude uses Constitution, not the better of STR/CON"*. The sheet helper does exactly what that comment forbids. **This is a genuine, confirmed rules-disagreement bug** between two parts of the same subsystem, not just redundant math — follower NPCs' displayed Fortitude can be higher than the system's own documented rule intends.
- **CONFIRMED gap:** Custom skills (`customSkills`) have **no canonical engine computation at all** — `derived-calculator.js` never touches them. Two independent sheet-layer implementations (`custom-skills-helpers.js:22-35`, `character-sheet/concept-context.js:709-713`) are the *only* math, computed twice, and both omit armor-check-penalty and condition-track-penalty that every named skill includes — a custom skill's total will not reflect Condition Track state, unlike every other skill.
- **Guarded/lower-risk duplicates** (fallback-only, prefer canonical when finite): `PanelContextBuilder.js:1428-1438` (grapple fallback, consistent today), `character-like-sheet.js:1140-1151` and the explicitly-`@deprecated` `:6121-6144` (skill total fallbacks, omit armor/condition, transient-only), `progression-framework/steps/summary-step.js:1127-1176` (chargen wizard preview, necessarily actor-less, but a 4th independent copy of the same skill/defense/grapple formulas that must be kept in sync by hand).

---

## Cross-cutting theme across every domain audited

The codebase already has the *right pattern* in exactly one place — `DerivedCalculator`'s skill contribution ledger, which sums named parts, compares the sum against the stored total, and logs a warning on mismatch (`derived-calculator.js:680-733`). **No other domain has this self-checking pattern yet.** The freeze's Phase 13 contribution-ledger contract should generalize this exact mechanism (already proven, already shipping) to BAB, defenses, damage threshold, HP, attack, damage, and grapple, rather than inventing a new architecture.

The single largest, most consistent finding across all 6 audits: **duplicate fallback formulas are pervasive (at least 25+ distinct reimplementation sites found across skills/initiative/defenses/BAB/grapple/attack/condition-track), but the large majority are guarded (only fire when canonical derived data is unavailable) and currently numerically consistent.** The genuinely load-bearing, CONFIRMED-wrong-today defects are a much shorter list — see the First Report below.

---

## Phase 2: Gar'ee golden fixture — live proofs and hand-computed divergences

Two confirmed defects above were reproduced **live, against the real production calculators**, under the `tests/helpers/foundry-shim` harness (not mocked, not reimplemented — the actual `DefenseCalculator`/`ThresholdEngine` classes imported and executed). This is stronger evidence than a code-reading classification alone, so it's recorded here in full with the exact commands and output.

### Live proof 1 — Flat-footed Reflex keeps a dodge bonus (CONFIRMED, executed)

```js
const actor = {
  type: 'character',
  system: {
    attributes: { dex: { base: 20, racial: 0, enhancement: 0, temp: 0 } },
    activeEffects: [{ target: 'defense.reflex', value: 3, enabled: true }] // simulates a +3 dodge bonus
  },
  items: []
};
const result = await DefenseCalculator.calculate(actor, [], {}, {});
```
Result: `reflex.total = 18` (10 + dex 5 + dodge 3, correct), `flatFooted.total = 13` (18 − 5, i.e. **only the Dex mod was stripped — the +3 dodge bonus survived into flat-footed**). SWSE RAW: a flat-footed character loses dodge bonuses along with Dex; correct flat-footed here is `10`. **Divergence: +3 (flat-footed Reflex is 3 points too high whenever the character has any dodge-type bonus active).**

### Live proof 2 — Damage Threshold disagrees between DerivedCalculator and ThresholdEngine (CONFIRMED, executed)

```js
const actor = {
  system: {
    size: 'medium',
    derived: {
      defenses: { fortitude: { total: 20 } },
      damageThreshold: 25 // what DerivedCalculator actually stores: fort 20 + Improved Damage Threshold's +5
    }
  }
};
ThresholdEngine.computeBaseThreshold(actor); // => 20
```
`ThresholdEngine`'s combat-time base (20) ignores the Improved Damage Threshold feat bonus that `DerivedCalculator` already folded into the stored/displayed value (25). **Divergence: −5 for any actor with Improved Damage Threshold** (the combat engine under-counts DT relative to what the sheet shows, meaning a hit that should NOT push the character down the condition track could incorrectly trigger a shift in actual play).

### HP: corrected against Gar'ee's real actor export, and live-executed against the real `ActorEngine.recomputeHP()`

**This section originally described a "20-point HP undercount" against Gar'ee's live actor. That was wrong and has been corrected** (see the HP domain section above for the full writeup). Gar'ee's actual, current `system.hp.max` is **108** — obtained directly from his real actor export, not hand-derived. He was never destructively recomputed. The finding is that `ActorEngine.recomputeHP()`'s formula, hand-derived from its quoted source, **would** produce 88 from his current data if it ever completed and returned a value — and, separately, it currently cannot even complete a value-changing call at all, due to a live-confirmed `ReferenceError` (Defect B in the HP domain section) that fires before any `return` statement in that branch is reached. The crash itself — not the 88 figure — is what was verified by importing and executing the real, unmodified `actor-engine.js` directly (bypassing this repo's test-only fake, which the standard harness entry point redirects to — see `tests/helpers/foundry-shim/path-loader.mjs`), with `ClassesDB` populated directly (it normally builds from a Foundry compendium pack unavailable under this harness) so `ActorAbilityBridge.getClasses()` could resolve a real class item.

**Correct RAW multiclass HP**, confirmed against Gar'ee's own `progression.hpGainHistory`/`classLevelHistory` (both recorded verbatim in his actor export — his HP is genuinely additive/historical, not something to hand-derive): starting HP + accumulated per-level gains through his actual level-up choices, landing on 108 exactly (his own history shows `previousMax: 96 → newMax: 108` at level 8, `+12` via "maximum" method, Soldier hit die 10 + CON mod 2). `HPCalculator.calculate()`'s dead-code algorithm (`hp-calculator.js`) independently arrives at the same 108 via `10*3+2` (level 1) `+ 5*(10+2)` (Soldier levels 2-6) `+ 2*(6+2)` (Scoundrel levels 7-8) — consistent with, not the source of, the real authority.

**`ActorEngine.recomputeHP()`'s formula, hand-evaluated (not executed to completion)** with `ActorAbilityBridge.getClasses(Gar'ee)[0]` confirmed = Soldier (see the HP domain section's "Open question resolved"):
```
hpAtFirstLevel = 10*3 = 30
hpPerLevel     = floor(10/2)+1 = 6
newHPMax       = 30 + (8-1)*6 + 2*8 = 30 + 42 + 16 = 88
```
This is the value the source arithmetic algebraically produces for Gar'ee's real inputs — it is **not** a value the live function was observed to return. Live-executing the real function against Gar'ee's actual state (`currentMax = 108`, which differs from the computed 88) enters the value-changing branch and throws the `ReferenceError` before reaching any `return` statement, so nothing is ever returned on that path. A separate, artificial isolation run — with `currentMax` deliberately set equal to the computed 88 so the function takes its early-return "no change" branch instead — confirmed that branch returns normally without crashing (proving the crash is confined to the value-changing branch, not the whole function), but that run cannot be read as "the function returned 88 for Gar'ee," since it required overwriting his real 108 input to avoid the branch that always crashes. In practice, on Gar'ee's real data, the calling hooks swallow the thrown error — so `system.hp.max` is never touched by this path at all today, for any actor with real per-level history.

### Certified/expected values not yet reproducible under this harness

The following golden values from the freeze charter require either (a) real class-progression/compendium data the lightweight test harness doesn't load (`BABCalculator.calculate()` returned `0` for Soldier 6/Scoundrel 2 under this harness — confirmed it needs `class-data-loader.js`'s async pack-data path, not available here), or (b) the live `ActorEngine`/`ModifierEngine` talent-registration pipeline this harness fakes. They are recorded as **targets to verify against the real Foundry runtime**, not fabricated pass/fail results:

| Value | Charter's certified expectation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| BAB | +7 | Not reproducible under this harness (needs real class-data pack load); formula read confirms Soldier(full)+Scoundrel(3/4, floor(2*0.75)=1)=7 is consistent with `bab-calculator.js`'s documented algorithm |
| Grapple | +12 (BAB 7 + DEX +5 + size 0) | `derived-calculator.js`'s formula (BAB+max(STR,DEX)+size+species) would produce this correctly if BAB is correct; **`houserule-grapple.js`'s live roll formula would instead produce BAB+STR-only = 7+2 = 9 — FIXED, and its fallback replaced (see the Grapple domain section)**, live-verified via `resolveGrappleBonus()` (`combat-stat-rules.js`) directly: BAB 7 + DEX +5 + medium size (0) = 12 |
| Stealth (shield inactive) | +24 | Formula-consistent per `DerivedCalculator`'s skill ledger (dex+halfLevel+trained+focus+misc = 5+4+5+5+5=24); **not independently executed** (needs real derived-skill pipeline) |
| Stealth (shield active) | +22 | **Not currently producible by any code path** — confirmed above, no Energy Shield ACP implementation exists anywhere |
| Initiative (shield inactive) | +19 | Formula-consistent (5+4+5+5=19); not independently executed |
| Initiative (shield active) | +17 | **Not currently producible** — same Energy Shield ACP gap |
| Knowledge (Tactics) | +10 | Formula-consistent (1+4+5=10), unaffected by ACP per the charter — not independently executed |
| Use Computer | +10 | Formula-consistent (1+4+5=10) — not independently executed |
